## /goal — FIX NOA GROUP DRAFT APPROVAL HANDOFF — 2026-09-21

### 31. Group scan owner summary reported discoveries as pending replies; `תגובות-גרופ` could not reach most drafts

**Defect**: after the 09-20 scan the owner received "490 תגובות ממתינות" although the scan created **18** drafts and **40** were pending in total. The owner also could not see 30 of the 40 (`תגובות-גרופ` was `LIMIT 10`, no paging) and the summary carried no draft IDs. Not a delivery failure: read-only trace showed the summary was sent at 17:22:30Z (in window), bridge "Sent OK", no quiet-hours/dedup suppression; there is (by design) no per-draft WhatsApp message.

**Root cause**: the summary title/body were built from the discovery set — `len(discoveries)` (posts FOUND) — instead of persisted `group_comment_drafts` state; drafts that already existed also rendered as "(אין)". Plus a visibility gap in the draft list command.

**Minimal fix** (reporting/handoff layer only; scanner, relevance, approval gate, publishing and transport untouched):
- `social/noa_ops.py`: `group_draft_summary()` (new = drafts *created since this cycle started*, total = all `pending_approval`, top-10 new pending by relevance with IDs), `format_group_summary()` (three distinct numbers: relevant posts / new drafts / total pending; IDs; `תגובות-גרופ` and `אשרתגובה <מזהה>` hints), `format_group_summary_fallback()` (used only if the query fails; never claims a pending count).
- `BACKEND_API_ROUTES.py::_group_scan_loop`: summary branch now uses those helpers. `alert_key=group_scan_discoveries_<fp>`, `cooldown_s=86400`, `notify_owner` transport: unchanged.
- `agents/owner_console.py`: `תגובות-גרופ [N]` pages 10 per page (`עמוד p/N · מציג a–b מתוך T`, next-page hint, out-of-range clamps); list is read-only; `אשרתגובה`/`דלגתגובה` untouched.

**Regression**: new `devtests/noa_group_summary_test.py` (10 unit + 6 functional steps: 490 discoveries + 18 new + 40 pending => exact counts; pre-existing drafts not counted as new; "490 תגובות ממתינות" absent; every ID shown is a real pending draft; paging reaches all pending drafts and clamps; dispatcher parses `תגובות-גרופ 2`; `אשרתגובה` approves exactly one draft through the owner gate with the publish step **mocked**; helpers contain no publish path; dedup key/cooldown/`notify_owner` intact; autonomy default OFF, `APPROVAL_REQUIRED=True`). One existing assertion in `group_scan_reporting_test.py` pinned the defective wording (`"תגובות ממתינות"` in the loop source); it now pins the corrected behaviour (title/body from `format_group_summary`, `{len(discoveries)} תגובות ממתינות` absent) while the dedup-key assertion is kept. Full battery: 15/15 suites exit 0.

**Production activation (2026-09-21, owner-approved)**: pre-checks clean (HEAD=origin=`d3cd917`, only expected files modified, `NOA_ENGAGEMENT_AUTOREPLY=0`, `APPROVAL_REQUIRED=True`, 40 drafts all `pending_approval`, no test rows). `pre_restart.sh` then `docker restart autospare_backend` at 05:17:54Z — backend only; WhatsApp bridge (start 09-20 12:35Z) and Facebook session untouched; backend healthy, new helpers loaded, autonomy OFF. Note: at 07:05:27Z the backend was killed by a **host-level OOM** (exit 137, kernel `global_oom`, uvicorn 2.7 GB RSS; Docker `unless-stopped` relaunched it) — unrelated to this change (new code paths had not run) and it reset the loop's 2h start delay; the fresh process loaded the fixed code.
**Paging (read-only, via `process_owner_message`)**: `תגובות-גרופ`, `… 2`, `… 3`, `… 4`, `… 9` on the 40 pending -> 4 pages, 40/40 reachable, out-of-range clamps to last page; re-verified after the scan on 56 pending -> 6 pages, 56/56 reachable.
**Natural scan (no scan forced)**: started 09:05:35Z (session AUTHENTICATED), finished 11:58:22Z: 386 groups, 3,013 items scored, **335 relevant**, **16 new drafts** (33 dups, 22 draft-budget-skipped, 4 failures), autonomous hook `disabled` x16. Persisted state after: 16 new / **56 total pending**, all `pending_approval`, 0 approved/posted/failed.
**WhatsApp handoff**: one summary, sent 11:58:23Z (in window), bridge "Sent OK", one send of 1878 UTF-16 units. Independent proof it is the NEW format: text rebuilt from persisted state with the new formatter = 1856 chars + 22 astral emoji (10x🆔, 10x💬, 📣, 🔎; JS counts 2 units each) = **1878 exactly**; the old format was 1194 chars. It reports 335 relevant posts / 16 new drafts / 56 pending (never "335 pending"), lists 10 draft IDs and the `תגובות-גרופ` / `אשרתגובה` hints. Dedup key `group_scan_discoveries_<fp>` (new fp `27d82b2f06`) and 24h cooldown present and unchanged; quiet queue empty. (Other bridge sends that day — 09:00-IL queue flush, system alerts, three `status@broadcast` posts — are pre-existing behaviour, not from this change.)
**Regression after activation**: `noa_group_summary_test` (10 unit + 6 functional), `group_scan_reporting_test` 11/11, `noa_group_draft_handoff_test` 11/11 — all exit 0; earlier full battery 15/15.

**Safety**: no WhatsApp message sent, no Facebook post/comment, no draft approved, no approval-gate/autonomy/scanner/relevance change.

**Git**: committed+pushed on owner approval: `backend/social/noa_ops.py`, `backend/BACKEND_API_ROUTES.py`, `backend/agents/owner_console.py`, `backend/devtests/group_scan_reporting_test.py` (modified); `backend/devtests/noa_group_summary_test.py` (new); `FIXES_TRACKER.md`. HEAD `d3cd917`.

## /goal — BAILEYS BRIDGE: `messages.upsert` LISTENER CRASH DEFECT CLOSED (pre-restart) — 2026-09-20

**Defect / root cause:** `sock.ev.on('messages.upsert', async (...) => {...})` is an async event listener; the emitter never awaits or catches it, so any throw becomes an unhandled rejection of the listener's promise. Its media decode was inside `try/catch`, but the inbound error branch was not: `try { await axios.post(BACKEND_WEBHOOK, ...) } catch (err) { console.error(...); await sock.sendMessage(jid, {fallback text}) }`. The fallback reply runs exactly when the backend webhook failed (backend restarting / timeout), i.e. when a socket problem is most likely; if `sendMessage` threw there (`Connection Closed` on a dead/reconnecting socket) nothing caught it: unhandled rejection -> not the contained orphan signature -> process exit, and the rest of the message batch was aborted.
**Minimal fix (at the owning listener boundary, no process-level swallowing):** new `safeListenerSend()` in `bridge_lifecycle.js`, used only at that one call site in `index.js`: (a) socket not usable -> skip WITHOUT calling Baileys (no query/orphan timer) and log `LISTENER_SEND_SKIPPED`; (b) expected dead-socket failure (`Connection Closed` / 428) -> caught, logged `LISTENER_SEND_FAILED_DEAD_SOCKET`, loop continues with the next message; (c) ANY other error is re-thrown unchanged (still fatal + visible via `UNHANDLED_REJECTION_FATAL`, as before). Healthy behaviour unchanged (fallback reply still sent on a healthy socket). No Baileys change, no auth/session change.
**Regression:** `node --test whatsapp-bridge/test_bridge_lifecycle.mjs` **19/19** (13 previous + 6 new: unit safeListenerSend; L1 healthy inbound forwarded, no fallback; L2 healthy + backend failure -> fallback still sent; L3 dead socket -> skipped, no Baileys call, logged, alive, batch continues; L4 race `Connection Closed`+orphan timer -> caught, logged, alive, both messages processed; L5 unrelated error NOT swallowed - logged and fatal as before). **Negative controls:** listener tests vs the pre-fix file: L1/L2/L5 pass, **L3 and L4 fail** with `UNHANDLED_REJECTION_FATAL | Error: Connection Closed` (the defect); orphan-timeout control retained: the same suite with the containment guard line removed fails `5+6 RACE ("process must survive the orphaned Timed Out")`, and the in-suite harness-validity tests still prove an unguarded orphan crashes. Existing bridge suites: send_readiness_guard 16/16, msgcache pass, atomic_write 27/27, safe_logging 30/30.
**Live verification (read-only):** bridge NOT restarted (same start `11:51:37Z`, pid, restarts 0); session `:11` connected, `/groups` ok, connection log unchanged since pairing (no 401/409/Failure). `auth_info/` not touched by the harness (tests run with cwd=temp dir and a stubbed `useMultiFileAuthState`; the live log never shows a test recipient; no stray temp dirs). The only `auth_info/` changes are the live bridge's own: owner Signal session files at 12:31:03Z = the live production alert it delivered (not from tests) and `creds.json` on its ~10-minute cadence (12:22:17Z before this task, 12:32:17Z). No real WhatsApp message was sent by this work. Deployed files validate under the container's Node 20 (`node --check` + module import; exports include `safeListenerSend`).
**ACTIVATION STILL PENDING:** the running bridge process still has the OLD code (its `/health` has no `state` field). All three lifecycle fixes (orphan-timeout guard + socket gating, event-derived `/health`, listener boundary) activate together at ONE deliberate bridge restart, which reuses the existing paired session (no re-pair).
**Remaining Baileys defects:** none known in the listener paths. Residual (not defects introduced here): Baileys 6.17.16 is old vs the current WhatsApp Web version (no 405 seen); backend link monitor reports `SESSION_INVALID` as generic "disconnected" (backend intentionally untouched); older bridge tests replicate logic rather than import it.
Files: `whatsapp-bridge/bridge_lifecycle.js`, `whatsapp-bridge/index.js` (1 import + 1 call-site hunk on top of the earlier uncommitted work), `whatsapp-bridge/test_bridge_lifecycle.mjs`, `FIXES_TRACKER.md`. Not committed.

## /goal — BAILEYS BRIDGE: ORPHANED-TIMEOUT CRASH + FALSE /health FIXED — 2026-09-20

**Defect 1 - root cause (Baileys `socket.js` `query()`, installed 6.17.16):** `const wait = waitForMessage(msgId)` (arms a `promiseTimeout` timer) is created BEFORE `await sendNode(node)`. On a dead/closing socket `sendNode` throws `Connection Closed` and `query` exits, so `wait` is never awaited; exactly `defaultQueryTimeoutMs` (60 s here) later the orphan rejects `Boom('Timed Out', 408)` with no handler and Node's default unhandled-rejection behaviour terminates the process (Docker restart). Measured: 169 crashes / 169 process starts in the dead-session period, 162 preceded by a failed send in the same run, gap send-error -> crash median 60 s. The orphan is created INSIDE the library, so no caller can attach a handler; process-level `unhandledRejection` is the only place it is observable, hence scoped, not blanket.
**Defect 2 - root cause:** `/health.connected = !!(waSocket && waSocket.user)`; `waSocket.user` is populated from stored `creds.json` even after WhatsApp rejects the login (401), so `/health` reported `connected:true` through the 07-29 and 09-14 outages.

**Fix (new `whatsapp-bridge/bridge_lifecycle.js` + minimal hunks in `index.js`; no Baileys upgrade/patch, `auth_info/` untouched):**
1. *Prevention (primary):* connection state derived from `connection.update` events (`CONNECTING/AWAITING_QR/CONNECTED/DISCONNECTED/SESSION_INVALID`); `/send`, `/typing`, `/delete`, `/groups`, `/group/create` return a controlled `503` before any Baileys call unless the socket is usable (state CONNECTED + `sock.user` + `ws.isOpen`), so no query can be started on a dead/reconnecting/logged-out socket.
2. *Containment (residual race):* `installOrphanTimeoutGuard` handles ONLY the exact signature (Boom `Timed Out`, status 408, captured stack contains `waitForMessage`), logs `ORPHAN_QUERY_TIMEOUT` to stdout + `connection_events.log` and keeps the process alive; any other unhandled rejection is logged (`UNHANDLED_REJECTION_FATAL`) and re-thrown = still crashes like Node's default. A `Timed Out` from any other operation is NOT contained.
3. */health:* `connected` = usable state (no per-request WhatsApp call); adds `state`, `session_invalid`, `state_since`, `last_disconnect`; existing fields kept (`ok, connected, awaiting_qr_scan, jid, expected_number, account_mismatch`) so `show_qr.sh` and the backend monitor/probe keep working. 401 -> `SESSION_INVALID` (no reconnect, as before); 428/503/515 -> `DISCONNECTED` -> new socket -> `CONNECTED`.
4. `index.js` also gained env-overridable `BRIDGE_PORT` and `BRIDGE_APP_DIR` (defaults unchanged: 3001, `/app`) purely so the harness can run the real file without touching the live `qr.txt`/event log.

**Regression:** new `whatsapp-bridge/test_bridge_lifecycle.mjs` **13/13** (`node --test`), running the REAL `index.js` + express against a stub Baileys that reproduces the orphan using Baileys' own `promiseTimeout` (no WhatsApp possible, no message sent): healthy health/send/groups; dead-socket `/send` + `/groups` -> controlled 503 with zero Baileys calls; race -> 500, process survives, `ORPHAN_QUERY_TIMEOUT` logged in stdout and event log; `/health` false with stored creds present; 401 -> SESSION_INVALID, no reconnect; 428/503/515 recovery; AWAITING_QR; harness validity (unguarded orphan crashes; unrelated rejection still crashes with the guard). **Negative control:** the identical suite against the pre-fix `index.js` -> 9 of 13 fail, and the race test reproduces the exact production sequence (`Send error: Connection Closed` -> `Error: Timed Out` -> Node fatal). Existing bridge tests: send_readiness_guard 16/16, msgcache pass, atomic_write 27/27, safe_logging 30/30 (`test_atomic_write_crash_harness.mjs` is a spawned helper by design).

**Live (read-only, bridge NOT restarted per instruction):** `:11` connected, `/groups` ok:true, 0 x 401/409/Connection Failure/conflict since pairing, restarts 0. IMPORTANT: the running process loaded the OLD code at 11:51:37, so both fixes are on disk and validated for the container's Node 20 (`node --check` + module import) but become ACTIVE only at the next bridge start (a restart keeps the paired session; no re-pair). Until then `/health` still has the old semantics.
**Remaining (not fixed here):** (a) `messages.upsert` error branch does `await sock.sendMessage(...)` inside a listener; if that throws (dead socket) it is an unhandled `Connection Closed` rejection, which is NOT the contained signature and would still terminate the process (same as before); (b) the backend link monitor reports SESSION_INVALID as generic "disconnected" (its probe only runs when `connected` is true) - adequate, backend intentionally untouched; (c) Baileys 6.17.16 is old relative to the current WhatsApp Web version (no 405 seen); (d) older bridge tests replicate logic instead of importing it.
Files: `whatsapp-bridge/bridge_lifecycle.js` (new), `whatsapp-bridge/test_bridge_lifecycle.mjs` (new), `whatsapp-bridge/index.js` (modified; already carried other uncommitted work), `FIXES_TRACKER.md`. Not committed.

## /goal — WHATSAPP/BAILEYS: PRESERVE EVIDENCE + TEST/PRODUCTION ISOLATION — 2026-09-20

**Diagnosis (see prior entries):** the bridge's Baileys credentials were invalidated server-side at 2026-09-14 22:50:46Z (`Stream Errored (conflict)`, code 401; every later login `<failure 401>`). Most likely trigger (inference, not a WhatsApp-confirmed reason): 99 real WhatsApp messages to 51 synthetic numbers in 14 min (01:35-01:50 local), the last account created 3 s before the conflict.

### Phase A - evidence preserved (nothing deleted, nothing printed)
- `whatsapp-bridge/auth_info.dead-20260914-2250Z/` = `cp -a` of the live `auth_info/`; **144/144 files byte-identical (sha256 manifests match)**, `creds.json` parses with all required structures; archive and evidence made read-only/`go-rwx`. The original `auth_info/` is untouched (still in place; the re-pair procedure moves it aside).
- `whatsapp-bridge/evidence-20260914-conflict/` (mode 700): `connection_events.log` copy, full `docker logs whatsapp_bridge` copy (contains the conflict event, 166 later 401 refusals; docker logs only reach back to ~09-11 and rotate), `auth_info.{source,archive}.sha256`.
- Existing `auth_info.dead-20260705`, `-20260729-1425`, `-20260818-1827`, `auth_info.personal-20260729-1847`, `auth_info.backup-20260811-204705` verified present/untouched (5/5). Temporary scratch copies containing recipient numbers were deleted.

### Phase B - how the test suite reached the production bridge (exact path)
1. Entry point: a single `pytest` run over `tests/` (`pytest.ini: testpaths = tests`) executed **inside the production container** (`docker exec autospare_backend ...`). Correction of an earlier statement: the `sec*` accounts are NOT from an ad-hoc script - `tests/test_security.py` builds them as `sec{tag}_{run}@example.com` (rtc/idor_a/inv_b/pay_b/file_b/adm/esc/dext; an f-string my first grep missed); `syscheck_*` = `tests/test_system.py`, `fulltest_*` = `tests/test_full_system.py`.
2. Those tests call `httpx.post("http://localhost:8000/api/v1/auth/register|login", ...)` (hardcoded `BASE_URL`) = the **live production server** and production DB.
3. `routes/auth.py::register` (line 137) and `BACKEND_AUTH_SECURITY.login_user` (line 710) call `create_2fa_code()` -> `WHATSAPP_2FA_PRIMARY=1` -> `send_whatsapp_2fa` -> `social/whatsapp_provider.send_message` -> `WHATSAPP_BRIDGE_URL=http://whatsapp-bridge:3001/send` = production Baileys bridge -> a 2FA text with a support link (=> the "url generation failed" x99) to the user's phone. register + login = **2 sends per user** (48 users x2 + 3 x1 = 99). Phones were generated as real-format `+9725xxxxxxx` numbers, i.e. potentially real people.
4. Root cause of the isolation failure: environment/config isolation - there is no test environment. Tests use the live server, prod DB and prod messaging config, and nothing in the app distinguishes a synthetic principal from a customer at the point of real delivery. (`tests/load_test_150_users.py` would do the same at 300 sends.)

### Minimal root-cause fix (one boundary, enforced at both ends)
- **Server end - the single delivery choke point** `BACKEND_AUTH_SECURITY.create_2fa_code()` (every register/login/resend passes through it): accounts on reserved domains (RFC 2606/6761: example.com/.net/.org, `*.test`, `*.example`, `*.invalid`, `*.localhost`) never receive a real message (WhatsApp OR SMS fallback). The code is still stored/returned, so test flows are unaffected; real customers' behaviour is unchanged (proved by test: WhatsApp still primary, SMS still only the fallback). Helper: new `backend/synthetic_recipients.py`.
- **Test end** `backend/tests/conftest.py` (fail-closed): forces the provider's bridge URL to an unroutable `*.invalid` sink for all in-process code; an explicit `TEST_WHATSAPP_BRIDGE_URL` is honoured but the run **aborts** (`pytest.UsageError`) if it (or the provider URL) is the production bridge; tests may not drive `/auth/register|login|send-2fa|resend` on a non-local host (the real domain) nor with non-reserved emails.
- Deployed: backend restarted (bridge not touched); server started 13:43:59 local, after the edits.

### Regression
`tests/test_whatsapp_test_isolation.py` **37/37** (reserved/real address tables; production-bridge recognition; bridge URL forced to sink; provider send cannot reach production; explicit production override aborts the run; real-domain/real-email test calls blocked; reserved account gets NO WhatsApp/SMS in all three channel modes; real customer delivery unchanged; guard ordered before channel selection). Negative control: with the boundary disabled the same call attempts WhatsApp delivery. Whole `tests/` still collects (657) with the new conftest (collect-only; nothing executed, nothing sent). `noa_ops_readiness_test` 32/32. No live registration probe was fired on purpose.

### Not done (by instruction) / still required
No WhatsApp message sent, no QR, no pairing, archive/original auth not deleted, NOA/Facebook/autonomous mode untouched, nothing committed. WhatsApp remains DOWN until re-paired (procedure reported to the owner). Note: `show_qr.sh` exits "already linked" while `/health` false-reports `connected:true`; with dead creds the bridge never emits a QR, so `auth_info/` must be moved aside first.
Files: `backend/synthetic_recipients.py` (new), `backend/BACKEND_AUTH_SECURITY.py` (+import, +guard), `backend/tests/conftest.py` (new), `backend/tests/test_whatsapp_test_isolation.py` (new), `whatsapp-bridge/auth_info.dead-20260914-2250Z/` + `whatsapp-bridge/evidence-20260914-conflict/` (new evidence, untracked).

## /goal — NOA TWO-PHASE OPERATIONAL READINESS (Phase 1 ACTIVE, Phase 2 READY-but-OFF) — 2026-09-20

**Baseline before changes:** HEAD `0d16c86` (branch `main`, in sync with origin), 60 pre-existing uncommitted/untracked files (Eurosender, WhatsApp, Rex, CLAUDE.md, ...) - none touched except `docker-compose.yml` hunks listed below.

**Scope:** finish architecture/controls/observability now; activate Phase 1 only (SCAN -> IDENTIFY -> ANALYZE -> DRAFT -> OWNER APPROVAL -> PUBLISH/REPLY -> RECORD); make Phase 2 (autonomous) the SAME pipeline behind the existing flag `NOA_ENGAGEMENT_AUTOREPLY` (kept `0`). Not reopened: OAuth, cookie-destruction, persistent-profile fix.

### Defects found (root causes) and fixes
1. **Drafting was broken in production.** Every `draft_group_comment` failed: Cerebras `402 Payment Required` (account credit exhausted - EXTERNAL, not fixable in code), Gemini/Groq-primary 429, and the last fallback `llama-3.3-70b-versatile` -> `404` (model removed from Groq; listed active models verified live: gpt-oss-120b/20b, compound...). Fix: `GROQ_MODEL` default -> `openai/gpt-oss-20b` in `hf_client.py` (2 places), `docker-compose.yml`, `.env` (config, not a credential); verified a real Hebrew completion. Caveat: 20b Hebrew quality is weaker - another reason autonomy stays OFF.
2. **Unbounded, wasteful LLM use in the group loop** (violates the "no LLM in background loops without limits" rule): every rescan re-drafted every already-drafted post. Fix: pre-draft `has_active_draft` check (DB-only) + per-cycle budget `NOA_GROUP_DRAFT_MAX_PER_CYCLE` (20), best-relevance first.
3. **`_save_draft` returned True on a duplicate** (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`), so duplicates looked like new drafts. Now `RETURNING`-based: True only on a real insert (docstring already promised this).
4. **Group approval had no failure/audit model.** A failed post silently reverted to `pending_approval`; a submitted-but-unverified comment (may already be public) was re-approvable => **duplicate public comment risk**. Also non-atomic select-then-update approval, in-memory rate limit (lost on restart), and `owner_console.py` had no `log` defined (its failure handler would itself raise NameError). Fix: migration `0061_noa_ops_audit` (audit columns `posted_at/skipped_at/approved_by/attempts/last_error/last_attempt_at`, terminal `failed` state kept reserved in the dedup index, `social_inbox` audit columns, `noa_scan_runs`); `submit_approved_comment` now reports `submitted`; single shared path `social/noa_ops.py` (atomic claim, outcome model posted / retry / failed / unverified=>failed-not-retryable, DB-backed per-group + autonomous hour/day limits, owner outcome notification).
5. **No persisted source for scan volume / duplicates / session failures** (logs only) - one row per cycle in `noa_scan_runs` (the previous EOD "no new ledger" decision is superseded: those metrics had no persisted source; drafts/approvals/publications still come from the existing tables).
6. **`fb_fetch_new` hid Graph errors** (an invalid token looked like a quiet day) - `FETCH_STATUS` now exposes fetch health; recorded per Page cycle.
7. **Autonomous flag was read once at startup and not even passed into the container.** Now evaluated EVERY cycle through `noa_ops.autonomous_gate` (flag AND readiness; fail-closed) and passed through compose (default 0). Page autonomous sends previously had no safety check - now `safety_check` + rate limit, same `send_page_reply` path the owner uses.
8. **Readiness must not lie:** the WhatsApp bridge `/health` reports `connected:true` while the session is logged out. Added check 13 (real last owner-delivery outcome recorded by `notify_owner`) to the release gate.

### Phase 2 architecture (READY, OFF)
One implementation, actor differs only: owner console `אשרתגובה`/`ענה` and autonomous both use `noa_ops.claim_group_draft` -> `post_claimed_group_draft` / `send_page_reply`. Autonomous adds only gates: flag + readiness gate (checks 1-11,13; not 12) + deterministic `safety_check` (no prices/discounts/phones/foreign links/hashtags/reasoning leaks, length) + hour/day ceilings. Anything doubtful stays `pending_approval` for the owner. Release = deliberate operator action (`NOA_ENGAGEMENT_AUTOREPLY=1` + recreate); readiness/`מוכנות` never change it. Read-only ops endpoint `GET /api/v1/system/noa-ops` (X-Collect-Secret, fail-closed) and WhatsApp commands `מוכנות` / `מדדים`; EOD report now appends the OPERATIONS block (scanned, relevant, rejected, drafts created/approved/rejected/pending/published/failed, duplicate events, session/scanner failures, approval latency, autonomous status, readiness).
Dormant non-audited alternates found (registered, no production caller; guarded by a test): `social/tools.py::facebook_group_comment`, `integrations/facebook_browser/task_queue.py`. Route them through `noa_ops` if ever wired.

### Live verification (real system, no public Facebook writes)
- Real bounded cycle through the production loops (temporary `SOCIAL_GROUP_SCAN_START_DELAY_S=60`/`MAX_GROUPS=4`, since removed): discovery sync 129 groups; 4 groups scanned; `noa_scan_runs`: items_scanned 40, relevant 9, rejected 31, drafted 1, failures 1 (LLM), `session_failed=false`; new draft persisted `pending_approval`; autonomous hook `disabled`. Page cycle: `facebook_ok=true`, 0 comments (Page currently has none - the Page->`social_inbox` path is covered by `engagement_lifecycle_test`, not fabricated).
- Persistent profile authenticated after two `--force-recreate`s with no login/cookie import; group scan ran after recreate.
- In-process readiness (`/api/v1/system/noa-ops`): 12 required checks PASS incl. EOD supervised task running and autonomous OFF; 403 without secret. Check 13 FAIL (see below) => `ready_for_release=false`, gate closed.
- Approved content reaching the Facebook handlers is proven with mocked handlers (`noa_ops_readiness_test`); no unsolicited/test public content created. EOD fires 21:00 IL (scheduled, first run with the new block tonight).

### BLOCKER (external, not modified here): WhatsApp is logged out
`whatsapp_bridge` has been disconnected since 2026-09-14 22:50 UTC (`401 Stream Errored (conflict)`, crash-loop, 155 restarts); every owner send fails `Connection Closed`. Effect: owner notifications AND the WhatsApp approval console (`תגובות-גרופ`, `אשרתגובה`, `ענה`) are unreachable; drafts accumulate safely in the DB (22 pending) and are approvable once re-linked. Needs the owner to re-pair the number (QR / phone) - genuine manual stop; bridge code/auth state deliberately not touched (unrelated uncommitted `whatsapp-bridge/index.js` work exists). Also external: Cerebras billing (402).

### Follow-up (same day): WhatsApp readiness blocker - root cause and fix
**Reported:** "WhatsApp is connected" vs readiness check 13 failing. **Investigated read-only, bridge/auth untouched.**
- **Actual state: CONNECTED_BUT_NOT_AUTHENTICATED.** `/health` = `connected:true` (it is `!!waSocket.user`, true with stale creds), but (1) the authenticated round-trip `GET /groups` -> `500 Connection Closed`, (2) the ONE controlled owner send through the real `notify_owner` path (labelled TEST, single call, alert key `noa_wa_delivery_verification_20260920`) -> `Connection Closed`, bridge log `Send error: Connection Closed`, (3) every bridge start logs `DISCONNECTED code=401 Connection Failure reconnect=false`, then an unhandled `Timed Out` crash (restarts 154 -> 164, crash-loop). Onset: `2026-09-14 22:50:46Z Stream Errored (conflict)` (session displaced); last inbound customer message `2026-09-14 22:36`. No second local container holds the credentials (checked mounts/processes). If your phone shows the device as linked, WhatsApp's server is still rejecting THIS bridge's stored credentials (401) - that is a server-side rejection the bridge cannot recover from without re-pairing.
- **Why "owner delivery outcome" was missing:** not a bridge signal. The outcome recorder (`notify_owner` -> Redis `autospare:owner_delivery:last`) was added in the same deploy that recreated the backend; no owner send had happened yet, so the key was empty when readiness ran. It is populated now (10:46:33 / 10:53:02 / 11:08:44, all `ok:false Connection Closed`). Empty-vs-failed had been conflated in one check.
- **Root cause of the SIX-DAY SILENT OUTAGE (real defect):** `_whatsapp_link_monitor_loop` (built after the identical 2026-07-29 outage, "must alert out-of-band") decided health from the same `/health.connected` flag, so it reported `ok` through both outages and never alerted. **Fix (one place, shared):** `noa_ops.classify_owner_channel` + `probe_owner_channel` use an authenticated round-trip (`/groups`, existing endpoint, no message, no bridge change) plus the last real send outcome. The link monitor now verifies a "connected" bridge with the probe (throttled `WA_LINK_PROBE_INTERVAL_S=1800` to avoid WhatsApp group-fetch rate limits), classifies `session_dead`, and alerts via Telegram + email (never WhatsApp); `unreachable`/`session_dead` are one "link down" class so a crash-looping bridge does not flap alerts. Check 13 uses the same classifier.
- **States now distinguished** (test J1, 11 cases): `DELIVERY_VERIFIED` (usable) / `AUTHENTICATED` (probe-proven, no send evidence yet - usable, an empty outcome is no longer a failure) / `CONNECTED_BUT_NOT_AUTHENTICATED` / `DELIVERY_FAILED` / `AWAITING_QR` / `DISCONNECTED` / `WRONG_ACCOUNT` / `BRIDGE_UNREACHABLE`. A stale failure never masks a probe-proven session.
- **Live result:** readiness check 13 = FAIL `CONNECTED_BUT_NOT_AUTHENTICATED ... Connection Closed`; link monitor logged `state None -> session_dead` and alerted via ['telegram','email'] (real alerts sent by the existing monitor: 2, the first a transient "unreachable" during a bridge crash-restart). **Phase 1 readiness stays BLOCKED** until the device is re-paired; check 13 flips to PASS on its own once the probe succeeds.
- Files: `backend/social/noa_ops.py`, `backend/BACKEND_API_ROUTES.py` (monitor + earlier outcome hook), `backend/devtests/noa_ops_readiness_test.py` (32/32). Bridge, its auth_info and uncommitted `whatsapp-bridge/index.js` untouched.

### Regression (after final deploy)
noa_ops_readiness 32/32 (new), fb_persistent_profile 11/11, fb_auth_lifecycle 25/25, hardening 12/12, degradation 10/10, cookie_destruction 10/10, dom_readiness 71/71, relevance_hardening pass, group_handoff 32/32, group_scan_reporting 11/11, noa_group_draft_handoff 11/11, social_hardening 85/85, social_publishers pass, engagement_lifecycle pass, noa_pipeline_controlled_e2e pass, noa_page_publish_approval pass, noa_eod_report pass. `fb_phase5h_observability_test` still fails = documented stale mock (see 30b), unrelated.

### Files
`backend/social/noa_ops.py` (new), `backend/routes/noa_ops_routes.py` (new), `backend/alembic/versions/0061_noa_ops_audit.py` (new, applied), `backend/devtests/noa_ops_readiness_test.py` (new), modified: `backend/BACKEND_API_ROUTES.py`, `backend/social/engagement.py`, `backend/social/tools.py`, `backend/social/facebook_browser/group_agent.py`, `backend/social/facebook_browser/group_scanner.py`, `backend/agents/owner_console.py`, `backend/hf_client.py`, `docker-compose.yml` (only my hunks: GROQ_MODEL default + NOA control passthrough), `FIXES_TRACKER.md`; `.env` (gitignored): `GROQ_MODEL`.

### Status
Phase 1: pipeline OPERATIONAL end-to-end; owner delivery/approval channel BLOCKED (WhatsApp logged out). Phase 2: architecture READY and verified, autonomous OFF, release gate correctly CLOSED. Autonomous engagement: **OFF**. Not committed/pushed.

## /goal — WHY THE FACEBOOK BROWSER SESSION DID NOT SURVIVE — 2026-09-20

### 30b. Scanner replayed cookies.json into a fresh ephemeral Chromium context; the persistent profile that held the real session was never used

**Evidence (live, not inferred):** `cookies.json` (7 cookies, c_user+xs present, 1-year expiries, written 09:01 Sep 19 by a healthy `_stop()`) failed the 20:10/21:35 health checks: Facebook served the "Continue as <name>" remembered-device picker (classified AMBIGUOUS because the picker check keys on Hebrew "היכנס", not "המשך" - same relogin outcome) and the live context lost c_user/xs = server-side rejection of the replayed `xs`. Meanwhile the native Chrome profile `/app/state/fb_native_test/profile` (c_user/xs refreshed 17:19, survived the 19:35 container recreate, stale SingletonLock harmless) opened headless from the same IP ~11h later was **still fully authenticated** (throwaway-copy test). Failure history shows the cookie-replay design dying repeatedly (AMBIGUOUS daily Aug 24-Sep 12, picker Sep 14-18). Not expired, not destroyed (`_stop()` guard worked; cookies.json intact), not a profile reset, not VNC/Xvfb (production uses neither; Chrome is spawned headless per scan). Only Chrome procs running were amayama's.

**Root cause:** `FacebookSession._start` used `chromium.launch()` + `new_context()` (spoofed Chrome/124 UA) + `add_cookies(cookies.json)`. A session is bound to the browser identity, not to cookies alone; the cookie-only replay does not persist. The persistent profile was a manual experiment no production code referenced.

**Fix (session.py only, single choke point - all 4 GroupAgent entry points use it):** `launch_persistent_context(_PROFILE_DIR)` (env `FB_PROFILE_DIR`, default the established profile; no UA override); cookies.json seeded ONLY when the profile has no c_user+xs (never overwrites live auth with a stale backup); `_stop` closes the context (flushes profile); cookies.json c_user+xs write guard unchanged; in-process `_PROFILE_LOCK` serializes sessions (Chrome allows one owner per profile). Health-check/validation NOT weakened. Not changed: credentials, Page token, cookies, auto-relogin.

**Verified live:** discovery found 129 groups and a 2-group scan ran (`session_failed=False`, 7 discoveries) with no login; session re-verified AUTHENTICATED after `pre_restart.sh` + `docker restart`.

**Tests:** new `fb_persistent_profile_regression_test.py` 11/11 (incl. live copy of real profile + stale backup ignored); lifecycle 25/25, hardening 12/12, degradation 10/10 (updated to expect context.close), cookie_destruction 10/10, relevance 81/81, dom/handoff/group_scan_reporting/noa_group_draft_handoff pass. `fb_phase5h_observability_test` fails in a `_scan_one_group` mock (group_agent, not session.py; not in the prior baseline) - pre-existing/unrelated, not fixed here.

**Closure verification (2026-09-20, same day):**
- **`fb_phase5h_observability_test` = PRE-EXISTING, unrelated.** Fails in Section 7 (`_scan_one_group` with a mocked page; `FacebookSession` is never instantiated). Run against the pre-change HEAD `session.py` (shadow package copy, traceback path `/tmp/base/social/...`) it fails identically at `group_agent.py:682` with `StopAsyncIteration`. Cause: commit `d28c15b` (scanner DOM-readiness/incremental-extraction rewrite, 2026-09-19) makes more `page.evaluate` calls than the test's fixed 3-item `side_effect`; the test file is untracked and predates that rewrite. Sections 1-6 pass. Not fixed here (stale test mock, not a code defect).
- **Identity:** login = personal account `khalil.ay.3` (c_user) acting as the Page/profile "autosparefinder" (i_user). `FACEBOOK_PAGE_ID` and the acting-as `i_user` resolve to the same entity (identical "Manage Page / autosparefinder" heading set) although the numeric ids differ. Intended AutoSpareFinder identity confirmed. Read-only navigation only; no cookies/tokens printed; no public write.
- **Recovery:** `docker compose up -d --force-recreate backend` (config hash identical, nothing unrelated applied): container `d3641f55fc70` -> `b353541893d9`, hostname changed, stale SingletonLock present, no VNC/cookie import -> AUTHENTICATED from the profile, 129 groups discovered, 2/2 groups scanned (`session_failed=False`, 7 discoveries).

**Open, not fixed (out of scope):** session is the AutoSpareFinder identity (`i_user` set) - scanning works, but comment-posting identity should be confirmed by the owner before approving group comments; `playwright_stealth` is absent from requirements (ad-hoc install lost on recreate; only affects fb_browser_login fallback); picker detector misses the Hebrew "המשך" variant.

## /goal — CLOSE NOA FACEBOOK PAGE + EOD REPORTING END-TO-END — 2026-09-19

Scope: extend the closed NOA Facebook Group workflow to (1) verify Page publishing after a
reported "approved but never appeared on the Page" symptom, (2) confirm/wire Page-feed
monitoring with the same approval gate, (3) add EOD reporting. Did **not** reopen the closed
Group workflow — no scanner/DOM/group-draft/group-approval code was touched; full Group
regression (see below) re-confirmed intact.

### 28. Facebook Page post approved via the Telegram-mirror button silently published to Telegram ONLY, yet the row was marked 'published' — root cause of the reported missing Facebook Page post

**Symptom reported by owner**: a post approved "for all platforms" ~2 days earlier never
appeared on the AutoSpareFinder Facebook Page.

**Investigation (code + live DB evidence, not inference)**: found the exact row —
`social_posts` id `8a6e91a7-edfd-4273-9444-7705706ea262`, `platforms=[discord,facebook,
telegram,tiktok]`, created 2026-09-17 17:02, `status='published'`, `published_at`
2026-09-17 17:29, **`approved_at IS NULL`**, `external_post_ids = {"telegram":565,
"published_platforms":["telegram"], ...}` — no `facebook`/`discord`/`tiktok` key anywhere.
The exact shape of `external_post_ids` (a `published_platforms` wrapper key, no
`approved_at`) uniquely fingerprints which of the TWO existing "approve a NOA post" code
paths ran: **not** the canonical WhatsApp `אשר <id>` command
(`agents/owner_console.py::_approve_and_publish`, which merges bare `published` with no
wrapper key and always calls `campaign_manager.approve_post_content()` first, setting
`approved_at`), but the **Telegram-mirror inline-button callback**
(`routes/webhooks.py::telegram_admin_webhook`'s `"approve_post"` handler) — a SECOND,
independently-written implementation of "approve and publish."

**Root cause**: that Telegram-callback implementation (a) **unconditionally published to
Telegram first**, regardless of which platform button was tapped ("every approved post goes
out here" — literal comment in the removed code); (b) only ever attempted **one** additional
platform, and explicitly **excluded `"telegram"`/`"post"`/`""`** from that single slot — so
whenever the tapped button's platform value resolved to any of those three (the generic
"✅ אשר פרסום" button, or a per-day post whose own `platform` label happened to be
`"telegram"`), Facebook/Discord/TikTok were **never attempted at all**; (c) marked the
**entire** multi-platform row `status='published'` the instant that lone unconditional
Telegram send succeeded — giving a false "fully published" signal while the row's other
target platforms were silently untouched; (d) never called
`campaign_manager.approve_post_content()`, so `approved_at` was never recorded — the human
approval itself left no audit trail on this path, unlike the WhatsApp path.

**Fix (root-fix, one canonical implementation — not a downstream patch)**: `routes/
webhooks.py`'s `"approve_post"` callback now resolves the real `SocialPost` row by its
`social_post_id` and delegates to the **same** `agents.owner_console._approve_and_publish()`
the WhatsApp path already uses. Both approval channels now share one implementation, one
`approve_post_content()` call (idempotent — a duplicate approval from either channel is a
safe no-op, verified live), and one per-platform loop over the row's real `platforms` column
— so an approval via either channel now genuinely attempts every platform on the post,
Facebook included, and `approved_at` is always recorded. A legacy fallback (best-effort
single-platform dispatch) is kept for the rare case of a `pending_post` memory entry with no
`social_post_id` (predates `_noa_enqueue_social_post`).

**Second, smaller defect found and fixed while consolidating**: `_approve_and_publish()`
itself did not carry the `p != "tiktok"` exemption the (now-removed) Telegram-callback code
had for TikTok's media-required check — `registry.dispatch("tiktok", ...)` auto-generates a
branded video when no `media_url` is supplied (`social/registry.py`), so TikTok must never
be skipped for "media required" the way Instagram genuinely must be. Consolidating both
approval channels onto `_approve_and_publish()` would have silently regressed TikTok's
existing no-media capability for the (now-unified) Telegram-button path. Ported the same
exemption into `_approve_and_publish()` — verified live (mocked dispatch) that TikTok is
still attempted with no `media_url` and Instagram-class media-required platforms still skip
correctly.

mechanism, as required): `fb_fetch_new()` reads the Page's recent posts
(`GET /{page_id}/posts`) then each post's comments (`GET /{post_id}/comments`), capturing
external_id, parent post id, author, text, permalink, and timestamp — fed through

(posting to the Page) — the three concepts (publish-to-Page / read-feed / respond-to-item)
are already cleanly separated exactly as this task's Phase D requires. No relevance-score
field is computed for Page comments today (unlike Groups' 0.2/0.4 thresholds) — a reasoned

### 29. NOA End-of-Day Facebook Activity report — new (Phase D, not a bug fix)

**Added** `_noa_eod_report_loop()` (`BACKEND_API_ROUTES.py`, registered via
`_supervised_task("noa_eod_report_loop", ...)`), firing once/day at a fixed IL local time
(`NOA_EOD_REPORT_HOUR_IL`, default 21:00 — same fixed-local-time pattern as
`_noa_marketing_loop`, not a container-uptime timer). Reuses the EXISTING owner channel
(`notify_owner` → `_wa_send_quiet`, quiet-hours-safe, daily `alert_key` dedup) — no new
transport. Every count comes from a real persisted table over a rolling 24h window (a plain
timestamp-bounded query, not a second parallel activity ledger, so a restart or re-run can
never double-count a prior day): `group_comment_drafts` for GROUPS, `social_inbox` (platform=
facebook) for PAGE, `social_posts` (all platforms) for PLATFORM RESULT and the
JSONB-`external_post_ids ? 'facebook'`-gated "Original Page posts published" count.

**Real architectural asymmetries surfaced and reported honestly, not hidden**: (1) for
Groups, "identified" and "drafted" are the same number by design — a discovery below the
0.4 relevance threshold is reported to the owner in the scan-cycle WhatsApp message but never
persisted anywhere, so counting it here would require a new ledger (explicitly against this
task's own instructions). (2) for Page items, "approved" and "published" are the same number
— the engagement pipeline's `ענה <id>` approves-and-sends in one atomic step, unlike Groups'
3-state `pending_approval → approved → posted`. (3) `social_posts` has no distinct terminal
`'failed'` status in its schema CHECK constraint — a row stuck at `'approved'` with no
`published_at` (every platform dispatch failed) is reported as "approved but not fully
published" rather than inventing a status the schema doesn't have.

**Bug found + fixed while building this**: `social_posts.created_at` is a plain (naive)
`DateTime` column (populated via `datetime.utcnow()`) while `group_comment_drafts`/
`social_inbox` use `TIMESTAMPTZ` — binding a tz-aware Python datetime
(`datetime.now(APP_LOCAL_TZ)`) against the naive column raised
`asyncpg.DataError: can't subtract offset-naive and offset-aware datetimes` (caught live
while testing, before this ever reached production). Fixed by using `datetime.utcnow()`
(naive) for the window boundary — verified live to compare correctly against both column
types.

**Files**: `backend/BACKEND_API_ROUTES.py` (new `_noa_eod_report_loop`, `startup()`
registration), new `backend/devtests/noa_eod_report_test.py` (6 structural checks — reuse of
`notify_owner`, no new ledger/table, fixed-local-time scheduling, daily alert-key dedup,
naive-UTC window — plus a controlled functional test seeding synthetic rows across all three
tables and asserting the exact identified/drafted/approved/published/pending counts and the
JSONB Page-post check; no WhatsApp message is sent by the test).

**STATUS: PASS.**

### 30. Blocker cleared same day — owner supplied a fresh token; completed the exchange chain and live-verified Phase B/C end-to-end

The owner supplied a fresh Facebook token and asked for a restart to pick it up. `docker
restart` alone does **not** re-read `.env`, so the container kept serving the stale token
until `docker compose up -d backend` recreated it (ran `pre_restart.sh` both times, per the
standing rule). The token that landed was a valid, unexpired **USER** access token — not
yet the page-specific token `facebook_publisher.py`/`engagement.py` require — so the direct
`/{page_id}/feed` POST correctly failed closed with Facebook's own
`(#200) ... requires pages_read_engagement and pages_manage_posts as an admin` error.
`debug_token` confirmed `"type":"USER"`, all required scopes present with the right
`target_ids`, but `expires_at` was only ~2 hours out — a **short-lived** user token, one step
short of CLAUDE.md's own documented chain (`app_secret → fb_exchange_token → long-lived →
non-expiring page token`).

**Second, genuine root cause found (not the owner's fault — a pre-existing infra gap)**:
completing that documented chain requires `FACEBOOK_APP_ID`/`FACEBOOK_APP_SECRET`, both
correctly present in `.env` but **never wired into `docker-compose.yml`'s explicit backend
`environment:` block** (unlike `FACEBOOK_PAGE_ID`/`FACEBOOK_PAGE_TOKEN`, which are) — the
container has never had access to them. This is exactly why the durable-token refresh had
apparently never been completed end-to-end before. **Fixed**: added
`FACEBOOK_APP_ID: ${FACEBOOK_APP_ID:-}` / `FACEBOOK_APP_SECRET: ${FACEBOOK_APP_SECRET:-}`
to `docker-compose.yml` (same pattern as the two existing lines), recreated the container.

With app id/secret now reachable, completed the full chain against the live Graph API:
short-lived user token → `fb_exchange_token` (60-day long-lived user token) → `GET
/{page_id}?fields=access_token` → resulting page token confirmed via `debug_token`:
`"type":"PAGE"`, **`"expires_at":0`** (non-expiring, matching the documented durable-credential
pattern), all required scopes present. Written to `.env` as the new `FACEBOOK_PAGE_TOKEN`,
container recreated once more.

**Live end-to-end verification (Phase B/C, previously BLOCKED — now genuinely proven, not
just mocked)**: re-ran the exact controlled test from #28 through the real, fixed
`agents.owner_console._approve_and_publish()` production path — this time reaching the REAL
Graph API. Result: `✅ אושר ופורסם: facebook: ✅ פורסם`; DB row shows `approved_at` and
`published_at` both set and `external_post_ids.facebook = "1170174359502072_
122131770902736783"` — a real Facebook post id. Fetched that exact post back from Facebook
(`GET /{post_id}`) and got a real `permalink_url` pointing at the live AutoSpareFinder Page —
proof the fixed approval path genuinely publishes to Facebook end-to-end, not merely that the
code no longer skips the attempt. Immediately deleted the test post (`DELETE /{post_id}` →
`{"success":true}`, re-fetch → object no longer exists) and the synthetic `social_posts` row,
per this task's own instruction to never leave an unwanted public post. Also live-verified
Page-feed reading (Phase C): `GET /{page_id}/posts` returned 5 real posts with no error
(confirming the credential fix reaches `social/engagement.py` too, which shares the same
token); `fb_fetch_new()` itself correctly returned 0 items because none of those 5 posts
currently have comments — genuinely empty, not a silent failure (verified by inspecting the
raw Graph API response directly, not trusting the wrapper alone).

## /goal — CLOSE NOA FACEBOOK POST HANDLING END-TO-END — 2026-09-19

Scope: trace the complete lifecycle of a genuine relevant Facebook group post discovered by
the scanner (now CLOSED/PASS, `d28c15b`) through relevance decision, persistence/handoff, NOA
decision, approval gate, Facebook execution, and result recording — with code evidence at every
stage, not inference from names/docs. Did **not** reopen the closed scanner investigation; no
scanner/DOM/extraction code was touched.

### 26. Scheduled Facebook-group-scan path never drafted or persisted a comment for any discovery — owner-facing approval commands had nothing to approve

**Root cause**: `_group_scan_loop()` (`BACKEND_API_ROUTES.py`, the automatic scheduled scan) calls
`facebook_group_scan()` → `GroupAgent.scan_groups()` and gets back discovery dicts (post_url,
post_text, group_id, relevance_score, suggested_action) — but nothing in this call chain ever
generated a draft comment or wrote a row to `group_comment_drafts`. The owner-facing WhatsApp
notification already read `d.get("draft_comment", "(אין)")`, so it silently always showed the
"(none)" placeholder, and the WhatsApp approval commands (`תגובות-גרופ`/`אשרתגובה <id>`,
`agents/owner_console.py`) had zero rows to ever act on for anything the automatic scan found.
A bridge function clearly written to solve exactly this, `campaign_manager.py::create_group_discovery_tasks()`
(drafts via the same `draft_group_comment()`, but never persists), was confirmed via a
repo-wide `grep` to have **zero callers anywhere** — dead code, never wired in.

Separately verified as NOT affected: the manually-triggered path (`agents/owner_console.py`'s
`"סרוק קבוצות"` WhatsApp command → `group_scanner.run_group_scanner()`) already had its own
correct draft+persist loop and was never broken — this defect was isolated to the scheduled
loop only.

**Fix**: Inserted a draft+persist block into `_group_scan_loop()` (right after `discoveries =
result.data.get("discoveries", [])`, before any reporting code runs) that, for each discovery
whose `suggested_action` is `"comment"` or `"post"` (i.e. `relevance_score >= 0.4` — discoveries
scored only high enough for `"monitor"` are deliberately left undrafted), calls the exact same
pre-existing `GroupAgent.draft_group_comment()` and `group_scanner._save_draft()` functions
already proven correct in `run_group_scanner()`'s own loop — no duplicate INSERT logic, no new
dedup logic (relies on the existing DB-level unique index; see #27 for a widening of that
index found while proving this fix). Populates `discovery["draft_comment"]` in place so the
pre-existing, unmodified notification code starts working correctly.

**Files**: `backend/BACKEND_API_ROUTES.py` (`_group_scan_loop`), new
`backend/devtests/noa_group_draft_handoff_test.py` (11 tests, source-text-level — importing
`BACKEND_API_ROUTES.py` directly hangs on unrelated startup side effects in this environment,
same constraint as `group_scan_reporting_test.py`).

**STATUS: PASS.**

### 27. `group_comment_drafts` dedup index only covered `pending_approval` — a rescanned already-answered post could re-enter the approval queue

**Root cause** (found while designing the controlled test for #26, not a defect introduced by
#26 — pre-existing since the 0059 migration, 2026-08-11): the unique index
`uq_group_comment_drafts_pending_post` was `ON group_comment_drafts (post_url) WHERE
status='pending_approval'` — a partial index scoped to ONE status. Once a draft moved to
`'approved'` or `'posted'`, its `post_url` became free again, so a later rescan of the SAME
still-visible Facebook post (plausible — popular posts stay visible for days across multiple
daily scan cycles) could insert a **second** `'pending_approval'` draft asking the owner to
approve another comment on a post NOA had already commented on. The approval gate itself was
never bypassed (an owner action is still required either way — this was never an automatic
duplicate post), but an owner who didn't recognize the repeated post could unknowingly approve
a genuine duplicate public comment.

**Fix (root-fix, not a downstream guard)**: New migration `0060_drafts_dedup_active` widens the
partial unique index to `WHERE status IN ('pending_approval','approved','posted')`, so a rescan
can never re-draft a post that already has a live or in-flight comment. `'skipped'` is
deliberately left out of the index — the owner explicitly declined that draft, and NOA may
legitimately propose a different comment on the same post later. `_save_draft()`'s existing
`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` (no explicit conflict target) automatically honors the new, wider
constraint with zero application-code change. Applied live via `alembic upgrade head`
(verified: 1 pre-existing unrelated `pending_approval` row from 2026-08-18 untouched, live
`pg_indexes` definition confirmed to include exactly `pending_approval`/`approved`/`posted`
and exclude `skipped`).

**Files**: new `backend/alembic/versions/0060_drafts_dedup_active.py`; extended
`backend/devtests/noa_group_draft_handoff_test.py` (+3 tests, including one that queries the
live index definition, not just the migration source).

**STATUS: PASS.**

### Controlled end-to-end proof (section 3 requirement — no real Facebook action taken)

New `backend/devtests/noa_pipeline_controlled_e2e_test.py` runs the REAL production functions
(`GroupAgent.draft_group_comment()` — a genuine LLM call, not a Facebook action —
`group_scanner._save_draft()`, and the exact SQL `owner_console.py`'s `אשרתגובה <id>` handler
executes) against the live catalog DB with a synthetic test group + post_url, proving the full
state machine end to end:

```
discovery (post_text/url/group_id/score=0.667/suggested_action="comment")
  → draft_group_comment() → real drafted Hebrew text
  → _save_draft() → group_comment_drafts row, status='pending_approval'
  → rescan while pending → still exactly 1 row (dedup, #27 unaffected — pre-existing behavior)
  → owner-approval SQL → status='approved'
  → second approve attempt on same row → 0 rows affected (cannot double-fire)
  → GroupAgent.submit_approved_comment() PATCHED to a mocked no-op (ok=True) — the ONLY step
    that would ever touch real Facebook; never actually invoked against Facebook
  → status update → status='posted'
  → rescan after posted → still exactly 1 row (NEW #27 dedup, proven live against the 0060 index)
  → cleanup — zero rows left behind
```

All 7 steps + cleanup passed. No real Facebook comment/post was made. No WhatsApp message was
sent. Test group/post rows removed after the run; production `group_comment_drafts` table
verified to hold only its 1 pre-existing unrelated row afterward.

**Observation, not a defect in scope**: the real LLM-drafted comment in this test cited a
specific OEM code (`23140-J150`) the model was not given — `draft_group_comment()`'s prompt
accuracy is pre-existing code untouched by this task; the mandatory owner-approval gate before
any comment is ever posted is precisely the safety net for this class of risk. Flagged for a
future session focused on `draft_group_comment()` itself, not fixed here (out of this task's
root-cause scope).

### Approval gate — verified with code evidence (section E), multi-layered, all owner-only

1. **Group-level**: `group_targets.status` `pending → approved` only via the owner's WhatsApp
   `אשרגרופ <id>` (`owner_console.py::_fb_group_approve`), which only matches
   `status='pending'` rows — reachable only through the owner-console command dispatch gated to
   `OWNER_WHATSAPP_PHONE` in `routes/webhooks.py`, ahead of the customer-facing brain.
2. **Comment-level**: `group_comment_drafts.status` `pending_approval → approved` only via
   owner WhatsApp `אשרתגובה <id>` (`owner_console.py::_fb_comment_approve`), which only matches
   `status='pending_approval'` rows — a second approve attempt on the same id is a no-op
   (verified live in the controlled test above).
3. **Tool-level defense in depth**: the separate LLM/agent-invocable tool surface
   (`social/tools.py::facebook_group_comment`/`facebook_group_publish`, registered in the
   `TOOLS` dict) independently fails closed on a live DB check that `group_targets.status ==
   'approved'` before ever calling the browser agent — even if an agent's own LLM reasoning
   decided to call the tool, it cannot succeed unless the owner already flipped that group's
   status via WhatsApp.
4. **Code-level invariant**: `APPROVAL_REQUIRED = True` is hardcoded in `group_agent.py` and
   checked inside `submit_approved_comment()`/`publish_group_post()` themselves — raises
   `RuntimeError` if ever `False`, so no caller can silently disable it.
5. `digital_department/policy.py` independently classifies both tool names as
   "Browser-approval-gated" and forbids even advisory marketing-context injection into them.

**Dead code found, NOT fixed (unreachable today, out of root-cause scope)**:
`integrations/facebook_browser/task_queue.py::BrowserTaskQueue` has zero callers anywhere in
the codebase (confirmed via grep) and, unlike `social/tools.py`'s gated functions, its
`_execute()` calls `submit_approved_comment`/`publish_group_post` with **no internal approval
check** — if a future change ever wires a caller to `BrowserTaskQueue.submit(action=
"group_comment"|"group_publish", ...)`, that caller MUST add its own approval-status check
first (mirroring `social/tools.py`'s pattern), or this becomes a real bypass. Recorded here so
a future session does not have to rediscover it.

### Execution — verified with code evidence (section F)

`submit_approved_comment()`/`publish_group_post()` (`group_agent.py`) use **Playwright browser
automation against the authenticated cookie session — NOT the Graph API** — navigate to the
post/group URL, locate the comment box, type at a human cadence, submit, and verify success by
checking the rendered page content contains a text fragment of the submitted comment; rate
limited to 3 actions/hour/group (`_is_rate_limited`/`_record_rate`); on failure, saves a
diagnostic screenshot (`_save_failure_screenshot`) and returns `{ok:False, error}`. The
approval-command background task (`owner_console.py::_fb_comment_approve`) records `'posted'`
only on confirmed success and reverts to `'pending_approval'` on failure (a legitimate,
owner-visible retry path, not a silent/automatic retry).

## Session — 2026-09-19 (Facebook Group Scanner — session recovery, discovery sync, DOM extraction root fixes)

Full arc: Facebook browser-session recovery → group discovery/DB sync root-cause fix → group scanner DOM-timing root fix → group scanner wrong-DOM-population extraction fix (partial, one sub-defect remains open). Control group used throughout: `https://www.facebook.com/groups/1098496702081798/` (owner manually confirmed 5 relevant part-request posts here; scanner originally found 0).

### 19. Facebook browser session was blocked (ACCOUNT_PICKER) on server-side Playwright; PoC chain to find a workaround
**Investigation**: Server-IP Playwright got ACCOUNT_PICKER even with valid cookies (`social/facebook_browser/session.py`). Audited: cookie-injection into a non-CDP Chrome (failed — Facebook still stripped `c_user`/`xs` even with zero CDP surface, confirmed via full process-tree inspection), then a genuine manual VNC login directly into a persistent server-side Chrome profile (`/app/state/fb_native_test/profile`, Xvfb `:97`, x11vnc, owner-driven).
**Fix**: Owner logged in manually via VNC into the persistent profile; cookies captured via the EXISTING `maintenance/fb_cookie_import.py` (human-run, values never touched by the agent — credential-handling boundary preserved throughout). Imported into production `/app/state/fb_browser_session/cookies.json`.
**Result**: `FacebookSession._health_check()` now returns `AUTHENTICATED — profile link found` (full DOM signal, not the IP-filtered cookie-presence fallback) — a stronger result than any prior session state.
**Regression**: `fb_session_hardening_regression_test.py` (12/12), `fb_cookie_destruction_regression_test.py` (10/10), `fb_session_degradation_regression_test.py` (10/10) — all pass, confirming `_stop()`'s degradation-protection guard still holds and a healthy session still persists correctly.
**STATUS: PASS.**

### 20. `group_targets` was a stale one-time snapshot — no automatic Facebook group discovery sync existed
**Root cause**: `discover_account_groups()` (real, live, fully-paginated — confirmed via debug-level scroll-stabilization trace) is only ever invoked by the manual WhatsApp command `"סרוק קבוצות"` (`agents/owner_console.py` → `group_scanner.run_group_scanner()`). The scheduled `_group_scan_loop()` (`BACKEND_API_ROUTES.py`) never discovers — it only re-scans whatever is already in the DB. Last real discovery event: 2026-08-18 (31 days before this session). Live discovery found **265** currently-joined groups vs. **33** rows in `group_targets` — of the 32 real pre-existing rows, only 1 (`pishpeshuk.cars`) was still a current membership; the other 31 remained scannable only because they are public groups the account had since left.
**Fix**: Inserted a discovery+upsert step (reusing `discover_account_groups()` + the existing `_upsert_discovered_groups()` verbatim — zero duplicate logic) at the start of every `_group_scan_loop()` cycle, wrapped in its own try/except so a discovery failure never blocks scanning. Also removed a stale hardcoded `scan_limit=129` default in `run_group_scanner()` (→ `None` = unbounded), which would have silently truncated scanning once discovery grew the population past 129.
**Files**: `BACKEND_API_ROUTES.py` (`_group_scan_loop`), `social/facebook_browser/group_scanner.py` (`run_group_scanner`).
**Live verification**: Discovery sync run twice — first run `discovered=265, new_count=264` (33→297 total rows); second run (idempotency check, post-restart) `discovered=265, new_count=0`. Zero deletions, zero status changes, zero rejections in both passes (verified by full before/after row-ID diff). Known test-contamination rows (`handoff_test_placeholder_999`, `dup_endpoint_a` — added during endpoint testing earlier in this session) and a pre-existing `musahnikim` duplicate (dated 2026-08-09/10, predates this session) were left untouched per scope.
**Regression**: 145/145 (`fb_group_handoff_test.py` 32, `fb_cookie_destruction_regression_test.py` 10, `fb_session_hardening_regression_test.py` 12, `fb_session_degradation_regression_test.py` 10, `fb_relevance_hardening_regression_test.py` 81) — re-confirmed after a production `docker restart autospare_backend`.
**STATUS: PASS.** Current population: 297 total / 297 eligible / 5 approved / 292 pending / 0 rejected.

### 21. Facebook group scanner DOM-readiness race — empty skeletons / comment fragments misreported as "zero posts"
**Root cause**: `_scan_one_group()` queried `[role="article"]` immediately after a fixed sleep (`domcontentloaded` + 2-4s + 2 short scrolls), with no verification that Facebook had actually streamed real feed content in yet. Confirmed live: 11/33 groups showed `dom_candidates=2, text_valid=0` with both matched elements at `text_length=0` (empty skeleton placeholders). Readiness itself used the same narrow selector/threshold, so a comment fragment (`"name · 1d 0526425133 Reply Share"`, ≥30 chars) could satisfy "ready" before real content existed — confirmed via 30s extended polling on 2 affected groups: both stayed at `count=2, nonempty=0, maxLen=0` for the entire window (stable empty state, not a slow-render race — ruling out "just wait longer" as a fix).
**Fix**: `_wait_for_feed_readiness()` rebuilt as a bounded poll (default 8s timeout, 0.5s interval, never raises) against a shared classifier function (`_IS_REAL_POST_JS_FN`), reused identically by both the readiness check and the extractor so they can never drift apart on what counts as "ready."
**Files**: `social/facebook_browser/group_agent.py`.
**Regression**: New `fb_dom_readiness_regression_test.py` (9/9 at this stage) + 145/145 existing = 154/154.
**STATUS: PASS** (this specific timing defect is fully closed; superseded by #22 below, which found the readiness/extraction selector itself was also matching the wrong DOM population).

### 22. Facebook group scanner extraction: `[role="article"]` matched comment fragments, never the real posts — PARTIALLY FIXED, ONE SUB-DEFECT LEFT OPEN
**Root cause (confirmed via live DOM forensics on the control group)**: `[role="article"]` consistently matched only comment-tail fragments (`role="article"` self-attribute, "Reply/Share" text, `aria-label="Comment by ..."`) and empty skeletons — **zero genuine posts ever matched it**. Genuine posts (owner-confirmed: Hyundai Tucson 2008 dashboard-part request, Toyota Corolla Hybrid hinges-for-sale, Chevy Malibu 2007 fuse-box request — all with real automotive text, 2 with images) lived in plain `DIV`/`SPAN` elements with **no** `role`/`data-pagelet`/`.du4w35lb` ancestor within 10-12 examined levels. Additionally, **Facebook virtualizes off-screen feed content** — a post present after one scroll depth can be unmounted from the DOM after scrolling further (confirmed: Tucson present after normal 2×600px scroll, gone after extended scroll to bottom) — so a "scroll to the end, extract once" architecture structurally loses posts regardless of selector correctness.
**Fix implemented** (`social/facebook_browser/group_agent.py`):
- Broadened candidate search to `div, span, li, article` unioned with the legacy selector (kept for defensiveness).
- Exclusion-based classifier (`_IS_REAL_POST_JS_FN`): rejects comment signatures (`Reply`/`Share`/`LikeReplyShare`/`View more answers`), sidebar/UI chrome (composer placeholder, "About", online-status widget, suggested-contacts, Page-added notices, ≥3× repeated "Facebook" avatar alt-text), and the group's own "Public group...members...Join Group" header card text. Deliberately does not require an image or composer (real posts can be text-only).
- Incremental scroll+extract loop replacing the old single-shot pass: extracts at every scroll step (bounded `_MAX_SCROLL_STEPS=6`, stops early after `_STABILITY_STEPS=2` no-new-post steps) and accumulates into an in-memory dict keyed by post URL (fallback: normalized text prefix) — survives later virtualization.
- **Structural `/members/` exclusion** (same-DOM confirmed): a candidate whose subtree/ancestor links to the CURRENT group's own `/groups/<id>/members` page is the group's header, never a post — verified against 2 genuine posts captured in the SAME DOM snapshot, neither of which ever carried this link (both linked to `/groups/<id>/user/<userid>/` instead). Implemented as a **bounded ancestor walk** (depth ≤8, starting at the element itself) after an initial descendant-only implementation was proven insufficient live (missed the bare-group-name case because the link lives on an ancestor, not inside the minimal text-matching element's own subtree).
- Fixed a narrow regex gap along the way: `^About ` (literal space) → `^About\s` (any whitespace) — the actual DOM text is `"About\n..."`.
**Live verification progression** (same control group, 4 sequential runs): text_valid 3→74→98→76→66 (comment-fragments-only → real population); discoveries went from 0 relevant posts found (pre-fix) to consistently finding the Tucson and/or Malibu genuine posts across every post-fix run. **No genuine post was ever rejected by any of these changes** (verified explicitly on every run — this was the standing stop condition throughout).
**REMAINING OPEN DEFECT (do not re-investigate from scratch — read this first)**: The group's own name/header/about text renders through **multiple distinct widget contexts** on this page — confirmed instances: `/members/` context (fixed), a `"Join Group"/"Share"` context (NOT covered by the `/members/` rule — different link target), and a plain About-description context with **zero links at all** in its ancestor chain (structurally isolated, cannot be excluded by any link-based rule). A dedicated same-DOM common-ancestor investigation (real DOM node identity, not text heuristics) found the bare-name/members/Join-Group trio DO share a common ancestor (confirmed one connected header block), but the About-description fragment shares no common ancestor with that trio within 12 levels, and only one genuine post was present in that specific investigation session (not the required two) — so the evidence bar for a unified "group-header identity" signal was explicitly NOT met. **Do not assume group-name-text-matching is a viable next step — it was deliberately ruled out** (a legitimate post could mention the group's own name). Next investigation should either: (a) capture a session with 2+ genuine posts AND the About-description fragment simultaneously present to properly test a common-ancestor signal beyond 12 levels, or (b) treat this as an acceptable residual false-positive rate rather than continuing to chase individual link-target variants.
**Files changed**: `backend/social/facebook_browser/group_agent.py`, `backend/devtests/fb_dom_readiness_regression_test.py` (grown to 55 tests across this and #21).
**Regression**: 200/200 (55 new/extraction + 145 existing) at last code change.
**Safety**: Zero DB writes, zero discovery/upsert invocations, zero posting/commenting/reacting, zero WhatsApp sends, zero session/cookie modifications across the entire investigation chain (verified before/after every single live test). No restart performed for this specific set of changes (only #20 required one). No commit/push.
**Git**: HEAD `2d405a1df51b546f8a307d837754a4ee5f0df5e7` (branch `main`) unchanged throughout — all changes are uncommitted working-tree edits pending owner review.
**STATUS: PARTIAL → CLOSED same day, see #23 below.** Core architecture (population, virtualization-safety, readiness) was root-fixed and evidenced here; the identity-classification sub-problem flagged as open was resolved by a single unifying root cause (#23), not by further per-widget patches.

### 23. Facebook group scanner: ONE root-level classification boundary replaces the per-widget header/about exclusion chase (closes #22's open item)
**Root cause**: Chasing individual group-header widget variants one at a time (`/members/` link, About-prefix regex, "Public group...Join Group" text, repeated-"Facebook" chrome) was structurally unbounded — Facebook renders its own group name/header/about content through multiple, uncorrelated widget contexts, and each newly-discovered rendering required its own new exclusion rule. A dedicated live investigation found the actual root distinction: Facebook exposes exactly **one `role="feed"` ARIA landmark** on the group page, wrapping only the real scrollable post list. In the same DOM snapshot: all 4 previously-fragmented group-owned variants (bare name, `"Join Group"/"Share"`, members-count, and critically the plain About-description that had **zero links at all** and defeated every link-based exclusion) sat **outside** this landmark; a confirmed genuine post (Tucson) sat **inside** it.
**Fix**: Added one containment check — `feedRoot.contains(el)` — computed once per extraction/readiness call (not per-candidate, for performance) and checked first in `isRealPostCandidate()`. Fails open (skips the check) if no `role="feed"` landmark exists on a given page variant, so the existing text/link exclusions remain as a fallback rather than a hard dependency. All previously-added exclusions (`/members/` ancestor-walk, About-prefix, comment signatures) were left in place as defense-in-depth — none were removed, since they remain independently correct and some (comment rejection) are NOT redundant with feed-containment (a comment can itself be a descendant of a feed-contained post).
**Files**: `backend/social/facebook_browser/group_agent.py` (`_IS_REAL_POST_JS_FN`, `_POST_READY_JS`, `_POST_CANDIDATE_JS`), `backend/devtests/fb_dom_readiness_regression_test.py` (+14 tests: `TestFeedLandmarkRootBoundary`).
**Live verification** (same control group, post-fix): first run — 6 discoveries, **zero** header/about/join-group false positives (all 6 were genuine automotive posts, including 2 newly-detected ones: a Mitsubishi Lancer engine/gearbox-mount request and an Arabic Mercedes/Crafter parts listing). Confirmed no genuine post was ever rejected by this change.
**Regression**: 64/64 in the FB extraction suite at this step (200 existing + 14 new − wait, see final count in #24) — full detail in #24's consolidated count.
**STATUS: PASS.**

### 24. Facebook group scanner: duplicate-inflation from nested DOM wrapper variants of the same post
**Symptom** (surfaced live during #23's validation): a single genuine Arabic parts-for-sale post ("قطع غيار لحافلات مرسيدس وكرافتر +972598444670") was extracted as **4 separate discoveries** — not false positives individually, but 4 different nested-ancestor text captures of the same underlying post, none carrying a reliable post-specific URL (all fell back to the bare group URL).
**Root cause**: The accumulator's dedup key was `post_url if post_url else text[:80].lower()`. Nested DOM wrappers around one post naturally produce several valid ≥30-char text captures that are substring/superstring variants of each other (e.g. the bare phrase vs. phrase+phone vs. full listing+business-card suffix) — each produces a *different* 80-char prefix, so none collided under the old key.
**Fix**: Candidates with a reliable, post-specific URL (i.e., not equal to the bare `group_url`) keep the exact existing exact-match dedup behavior, unchanged. Candidates with no reliable URL are now merged by normalized (whitespace-collapsed, lowercased) substring relationship against other same-bucket candidates: an exact-or-substring match is dropped, a superstring replaces the existing (shorter) entry with the more complete text. Distinct posts that merely share the bare-group-URL fallback are NOT merged unless an actual substring relationship exists.
**Files**: `backend/social/facebook_browser/group_agent.py` (`_scan_one_group`, new `_normalize_for_merge` helper), `backend/devtests/fb_dom_readiness_regression_test.py` (+7 tests: `TestSubstringMergeDedup`, `TestNestedWrapperDuplicateInflation`).
**Live verification** (same control group, final run): **3 discoveries, 3 genuine, 0 false positives, 0 duplicates** — Mitsubishi Lancer engine/gearbox mount, Hyundai Tucson dashboard/AC part (consistently detected across many runs), Chevy Malibu fuse box (consistently detected across many runs). `text_valid` dropped 59→24 in the same session purely from merge collapse, confirming the fix is doing real work, not just coincidentally quiet feed content.
**STATUS: PASS.**

### 25. Facebook group-scan owner-facing reporting: wrong key + masked auth failures
**Root cause**: `_group_scan_loop()` (BACKEND_API_ROUTES.py) read `result.data.get("groups_scanned", 0)` — a key `facebook_group_scan()` (social/tools.py) never sets (it returns `groups_selected`/`groups_attempted`/`groups_fetched`) — so the owner-facing "N groups scanned" WhatsApp message always silently showed 0. Separately, the loop never checked `result.data["session_failed"]`, so a Facebook authentication failure fell through to the same "no discoveries found" branch and was reported to the owner as an ordinary empty scan ("סרקנו 0 קבוצות... לא נמצאו דיונים"), masking a real outage as a quiet day.
**Fix**: (1) Read `groups_attempted` instead — the correct semantic for "we scanned N groups" (incremented per-group before the attempt, regardless of later success/failure; not `groups_selected`, the eligible-input count before any attempt, nor `groups_fetched`, which excludes attempted-but-errored groups). (2) Added an `if session_failed:` branch (checked before the empty-discoveries branch) that sends a distinct, honest `severity="error"` alert (`alert_key="group_scan_auth_failed"`) instead of silently falling through.
**Files**: `backend/BACKEND_API_ROUTES.py` (`_group_scan_loop`), new `backend/devtests/group_scan_reporting_test.py` (11 tests). Note: this test suite verifies the fix at the **source-text level** rather than by importing/executing `BACKEND_API_ROUTES.py` — that module hangs on unrelated startup side-effects when imported standalone in this environment (the same reason every other regression suite in this project tests narrower, focused modules instead).
**STATUS: PASS.**

## Session — 2026-09-02 (FINAL PASS — Agent/Task Reporting Content Quality)

### 18. db_update_agent WhatsApp reports only showed aggregate counts (no delta, no task names)
**Root cause**: When 2 tasks failed, the owner received `db_update_agent — 2 משימות נכשלו במחזור` with no task names, no indication of whether the failure was new or recurring, and a raw `docker logs` command as the primary diagnostic. The old sig-based dedup (SHA256 of sorted failing-task-set, 24h TTL) suppressed all repeats but never told the owner what changed.

**Fix**: Replaced the sig-based dedup with per-cycle delta tracking via a Redis key (`autospare:dbagent:prev_failed_tasks`, 4h TTL, JSON dict `{task_name: error_preview}`):
- **NEW failure** — tasks in this cycle not in previous → title says `(חדש)`, severity escalated to `error`, shows `🆕 חדש:` section with task names + error tails
- **ONGOING** — same task set as previous cycle → SUPPRESSED (no message), state updated
- **CHANGED** — any task appeared/disappeared → alert fires with `🔄 מתמשך:` for still-failing + `🆕 חדש:` for new ones
- **RESOLVED** — all tasks succeeded but previous cycle had failures → `✅ db_update_agent — כל המשימות שוחזרו` with names of recovered tasks; prev key deleted

**BEFORE** (from real production screenshot):
```
🟡 [בריאות המערכת] db_update_agent — 2 משימות נכשלו במחזור
(19 משימות הצליחו, 1804s)
לוגים: docker logs autospare_backend | tail -50
```

**AFTER** (first occurrence):
```
🔴 [בריאות המערכת] db_update_agent — 2 משימות נכשלו (חדש)
🆕 חדש:
  • normalize_part_types: asyncpg.TooManyConnectionsError: sorry, too many clients
  • task_categorize_parts: Task timed out after 3600s
(19 הצליחו · 1804s)
```

**AFTER** (same failures next cycle → suppressed, owner gets nothing — correct)

**AFTER** (one task recovers):
```
🟡 db_update_agent — 1 משימות נכשלו (מתמשך)
🔄 מתמשך:
  • task_categorize_parts: Task timed out after 3600s
(20 הצליחו · 1701s)
```

**AFTER** (full recovery):
```
✅ db_update_agent — ✅ כל המשימות שוחזרו
שוקמו: normalize_part_types, task_categorize_parts
(21 הצליחו · 1621s)
```

**Tests**: `devtests/agent_reporting_quality_test.py` — 12 checks, all pass (delta key, TTL, NEW/ONGOING/RESOLVED/IDENTICAL logic, severity escalation, functional simulation). All prior passes (notify_policy_test 8 tests + pass4_content_quality_test 14 tests) confirmed intact via subprocess. **36/36 pass.**
