## Session — 2026-09-01 (Notification dedup — second-pass audit)

### 14. Phase 1 — Critical alert routing broken: notify_owner(severity="critical") did not bypass quiet hours
**Root cause**: `notify_owner()` called `_wa_send_update(text)` without any `critical` flag. The `_is_critical` computation was missing entirely, so every alert — even `severity="critical"` — was subject to quiet-hours queuing and could be silently delayed until 09:00.
**Fix**: Added `_is_critical = severity == "critical"` inside `notify_owner()` then passed it as `_wa_send_update(text, critical=_is_critical)`. Also propagated `critical=True` to supervised-task crash callbacks (`_supervised_task._on_done`) and to health-monitor admin sends (`_admin_critical = (state == "error")`).
**Tests**: `notify_policy_test.py` tests 1, 6, 7, 8 — all pass.

### 15. Phase 1 — NOA engagement loop spammed owner every 15 minutes with pending-reply alerts
**Root cause**: `_noa_engagement_loop` fired `notify_owner("social", "NOA: {N} תגובות חדשות...")` on every 900s loop pass whenever drafts were pending, with no alert_key cooldown. Owner received up to 96 identical WhatsApp messages per day.
**Fix**: Added `alert_key="noa_engagement_pending_reply"` + `cooldown_s=3600` so the same pending-reply alert fires at most once per hour.
**Tests**: `notify_policy_test.py` test 5.

### 16. Phase 2 — Forensic audit: 4 notification call sites missing alert_key deduplication
**Root cause**: Second-pass audit of all 20+ proactive owner-notification call sites revealed 4 bare `notify_owner` calls with no `alert_key`, each capable of generating repeated identical messages:
1. `_group_scan_loop` discoveries (daily scan): re-notified for same undismissed discoveries on restart
2. `_supplier_sourcing_loop` (weekly): re-notified same pending candidates if owner hadn't approved
3. `_noa_marketing_loop` post approval: could double-fire if loop restarted mid-cycle
4. `execute_campaign` (BACKEND_AI_AGENTS): no per-campaign dedup, could re-notify on re-run

**Fix**: Added content-fingerprinted `alert_key` to all four sites:
- Group scan discoveries: `alert_key=f"group_scan_discoveries_{_disc_fp}"` — MD5 of sorted post_id/text set, cooldown_s=86400
- Supplier sourcing: `alert_key=f"supplier_sourcing_{_sup_fp}"` — MD5 of sorted domain set, cooldown_s=7*86400
- NOA post approval: `alert_key=f"noa_post_ready_{social_post_id}"`, cooldown_s=86400
- execute_campaign: `alert_key=f"campaign_ready_{campaign_id}"`, cooldown_s=86400

**Confirmed already handled (no change needed)**: amayama monitor (has `alert_key="amayama_harvester_down"` + 4h cooldown), pending-posts reminder (per-oldest-id key + 24h), health monitor (per-service keys), meili parity (24h), chrome zombie (6h), campaign publish failure (per post+platform), NOA coherence gate, stuck orders (own Redis dedup), status loop (in-memory state dedup).

**Before**: Owner received duplicate WhatsApp messages for the same undismissed pending posts/suppliers/campaigns across restarts or within the same day.
**After**: Every notification call site has either an `alert_key` cooldown, a state-machine dedup, or an in-memory guard. No message fires more than once per its natural period for identical content.

**Tests**: `devtests/agent_reporting_quality_test.py` — 14 tests, all pass. `devtests/notify_policy_test.py` — 8 tests, all pass. 22 total.

## Session — 2026-08-22 (WA notifications + GROQ model fix)

### 11. No recurring reminder for pending social posts
**Root cause**: System notified owner once on post creation — if that WA was missed, the post sat in `pending_approval` forever. Found 41 posts accumulating silently, oldest 33 days old (808 hours).
**Fix**: Added pending-post reminder to the health monitor loop. Every monitor cycle it queries `social_posts WHERE status='pending_approval' AND age > 2h`, sends a daily digest via `notify_owner` (24h cooldown per oldest-post-id), lists all pending IDs with platforms and age. Owner now gets a daily nudge until posts are approved/rejected.

### 12. GROQ model `llama-3.3-70b-versatile` removed from GROQ catalog — 404 on all fallback calls
**Root cause**: GROQ deprecated `llama-3.3-70b-versatile`. Their current catalog no longer includes it. Every time Cerebras (402, billing inactive) + Gemini (429 rate-limited) both failed, the GROQ fallback also failed with 404 model_not_found — meaning post generation had ZERO working LLM.
**Fix**: Updated `GROQ_MODEL` in `.env` to `groq/compound` (tested: 200 OK, good Hebrew output, 131k context). Also updated the default in `hf_client.py`. The `groq/compound` model is fast and proven to work from our server IP.

### 13. False "Cloudflare IP block" diagnosis on GROQ
**Root cause**: My initial test used Python `urllib` whose default User-Agent (`Python-urllib/3.11`) is blocked by GROQ's Cloudflare WAF. This gave 403/1010 and I incorrectly concluded our server IP was blocked. In reality, `httpx` (what `hf_client.py` actually uses) returns 200 OK from the same IP. GROQ is fully accessible from our Contabo server.
**Fix**: Corrected the diagnosis. No IP-level block exists for GROQ. Facebook IS server-IP blocked (separate issue, browser path is the workaround there).

## Session — 2026-08-19 (Security audit: FB group scanner + ingest endpoint)

### 5. CRITICAL: COLLECT_SECRET hardcoded in git-tracked JS file
**Root cause**: `backend/devtests/fb_group_browser_scanner.js` had the live COLLECT_SECRET value `d5b9b695…` directly in source on line 17 (`const SECRET = '...'`). File was staged but not yet committed — caught before it entered git history.
**Fix**: Rewrote the JS entirely. Secret is now read at runtime via `window._NOA_SECRET` (owner sets it before running) or browser `prompt()`. No secret ever hardcoded in source.

### 6. CRITICAL: Fake UUIDs in JS scanner group list
**Root cause**: All 30 group entries in the old JS used synthetic UUIDs (`e7c3b2a1-0001-...`) that don't exist in `group_targets`. Every `_save_draft()` call would fail with an FK violation and silently return False — scanner appeared to work but saved nothing.
**Fix**: Removed the hardcoded group list entirely. Owner supplies `window._NOA_GROUPS = [{id, url, name}]` from real DB IDs (via owner WhatsApp console `fb-groups` command or API endpoint). JS validates each entry against `FB_GROUP_RE` before using.

### 7. HIGH: No rate limiting on /ingest-group-posts
**Root cause**: The new endpoint accepted unlimited calls per hour — anyone with the COLLECT_SECRET could spam LLM drafting calls.
**Fix**: Redis rate limit (20 calls/hour per real IP) using `X-Real-IP`/`CF-Connecting-IP` header, inline in the endpoint handler. Degrades gracefully if Redis is unavailable.

### 8. HIGH: LLM prompt injection via caller-supplied post_text/group_name
**Root cause**: Untrusted strings from Facebook posts (and the group name field) were passed directly into the NOA LLM prompt with no sanitization.
**Fix**: `_sanitize_for_llm()` strips invisible Unicode obfuscation characters (Facebook's virtualization artifacts) and ASCII control chars from all caller-supplied strings before they enter any LLM prompt.

### 9. MEDIUM: group_url not validated to be a Facebook URL
**Root cause**: Any string could be passed as `group_url` and would flow into the discovery dict sent to the LLM.
**Fix**: `_FB_GROUP_URL_RE` validates group_url must match `https://(www.)?facebook.com/groups/<slug>`. Returns HTTP 400 on mismatch. post_url inside each post is also validated to be a facebook.com hostname.

### 10. MEDIUM: Unknown group_id wasted LLM calls before FK rejection
**Root cause**: group_id was only caught at DB insert (FK constraint), after the LLM had already generated drafts for all 15 posts.
**Fix**: Pre-validate group_id against `group_targets` with a SELECT before starting any LLM work. Returns HTTP 404 immediately for unknown IDs.

## Session — 2026-08-18 (Facebook group scan pipeline + LLM fallback fix)

### 1. Cerebras 402 not triggering LLM fallback
**Root cause**: `hf_client.hf_text()` only fell back to Gemini/GROQ on HTTP 429. HTTP 402 ("Payment Required" — account inactive/billing) raised an exception directly, skipping Gemini/GROQ.
**Fix**: Changed condition `if resp.status_code == 429:` → `if resp.status_code in (429, 402):` in both occurrences in `hf_client.py`. Now 402 triggers the same Gemini→GROQ fallback chain as 429.

### 2. group_scanner._wa_notify used wrong import
**Root cause**: `_wa_notify` in `group_scanner.py` imported `_wa_send_quiet` from `BACKEND_AI_AGENTS` (doesn't exist there).
**Fix**: Changed import to `from BACKEND_API_ROUTES import _wa_send_quiet`.

### 3. New backend endpoint: /api/v1/system/ingest-group-posts
**Purpose**: Accepts pre-scraped Facebook group post data from the browser (authenticated), runs NOA's `draft_group_comment()`, saves to `group_comment_drafts`. Bridges the gap where server-side Playwright can't authenticate (missing httpOnly `xs` cookie) by allowing the owner's authenticated browser to send post data to the server.
**Location**: `routes/system.py` (appended to end). Uses text/plain CORS simple-request pattern (same as `/collect`). Auth via COLLECT_SECRET in body.

### 4. group_targets — cleaned test data, 31 real automotive FB groups loaded
**Status**: 31 Facebook groups in `group_targets` (5 approved, 26 pending). 1 draft comment saved to `group_comment_drafts` (status=pending_approval) for group "חלפים לרכב".

**Next**: Owner types `תגובות-גרופ` in WhatsApp console to see drafts and `אשרתגובה <id>` to approve.

## Session — 2026-08-11 (Playwright zombie fix + Agent audit root fixes)

### 1. Playwright `browser.close()` zombie accumulation (22 Chrome zombies/cycle)
**Root cause**: `browser.close()` was called in an outer `finally` block after `async with async_playwright() as p:` had already exited. When the `async with` block exits it shuts down the Playwright NodeJS server; Chrome then receives SIGKILL from the dying server, reparents to uvicorn, and Python's waitpid cannot reap it. 22 zombie Chromium processes accumulated per scraper cycle.

**Fix**: Moved `context.close()` and `browser.close()` INSIDE the `async with async_playwright() as p:` block. Also audited all Playwright usage: switched from `asyncio.Lock()` (serializes all callers) to `asyncio.Semaphore(PLAYWRIGHT_MAX_CONCURRENT)` to allow proper concurrency without OOM risk. Added OS-level Chrome zombie reaper in `_zombie_reaper_loop()` scanning `/proc/*/cmdline` for headless Chrome main processes.

**CLAUDE.md**: Rules added to Background Jobs section and Deployment & Config section.

### 2. `_MODEL_LEXICON` missing BMW/Audi/Mercedes/Peugeot/Citroen models
**Root cause**: `_MODEL_LEXICON` in `BACKEND_AI_AGENTS.py` had no entries for "3 Series", "5 Series", "X3", "X5", Audi A3/A4/A6/Q3/Q5, Mercedes C-Class/E-Class/GLC/GLE, and many other common models. Free-text vehicle extraction (`_extract_vehicle_from_text`) returned empty model → broad make-only search → irrelevant results (BMW radio returned for "brake pads BMW 3 Series 2021").

**Fix**: Expanded `_MODEL_LEXICON` from 27 entries to 80+ covering Toyota, Honda, Nissan, Kia, Hyundai, BMW (3-Series/5-Series/7-Series/X1/X3/X5/M3/M5), Mercedes (C-Class/E-Class/S-Class/GLC/GLE/CLA/GLA), Audi (A1/A3/A4/A6/Q3/Q5/Q7/TT), VW, Skoda, Seat, Mazda, Ford, Renault, Citroen, Peugeot, Subaru, Mitsubishi, Suzuki.

**Verified**: `_extract_vehicle_from_text("I need brake pads for BMW 3 Series 2021")` → `('bmw', '3 series', '2021')` ✅

### 3. `zai-glm-4.7` reasoning model giving wrong responses for customer-facing agents
**Root cause**: When Cerebras primary 429d, `hf_text` fell through to `CEREBRAS_FALLBACK_MODEL = zai-glm-4.7` (a reasoning model) before trying Gemini/GROQ. `zai-glm-4.7` ignores customer-service system prompts and returns wrong-context responses — `orders_agent` got a parts-finder reply ("איזה חלק אתה מחפש") for "אני רוצה לשלם עכשיו". This caused 22.7s waits + wrong answers.

**Fix**: Added `skip_fallback_model: bool = False` parameter to `hf_text()`. `hf_text_fast()` (used by all `_fast_agents`: orders, security, tech, router, etc.) now passes `skip_fallback_model=True` — on 429 it skips the reasoning model and goes directly to Gemini → GROQ, which properly follow customer-service system prompts.

### 4. 50+ second response times for security/marketing agents
**Root cause**: Full fallback chain (Cerebras primary → zai-glm-4.7 → Gemini → GROQ) ran sequentially and took 34-51 seconds for customer-facing fast agents. No hard timeout existed in `BaseAgent.think()`.

**Fix**: Wrapped `hf_text_fast()` call in `asyncio.wait_for(timeout=28.0)` for all `_fast_agents`. On timeout, falls through to `_offline_reply` immediately instead of holding the customer connection open. `asyncio.TimeoutError` now caught separately from general exceptions in `think()`.

### 5. Agent tone redesign — offline replies too dry/robotic
**Root cause**: `_offline_reply()` messages for orders/security/finance/marketing agents were functional but cold ("כדי לעזור במצב הזמנה, שלח מספר הזמנה"). The live PROFESSIONAL_SKILLS & TRAITS block in the system prompt (added G5) wasn't reflected in the fallback messages which display when all AI providers are unavailable.

**Fix**: Redesigned offline replies to match the warm/human tone contract:
- `orders_agent`: "היי! אני ליאור, האחראי על ההזמנות שלנו. שלח לי את מספר ההזמנה ואני בודק מיד 📦"
- `security_agent`: "היי, אני כאן בשבילך! 🔐 ספר לי מה קורה: בעיה עם סיסמה, קוד אימות, או חשבון נעול?"
- `_human_recovery_reply` (generic fallback): warmer + emoji, shorter sentences, less formal

## Session — 2026-08-07 (Social Campaign Architecture — Phases 2–9 complete)

Completed the full 9-phase Facebook Social Media Automation Architecture over the prior session (Phases 1–8) and this session (Phase 9). All phases delivered against the "AI Digital Marketing Department" goal — not a standalone posting bot, but a layered system integrated into the existing multi-agent platform.

**Phase 2 — API test suite (`devtests/campaign_api_test.py`, 33 tests)**

Covers: auth gate (401 on all 8 protected endpoints), campaign CRUD, group target lifecycle, engagement events, analytics, tool registry, response schema validation, error handling (non-existent IDs, invalid status, malformed UUIDs). All 33 pass.

**Phase 3 — Agent hierarchy wired (`BACKEND_AI_AGENTS.py`)**

AVI (RouterAgent) → SHIRA (MarketingAgent) → NOA (SocialMediaManagerAgent) → `social/tools.run_tool()`.

- `SHIRA.delegate_to_social_campaign()` — takes a marketing objective from the Department Manager layer, calls NOA to generate a plan, creates a `campaigns` DB record, and calls `NOA.execute_campaign()`.
- `NOA.execute_campaign()` — loads the campaign plan, generates per-platform content via `generate_post()`, dispatches to `run_tool()` for each platform. Supports `dry_run=True` (generates + previews content without publishing). Campaign status promoted to `active` on first successful publish.
- `/api/v1/campaigns/{id}/execute` endpoint wired to `NOA.execute_campaign()` for REST callers.

**Phase 4 — Tool layer (`social/tools.py`)**

3 bug fixes applied:

| Bug | Fix |
|---|---|
| `CAST(:inp AS jsonb)` / `CAST(:res AS jsonb)` — `::jsonb` casts collided with SQLAlchemy `:name` param parser | Replaced with `CAST(:param AS type)` in `_log_action` |
| `list_tools()` returned blank descriptions — `split("\n")[1]` was the empty line | Fixed to `next(ln.strip() for ln in doc.splitlines() if ln.strip(), "")` |
| `list_tools()` missing metadata fields | Added `requires_approval` and `inputs_preview` to each tool dict |

**Phase 5 — Meta integration façade (`integrations/meta/`)**

6 modules as a structured namespace over the existing `social/meta_client.py` and `social/facebook_pages.py` (no code duplication — thin re-exports + net-new functionality):

| Module | Role |
|---|---|
| `auth_manager.py` | `is_configured()`, `validate_token()`, `exchange_for_long_lived_token()` (full token exchange chain) |
| `graph_client.py` | Re-export of `graph_get`/`graph_post` from `meta_client` |
| `facebook_pages.py` | Re-export of all FB page ops |
| `instagram.py` | NEW: `publish_image_post()` (2-step container→publish), `get_media_insights()`, `get_recent_media()` |
| `webhook_handler.py` | NEW: `verify_signature()` (HMAC-SHA256, fails closed), `parse_event()`, `extract_page_comments()`, `extract_ig_comments()` |
| `rate_limiter.py` | `check_rate_limit()`, `rate_limit_status()` — reads from `meta_client`'s rolling window state |

**Phase 6 — Browser integration façade (`integrations/facebook_browser/`)**

- `__init__.py` — re-exports `GroupAgent` + `FacebookSession` from `social/facebook_browser/`; documents `APPROVAL_REQUIRED = True` safety invariant
- `task_queue.py` — NEW: `BrowserTaskQueue` singleton that serializes all Playwright actions (one session at a time, `_MAX_CONCURRENT = 1`, `_MAX_RETRIES = 2`, 180s timeout per task). Prevents concurrent FB sessions corrupting each other's DOM state.

**Phase 7 — Feedback loop**

`social/feedback_analyzer.py` was implemented in the prior session. Two SQL `::jsonb` casts fixed (same pattern as Phase 4). Verified end-to-end: engagement events insert → `generate_analytics_report()` reads them → `analytics_reports` row created → `get_top_performers()` for NOA's weekly brief.

**Phase 8 — Cerebras gating (`integrations/meta/cerebras_gate.py`)**

`needs_llm(action)` classifies every social action:
- **Required** (LLM must run): `generate_campaign_plan`, `generate_post`, `synthesise_analytics_insights`, `draft_group_comment`, `route_message`
- **Forbidden** (raises `ValueError`): `schedule_post`, `execute_api_call`, `db_read`, `db_write`, `rate_limit_check`, `signature_verify`, `filter_list`, `sort_results`, `update_status`
- Unknown actions → `False` (safe default: no LLM)

`llm_budget_context()` returns current `CLEANUP_LLM_BATCH`/`CLEANUP_LLM_MIN_INTERVAL_S`/`CLEANUP_LLM_DAILY_MAX_CALLS` values — callers log these for budget traceability.

**Phase 9 — End-to-end test (`devtests/e2e_campaign_test.py`, 32 tests)**

Exercises the full campaign flow with campaign "Toyota Brake Parts Israel":

```

  ↓
create_campaign()             →  DB record (id=..., status=draft)

NOA.generate_post() ×2        →  Facebook content (730 chars), Instagram content (598 chars)
  ↓
NOA.execute_campaign(dry_run) →  2 platforms queued, 0 real posts, dry_run previews confirmed
  ↓
engagement_events INSERT      →  reach=4200, engagement=181 linked to campaign
  ↓
generate_analytics_report()  →  analytics_reports row created, LLM insights generated
  ↓
DB audit                      →  campaigns/engagement_events/analytics_reports: 1 row each ✅
```

**All 32 tests pass. Total across both test suites: 65/65.**

## Session — 2026-08-06 (Facebook Social Media Automation Architecture)

Full social media campaign infrastructure added. Architecture audit confirmed 5 critical gaps; all implemented and verified live.

**What was built (all integrated into existing platform — no standalone system):**

| Component | File | What it adds |
|---|---|---|
| Meta auth layer | `social/meta_client.py` | Token validation, per-endpoint rate limiting (rolling window), exponential backoff retry, `platform_accounts` table upsert |
| Facebook Page ops | `social/facebook_pages.py` | `get_post_insights()`, `get_page_insights()`, `get_comments()`, `reply_to_comment()`, `publish_video()` — all routed through `meta_client` |
| FB Group browser agent | `social/facebook_browser/` | Playwright session (cookie persistence, health-check, human-like delays); `GroupAgent` scans approved groups, drafts comments, posts only after explicit owner approval. **APPROVAL_REQUIRED = True hardcoded** |
| Formal tool system | `social/tools.py` | Typed `ToolResult` schema; 9 tools: `facebook_publish_page_post`, `facebook_reply_comment`, `facebook_get_insights`, `instagram_publish_post`, `facebook_group_scan`, `facebook_group_comment`, `facebook_group_publish`, `telegram_publish`, `whatsapp_send_message`; agent_actions logging; `run_tool()` dispatcher |
| Campaign manager | `social/campaign_manager.py` | `campaigns` CRUD, `group_targets` lifecycle (pending→approved), `link_post_to_campaign`, performance counters, group discovery task drafting |
| Feedback analyzer | `social/feedback_analyzer.py` | Polls FB Insights every 6h, writes `engagement_events`, updates campaign totals, generates `analytics_reports` with LLM insights, `get_top_performers()` for NOA's brief |
| Campaign API | `routes/campaigns.py` | 12 endpoints: campaign CRUD, analytics, group target management, group scan trigger, tool dispatcher, platform status |
| DB migration | `alembic/versions/0055_social_campaign_tables.py` | 5 new tables: `campaigns`, `group_targets`, `engagement_events`, `platform_accounts`, `analytics_reports` |
| Background loops | `BACKEND_API_ROUTES.py` | `_social_feedback_loop()` (6h, `SOCIAL_FEEDBACK_ENABLED`), `_group_scan_loop()` (24h, `SOCIAL_GROUP_SCAN_ENABLED`) — both supervised |

**Key architecture decisions:**
- Facebook Groups API is dead (Apr-2024) — browser-only path via Playwright. Browser agent NEVER posts autonomously; every action requires owner approval via the WhatsApp console.
- Does NOT replace existing `social/engagement.py` or `social/registry.py` — builds on top.
- Campaign plans still generated by `SocialMediaManagerAgent.generate_campaign_plan()` — no LLM duplication.
- Rate limiting in `meta_client.py` is in-process (rolling time buckets) — no Redis dependency for social layer.
- `APPROVAL_REQUIRED = True` in `group_agent.py` is a hardcoded safety invariant; cannot be toggled via env.

**Verified live (2026-08-06):**
- All 10 new Python modules import cleanly inside `autospare_backend`
- Migration applied successfully (5 tables confirmed in live DB)
- 12 campaign routes registered in OpenAPI (`/api/v1/campaigns/*`)
- Auth gate working (401 on unauthenticated request)
- Both supervised loops registered at startup

## Session — 2026-08-06 (ASAP Network — 4 approved sellers imported)

ASAP Network is an auto-parts aggregator that supplies ACA-standard CSVs (products + ACES fitment) for approved brands. Four brands were approved by the owner and harvested via the browser → `/api/v1/system/asap-collect` relay → `backend/importers/asap_import.py`.

**Transport pattern (CORS simple-request, learned 2026-07-12 rule confirmed):** The browser JS posts to the relay with `Content-Type: text/plain` and auth in the JSON body (15-min single-use Redis nonce from `maintenance/mint_asap_nonce.py`) — no CORS preflight, so our global `CORSMiddleware` passes it through from any origin. Fire-and-forget async IIFE stores CSV to `window._var`; relay posted in a separate JS call to avoid the CDP 45s timeout.

**Shared ACES fitment file:** Datasheet 1878444 is a single file covering all 4 brands. Importing it once links fitment for whichever brands' products are in the DB at that point — re-importing after new brands are added backfills those automatically.

| **Total** | | **1,906** | **8,320** | |

## Session — 2026-08-06 (Amayama harvester — went AROUND the wall, live and harvesting)

| Item | Status | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| **Diagnosed why `amayama_fs` (FlareSolverr) produces zero output** | ✅ Root cause confirmed | Live-tested the exact FlareSolverr session/cookie technique: homepage clears CF fine (login cookie still valid), but **every deeper page — search/find/product — 302-redirects to a Cloudflare Managed Challenge that never resolves.** Also reproduced with a fresh vanilla Playwright + Chromium instance (own test, not FlareSolverr) — same permanent hang on "Performing security verification," 100% reproducible. Root cause: this is Cloudflare's **CDP `Runtime.enable` fingerprint detection** — it structurally blocks ANY Chrome-DevTools-Protocol-driven browser (FlareSolverr's internal browser AND vanilla Playwright both use CDP), not a captcha a human/service could solve token-by-token. |
| **Went AROUND it: real (non-CDP) browser session, proven live** | ✅ Live and harvesting | A genuine, already-logged-in Chrome tab (not automation-framework-driven) hits **zero interstitial** on the identical URLs that permanently hang FlareSolverr/Playwright — confirmed with same-origin `fetch()` calls returning real offer tables (90915-YZZD4 → 8 offers, 04465-33471 → 4 offers, both real prices+shipping, no captcha, no CF page). This proves Amayama's wall is anti-automation-**framework**, not anti-scraping in general. Built `backend/amayama_browser_harvester.js` (same architecture as the existing `rockauto_browser_harvester.js`): `AMAYAMA.auth(secret)` then `AMAYAMA.autorun(rounds, batch)` — pulls unpriced OEMs from our own feed, fetches each part page same-origin, parses every warehouse offer row, relays to `/collect`. |
| **Parser bug found + fixed during live testing** | ✅ fixed | A search matching multiple cross-reference groups (OEM + aftermarket brands) repeats the header row with DIFFERENT column counts (6/7/8 cells) — a fixed column index silently produced 0 rows on ambiguous OEMs. Fixed to detect data rows by the presence of a `.shipping_price` cell (verified present on every real offer, absent on every header) regardless of column layout. Also fixed a second bug: `doc.baseURI` on a `DOMParser`-parsed document is NOT the fetch's real URL — it silently fell back to the calling tab's stale URL and leaked the PREVIOUS OEM's brand/part_num onto every subsequent result. Fixed by threading the fetch's own `r.url` into the parser explicitly. |
| **End-to-end verified against the LIVE DB** | ✅ verified | Test batch relayed → `import_pid` returned → `amayama_price_import.py` log: `matched=1 price_filled=1 supplier_offers=1`. Queried the DB directly: a real catalog part (front brake pads, OEM 04465-33471) now carries a genuine Amayama `supplier_parts` row (`price_ils=110.43`) alongside its existing IL price (`base_price=757.62`, untouched — price-fill only, never overwrites). |
| **Now running a full production batch** | 🔄 in progress | Kicked off `AMAYAMA.autorun(40, 40)` (up to 1,600 OEMs) live in the owner's authenticated browser tab against the real `/unpriced-oems` feed (Japanese-brand parts, ~938K unpriced at start). Sequential with human-paced delays (900ms+jitter) — matches the 2026-07-12 finding that concurrency gets penalized. Progress tracked via `window.AMAYAMA.stats` in-tab and a DB-count monitor. |
| Monitor supervisor updated to watch the real harvester | ✅ done | `_amayama_harvest_monitor_loop()` in `BACKEND_API_ROUTES.py` now reads the `amayama_browser` heartbeat (recorded on every `/unpriced-oems` hit from the tab) instead of the dead `amayama_fs` one, and the stall alert tells the owner to reopen the tab + re-run `AMAYAMA.autorun(...)` instead of referencing the retired FlareSolverr cookie file. |
| `AMAYAMA_HARVEST_ENABLED` (FlareSolverr path) | Left at `0` — correctly, now for a different reason | Not "stuck," just superseded: the FlareSolverr path is structurally blocked by CDP fingerprinting and always will be while it's CDP-driven; the browser harvesters are the active, working replacement. No captcha-solving service needed — the wall was never actually a solvable captcha. |
| **Moved off the owner's PC entirely — now self-hosted on the server (owner request: "make it live on the server, not on my PC")** | ✅ done, durable across restarts | Same "real, non-CDP browser" principle, just run BY the backend instead of by a human: a genuine headful Chrome under **Xvfb** (`:99`, already available — pulled in by Playwright's own `--with-deps` install), launched as a **plain subprocess with no `--remote-debugging-port` at all** — zero CDP connection ever exists, so there's nothing for Cloudflare's fingerprint check to catch. A small **unpacked MV3 extension** (`/app/state/amayama_ext/` — `manifest.json`+`background.js`+`content.js`) sets the account's login cookie via the `chrome.cookies` API (no SQLite/profile-file hacking) and auto-runs the harvest loop as a content script on amayama.com — same parser as `amayama_browser_harvester.js`, self-starting instead of console-pasted, distinguished by `source=amayama_server_browser`. New supervisor `_amayama_server_browser_loop()` in `BACKEND_API_ROUTES.py` (registered in `startup()`) ensures Xvfb+Chrome are alive and relaunches on crash/restart with backoff — **verified live**: after a full `docker restart`, the supervisor auto-launched Xvfb + Chrome + the extension with zero manual steps and was hitting the feed endpoint (`source=amayama_server_browser`) within 90s, unattended. |
| Cookie-setting approach chosen deliberately | — | Considered writing the login cookie directly into Chrome's on-disk `Cookies` SQLite DB (the standard headless-Chrome-deployment trick, `v10`/`peanuts` AES encryption when no OS keyring is present) — rejected as needlessly fragile/reverse-engineered when the extension `chrome.cookies.set()` API does the identical thing as a first-class, stable browser API with zero encryption-format risk. |
| Root gotcha hit twice during setup | fixed | `docker exec -d`/`nohup … & disown` background processes were getting killed when their exec session ended (container `docker exec` sessions don't fully detach a backgrounded child by default) — fixed with `setsid` to force a genuinely independent session. Also: Chrome refuses to run as root without `--no-sandbox` (expected in any root-run container). |
| Two harvesters can run concurrently without conflict | by design | The owner's manual PC tab (`amayama_browser_harvester.js`, source `amayama_browser`) and the new server instance (`amayama_server_browser`) both write through the same `amayama_price_import.py` price-fill-only path (never overwrites an existing price) — running both is pure extra throughput, not a race. The monitor (`_amayama_harvest_monitor_loop`) now watches all three heartbeat sources (`amayama_server_browser`, `amayama_browser`, retired `amayama_fs`) and only alerts if ALL are stale. |

Documented in `docs/POSTMORTEMS.md` (2026-08-06 entries) for the full incident + resolution record.

## Session — 2026-08-06 (Importer rules compliance sweep)

| # | Item | Status | Detail |
|---|------|--------|--------|
| 1 | **13 importers missing `warranty_source` in `supplier_parts`** | ✅ fixed | `car_parts_ie_import_generic`, `lr_import`, `supplier_pdf_import`, `sng_barratt_jaguar_import`, `kia_import`, `import_delek_brands`, `import_from_excel`, `lexus_pdf_import`, `isuzu_excel_import`, `zeekr_full_import`, `jaguar_batch_import`, `colmobil_import_v2`, `mazda_il_importer` — all now call `warranty_policy.resolve()` and write `warranty_source` alongside `warranty_months`. Never hardcode `12` — provenance is mandatory. |
| 2 | **`warranty_months` hardcoded in specs and INSERT** | ✅ fixed | All files that hardcoded `'warranty_months': 12` or `warranty_months, 12` in SQL now compute via `_warranty_resolve(None)` → `(12, 'platform_default')`. Provenance is now set correctly. |
| 3 | **`delek_multi_importer.py` ON CONFLICT used column form** | ✅ fixed | `ON CONFLICT (supplier_id,supplier_sku)` → `ON CONFLICT ON CONSTRAINT supplier_parts_supplier_id_supplier_sku_key`. Also added `warranty_months/warranty_source` to DO UPDATE SET. |
| 4 | **`mazda_il_importer.py` variable name bug** | ✅ fixed | `_warranty_resolve(part.get("warranty") ...)` used the wrong variable name `part` instead of `p` (the loop variable). |
| 5 | **`isuzu_excel_import.py` missing supplier_parts entirely** | ✅ fixed | Added `ensure_supplier()`, full `supplier_parts` upsert block, and fixed specs to use the enriched `r["specs"]` dict instead of the hardcoded minimal dict. |
| 6 | **`isuzu_excel_import.py` specs not wired to catalog INSERT** | ✅ fixed | The rich specs dict (with `category_hint`, `name_he`, `vehicle_context`, `warranty_months`) was built but the catalog INSERT was writing a different hardcoded minimal dict. Now unified. |
| 7 | **`jaguar_batch_import.py` hardcoded `'כללי'` category** | ✅ fixed | Temp table now includes a `category` column pre-computed via `categorize_on_ingest(name=title)` per row in `prepare_part()`. |
| 8 | **`zeekr_full_import.py` specs missing `category_hint`/`name_he`/`vehicle_models`** | ✅ fixed | Enriched specs dict + `upsert_supplier_part` now accepts and writes `warranty_months`/`warranty_source` parameters. |
| 9 | **`colmobil_import_v2.py` DO UPDATE missing warranty fields** | ✅ fixed | Added `warranty_months=EXCLUDED.warranty_months, warranty_source=EXCLUDED.warranty_source` + `category_hint`/`part_type_text` to spec_base. |
| 10 | **`supplier_pdf_import.py` hardcoded warranty computation** | ✅ fixed | `warranty_months = (pdf_row.warranty_years or 1) * 12` → `_warranty_resolve(_raw_warranty)`. Source is now `'supplier'` when the PDF contains a warranty figure, `'platform_default'` otherwise. `warranty_source` threaded through the tuple and the function signature. |
| 11 | All 13 modified files pass `python3 -m py_compile` and container import checks | ✅ verified | `warranty_policy.resolve(None)` = `(12, 'platform_default')`, `resolve('12 months')` = `(12, 'supplier')`, `resolve('אחריות לשנתיים')` = `(24, 'supplier')`. |

**Rules reinforced:** (1) `warranty_source` is mandatory whenever `warranty_months` is written — `platform_default` vs `supplier` is the only way a surface can reliably say "this is a manufacturer promise, not our default." (2) Always grep the CALL SITE, not the import line — `from warranty_policy import resolve` is not the same as calling it. (3) Per-row savepoints (`async with conn.transaction():`) on supplier_parts upserts prevent cascade aborts. (4) `ON CONFLICT ON CONSTRAINT supplier_parts_supplier_id_supplier_sku_key` is the ONLY correct form — column-pair syntax silently fails when the constraint names don't match.

## Session — 2026-08-05 (Part-number search 300×, and the catch-all's real cause)

| # | Item | Status | Detail |
|---|------|--------|--------|
| 1 | **A part-number search TIMED OUT — the most valuable query we serve** | ✅ verified | `NGK 91106` never returned (180s cap). `EXPLAIN`: `Seq Scan on parts_catalog`, cost 484,438, `Rows Removed by Filter: 4,550,915`. Cause: the predicate wrapped the column in a function — `regexp_replace(UPPER(COALESCE(sku,'')),'[^A-Z0-9]','','g') = $1` — and **a function on a column makes every index on that column unusable**. A customer holding the part in their hand, typing the number printed on it, got nothing. |
| 2 | Adding the right index was **not** enough | ✅ verified | Built `idx_pc_search_norm_sku` / `idx_pc_search_norm_oem` (expression indexes matching the predicate byte-for-byte, 136 MB, 21s each). The planner **still** chose the seq scan: the three branches were `OR`-ed under a `LIMIT`, and it estimated 20,321 matches per branch (actual ~1), so "scan until the limit fills" looked cheap. Forcing the index made it **worse**. Measured: seq scan **12.4s** · forced index **20.0s** · `UNION` **0.00s**. |
| 3 | Root fix — restructure the query, don't fight the estimate | ✅ deployed | `identifier_match_sql` is now `pc.id IN (SELECT … UNION SELECT … UNION SELECT …)` — three separately-indexed lookups instead of one `OR`-ed scan. Each branch can use its own index, and the fix does not depend on the planner's row estimate being good. Results proven **identical** before deploying (`identical=True` on every probe), because a faster query that returns different rows is not a fix. |
| 4 | Live verification (post-restart, cold module) | ✅ verified | `NGK 91106` **180s+ → 0.33s** (44 parts, 3 with images) · `0986424815` 30.7s → **0.24s** · `1K0615301AA` 33.3s → **0.34s** · `brake pad` 0.25s (never used this path, unchanged). **~300–600×**, same rows. |
| 5 | The index build failed twice and left **INVALID** indexes | ✅ fixed | `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` hit `LockNotAvailableError` during autovacuum. It leaves an INVALID index behind, and a retry with `IF NOT EXISTS` then **silently skips forever** — search stays slow while the index appears to exist. Fixed by drop-then-build with a 15-min `lock_timeout`, and verified via `indisvalid`, not by mere presence. |
| 6 | **Owner asked why ~298K parts are still in `כללי`** — bucketed by CAUSE, not guessed | ✅ measured | 298,019 parts, every one replayed through the LIVE matcher and the LIVE blocklist: **7.6% (22,683)** the matcher classifies *now* — a real drift gap · **9.6% (28,469)** bare fasteners, blocklisted on purpose · **4.0% (11,993)** brand/position/generic words only · **1.6% (4,794)** pure codes, no words at all · the rest carry only ambiguous head-nouns (`support`, `holder`, `duct`, `insert`, `retainer`). So **~15% is deliberate** — the standing rule is that a wrong category is worse than the catch-all. |
| 7 | **ROOT CAUSE of the drift: car-parts.ie ingest never looked at the part NAME** | ✅ root-fixed | `car_parts_ie_import_generic.py` imported `categorize_on_ingest` **but never called it** — line 268 categorized from the URL slug alone (`_map_category`). Blocks with no slug, or a slug we don't map, fell straight to the catch-all while the name said `"BOSCH … AUDI Q3 Sportback (F3N) brake pads"` in plain English. Measured: **49.3% of parts harvested in the previous 2 days landed in `כללי`** this way. Fix keeps the slug as the primary signal (it is the site's own taxonomy) and falls back to the name only when the slug yields the catch-all. |
| 8 | Proven on the rows that actually failed | ✅ verified | Replayed OLD path vs NEW path over the 561 real catch-all rows created in the last 2 days: **550 rescued (98.0%)**, 11 genuinely unclassifiable. Examples: `… fuel pump`→fuel-air · `… suspension ball joint`→suspension-steering · `… wiper motor`→wipers-washers · `… timing chain`→belts-chains · `… electric system`→electrical. |
| 9 | Duplicate OEM groups re-accumulated → parity FAIL, now cleared | ✅ verified | The parity gate failed on **106 duplicate groups** that arrived from ongoing harvesting since the last merge. Ran `merge_master_parts.py --all-brands` → `groups=0` in 790s, and confirmed independently in the DB: **duplicate OEM groups remaining: 0**. This is drift, not regression — exactly what the weekly maintenance loop exists to absorb. |

**Rules reinforced:** (1) *A function on a column makes every index on that column unusable* — index the expression, or don't wrap the column. (2) *An expression index only matches if the expression is byte-identical to the predicate*, `COALESCE` included. (3) *`CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` leaves an INVALID index on failure, and `IF NOT EXISTS` then skips it forever* — always check `indisvalid`, never presence. (4) *`OR` across expressions under a `LIMIT` defeats expression indexes* — `UNION` the branches so each gets its own index, and the plan stops depending on a row estimate. (5) *Prove a rewritten query returns IDENTICAL rows before deploying it* — faster and wrong is not faster. (6) *An importer that imports the shared helper is not an importer that CALLS it* — grep the call site, not the import line. (7) *Bucket a backlog by CAUSE before proposing a cure*: most of this catch-all is there by design, and only the measured 7.6% was a real bug.

## Session — 2026-08-04 (Category split, picture recovery, and the autovacuum that never ran)

| # | Item | Status | Detail |
|---|------|--------|--------|
| 1 | **Owner's 2 db-agent errors: `supplier_parts` had NEVER been autovacuumed** | ✅ verified | `last_autovacuum = NULL`, 229,177 dead tuples. `normalize_categories` and `refresh_min_max_prices` both died on `QueryCanceledError` every cycle. Cause: autovacuum's default 20% scale factor means it waits for 800,000 dead rows on a 4.1M table — the table never got there, so autovacuum was effectively disabled. `VACUUM (ANALYZE)` → dead 229,177→1,096 (parts_catalog 512,885→9,651), then **scale factor lowered to 2%** on the three big tables so it cannot recur. Both tasks re-run and verified `status=ok`. |
| 2 | Category split — `electrical-sensors` → `electrical` + new `audio-electronics` | ✅ verified | Owner's rule: sensors merge INTO electrical; electronics gets its OWN family, not the catch-all. 25 canonical categories. 426,059 rows renamed, 19,609 audio/infotainment parts moved into the new family with provenance. Old id kept as an alias so cached documents and external callers keep resolving. |
| 3 | I over-reached on the label and had to be corrected | ✅ fixed | The owner approved a TWO-way merge; I wrote the label as "Electrical & **Electronics**", folding in a third concept. It was in three places — display label, family label, and (worst) the aliases + VARIANT_MAP, where it silently routed incoming "electronics" into electrical. **An alias IS a merge.** Now `electronics`→`audio-electronics`, and the label is plain `חשמל`. |
| 4 | Keyword drift: 1,565 freshly-split parts pulled back into electrical | ✅ root-fixed | `speaker`, `antenna`, `amplifier`, `multimedia`, `head unit`, `display`, `lcd`, `monitor` were still in the ELECTRICAL rule block while also belonging to audio-electronics. **Two families claiming one keyword means the split loses.** Moved them into a dedicated audio-electronics RULES block; verified no shared keywords remain. Caught only because the split stamps provenance — the count drifting down was traceable to the exact cause. |
| 5 | `Relay Housing` → engine | ✅ fixed | `housing` is a hand-written ENGINE keyword and hand-written always beats learned, so the learned `relay housing` could never win. Added `relay housing` / `fuse housing` / `connector housing` as hand-written electrical rules. `Thermostat Housing` still → cooling (no over-reach). |
| 6 | **Pictures: 99% of the "missing images" was one blocked host** | ✅ verified | 97,590 parts were `no_source`; **96,714 of them were `media.autoteile-meile.de`**, which 403s this server's IP regardless of UA/Referer. Compounded by `SELECT DISTINCT ON (pc.id)` — only the PRIMARY image was ever tried, and since the candidate query excludes parts that already have a verdict, a dead primary permanently hid working alternates. Fixed both: try ALL images in priority order, and mint a `cf_clearance` cookie once per host then fetch with plain urllib (never route images through the browser — that is the CPU leak). Proven on 25 real parts: **19 ok / 5 rejected_ad / 1 genuine 404**, previously all 25 `no_source`. |
| 7 | Honest scope on pictures | ℹ️ | This recovers ~96,000 images. It does NOT close the real gap: **3,718,716 parts (91.7%) have no image URL at all**. That needs an image SOURCING pipeline (exact-OEM-only, eBay/AliExpress/manufacturer sites, no Amazon per the owner) — a separate build, best done after the index since it is additive. |
| 8 | Full-catalogue recheck: 3 defects, all mine | ✅ fixed | (a) marked `done` with `batches_run=0` — the no-progress rule was guarded against ERRORED batches but not INTERRUPTED ones (a restart leaves attempts=0 too); (b) **no cursor** — improve-only skips without writing, so `updated_at < run_start` never retired rows and the scan re-read the same head of the table forever, while catch-all rescues made it LOOK healthy; (c) **20:1 no-op writes** — 4,362 rows written for 217 real changes, which across 4M parts would have rebuilt precisely the bloat from item 1. Now: keyset cursor persisted across runs, and 185 writes for 185 changes. |
| 9 | Two processes running the same thumbnail script | ✅ fixed | The supervisor's stand-down matched the step KEY (`part_thumbnails`) but the script had been queued as `thumbnails_retry_blocked`. Now matches on the SCRIPT (`owns_script`). **Key the contract on the contended resource, not on a label.** |
| 10 | A destructive full re-categorisation, prevented by diffing first | ✅ | The owner asked to re-run all 4M parts through the pipeline. The dry run projected **~290,411 parts pushed INTO the catch-all vs ~6,128 rescued** — 47:1 damage. Cause: today's categories came from importers/TecDoc, sources that know more than a keyword match on a name. Built `--improve-only` instead (catch-all → real only, never the reverse): same rescues, 83% fewer writes, and the dangerous lateral transitions (`air-conditioning-heating→body-exterior` 3,501, `body-exterior↔interior-comfort` churn, `audio-electronics→electrical` 855 which would have undone the split) eliminated entirely. |

**Rules reinforced:** (1) *A percentage-based autovacuum threshold does not scale* — on a large table the default 20% is effectively "never"; `last_autovacuum IS NULL` is an alarm. (2) *A write that changes nothing still costs a dead tuple.* (3) *When a batched job's "already done" marker is a side effect of WRITING, any mode that skips writes needs its own cursor.* (4) *An alias is a merge* — routing tables are as load-bearing as labels. (5) *A blanket "no source" verdict deserves suspicion*: check whether it is one blocked HOST before concluding the data is missing. (6) *Diff a bulk re-derivation before running it* — re-deriving from names discards what importers knew.

## Session — 2026-07-29f (Queue ENABLED by the owner — six bugs that only appeared under real load)

The queue passed 50+ tests while disabled. Enabling it surfaced six defects that no
test had reached, five of them in code the tests had "covered". Recorded because the
pattern matters more than the individual bugs: **a control plane is not proven until it
has actually driven the real jobs.**

| # | Item | Status | Detail |
|---|------|--------|--------|
| 1 | **`status()` ran DDL on a READ path → deadlock** | ✅ fixed | `status()` called `ensure_table()`, whose `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` / `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` take an **AccessExclusiveLock** on `pipeline_queue` — while the runner held row locks on the same table. Live: *"waits for RowExclusiveLock … blocked by …; waits for AccessExclusiveLock … blocked by …"*. It both 500'd the endpoint and killed the runner's batch. DDL is now startup/seed-only, and `ensure_table()` short-circuits when the schema is already complete. **Never run DDL from a request path.** |
| 2 | **The merge step was a silent no-op** | ✅ fixed | `merge_master_parts.py --brand` defaults to **`bmw`**, so `--limit 500` merged one brand that was already done — *"0 identity groups"*. The no-progress rule would then have marked the step **done with 272,152 groups remaining**. Now `--brand '%'` (the arg is used as `manufacturer ILIKE $1`). **A step that exits 0 is not a step that did the work.** |
| 3 | **The wildcard exposed a CROSS-BRAND merge — 696,073 parts** | ✅ fixed + quantified | The discovery query grouped by the normalised OEM **alone**. Harmless while `--brand` pinned it to one manufacturer; the instant a wildcard was passed it would merge the same OEM number **across different manufacturers**. Measured before enabling: **255,605 cross-manufacturer groups covering 696,073 parts** would have been fused. `GROUP BY` now includes `manufacturer_id`, matching the owner's rule ("same manufacturer + same OEM"). **An OEM number is only unique WITHIN a manufacturer.** |
| 4 | **A fixed command never reached the running queue** | ✅ fixed | `seed_default_plan` used `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, so correcting `cmd` in `DEFAULT_PLAN` did nothing for an already-seeded queue — the runner kept executing the old, broken command line while the source read correctly. Now `DO UPDATE` re-syncs the **definition** columns (cmd, count_sql, batch_limit, measure_every, order, title) on every seed while never touching the **progress** columns. **Config held in a table must be re-synced from code, or code stops being the source of truth.** |
| 5 | **Status endpoint 500 while the queue was healthy** | ✅ fixed | The step rows carry timestamps and `JSONResponse` cannot serialise a `datetime`. The observe surface failed for a reason entirely unrelated to the thing being observed. Timestamps are stringified. |
| 6 | **Corrected discovery query exceeded the script's own timeout** | ✅ fixed | Adding `manufacturer_id` to the group key made discovery correctly slower, past the script's hardcoded `statement_timeout = '120s'` → `QueryCanceledError` every batch. Discovery is a READ, so it now gets its own ceiling (`MERGE_DISCOVERY_TIMEOUT`, default 600s) and the tight 120s is restored for the write transactions. |
| 7 | **Two processes running the same job** | ✅ fixed | The standalone `_thumbnail_import_loop` was running `build_part_thumbnails.py` at the same time as the queue's own thumbnail step — same script, same candidate query, pure contention. Added `job_queue.owns_step()`; the supervisor stands down while the queue owns the step and resumes afterwards for newly imported parts. |
| 8 | **The thumbnail counter measured work that does not exist** | ✅ fixed | `count_sql` counted every active part without a thumbnail (**4,006,844**) while the script only processes parts that HAVE a `parts_images` source row — it reported `candidates=0`. A progress bar for phantom work. The count now mirrors the script's own candidate query; real remaining is **0**. The actual gap is image **sourcing** (~0.7% of the catalogue has any source image), which is a different job. |
| 9 | Throughput tuned from measurement | ✅ 2026-07-29 | A batch costs ~90s of GROUP-BY discovery over 4.3M rows plus ~23s of merging per 1,000 groups — discovery is paid ONCE per batch regardless of size, so a 1,000-group batch spent ~80% of its time re-finding work. Raised to 5,000 (still far inside the 2400s batch timeout). Live measured after the change: **~24,300 parts merged/hour**. |
| Live state at hand-off | ▶️ running | merge step running, **77,529 parts merged**, 266,739 groups remaining, ETA ~11h. 29/29 supervised loops running. Load ~12-15 on 6 vCPU (harvester idle, so the queue has the box). Observe with WhatsApp **`תור`** or `GET /api/v1/system/job-queue`; **`עצור`** stops at a clean batch boundary. |

**Rules reinforced:** (1) *A control plane is not proven until it has driven the real jobs* — 50+ green tests, then six failures in the first hour of real use. (2) *Never issue DDL from a read path* — `IF NOT EXISTS` still takes an exclusive lock. (3) *A dedup key must carry every column that makes the identifier unique* — an OEM number means nothing without its manufacturer. (4) *Definitions stored in a table must be re-synced from code on every boot*, or a fix in source silently never ships. (5) *Verify a counter against what the worker actually processes* — a 4M "remaining" for work with 0 candidates is worse than no number at all.

## Session — 2026-07-29e (Job queue runner — built, verified, DISABLED pending the owner's go)

| Item | Status | Detail |
|------|--------|--------|
| **NEW `backend/job_queue.py` — sequenced pipeline runner** | ✅ 2026-07-29 verified | The "run the jobs in a queue and we only observe" layer. Runs the heavy catalogue jobs ONE AT A TIME in a deliberate order — merge → categorize → thumbnails → reindex → parity — with measured counters, safe stop/resume and a single status view. Self-creating `pipeline_queue` table (no migration), claim via `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` so two runners can never drive the same step. |
| **DISABLED BY DEFAULT** | ✅ 2026-07-29 | `JOB_QUEUE_ENABLED=0`. The owner's standing gate is that infrastructure is verified BEFORE anything is triggered, so the runner existing must not mean the runner is running. The table and plan are seeded while disabled so the queue and its measured counters can be inspected first. Verified live: `enabled: False, done 0/5`, nothing has run. |
| **Order is deliberate, not alphabetical** | ✅ 2026-07-29 | Merge FIRST so every later step works on deduplicated rows rather than repeatedly on rows about to be merged away; categorize on the clean set; thumbnails last of the write steps (slowest per row); reindex only AFTER all writes or search serves the pre-merge state; parity last because a pipeline is not "done" because its steps exited 0. |
| **Measurement is periodic — I nearly reintroduced "scan-for-nothing"** | ✅ 2026-07-29 | The module's own docstring demanded measured (not self-reported) progress, so the first version counted before AND after every batch. Measured live, those counts take **81s (merge groups)** and **66s (thumbnails)** — ~150s of full-table scanning per batch to learn what a 300-row batch did, which is exactly the anti-pattern CLAUDE.md names. Each step now declares `measure_every` (25 / 1 / 40); a count runs on the first batch and then once per window, and the number is shown with its age (`remaining_at`) so a stale figure is visible AS stale. |
| **BUG the tests caught: a FAILED step was being marked `done`** | ✅ 2026-07-29 | On an error `batches_run` is not incremented, so the next call re-measured, saw remaining unchanged, and applied the "no progress ⇒ finished" rule — marking a **broken** step complete and letting the queue advance to steps that depend on it. Fixed: the no-progress rule only applies when the previous batch actually SUCCEEDED (`attempts == 0`). Found by test 7, not in production. |
| **BUG: `AmbiguousParameterError` — a repeat of a documented class** | ✅ 2026-07-29 | `SET status=:s … CASE WHEN :s='failed'` bound one param in two type contexts (varchar column vs text literal) → asyncpg refused. Same trap already recorded for `parts_images`. Fixed with a separate boolean param. |
| **BUG: I invented a CLI flag (`--scope backlog`)** | ✅ 2026-07-29 | `categorize_parts_batch.py` accepts only `buckets` / `all`; argparse would have aborted that step on its first batch — **after** the merge step had already run for hours. Now `--scope buckets`. New `devtests/job_queue_commands_test.py` validates, for every step, that the script exists, every flag is declared by that script's parser, every `choices=`-constrained value is legal, `{batch}` is substituted, and `--help` is reachable without doing work. |
| **NEW `maintenance/pipeline_parity_check.py`** | ✅ 2026-07-29 verified | The final gate: 10 read-only checks that prove the OUTCOME. Live result today — active parts 4,347,437 · **duplicate OEM groups 272,152 (FAIL, correct: not merged yet)** · merged-losers-still-active 0 · catch-all 370,941 (WARN) · non-canonical categories 0 · orphaned supplier_parts 0 · orphaned fitment 0 · no-thumbnail-verdict 4,006,844 (WARN) · priceless available offers 0 · **Meili lag −87 (in sync)**. Read-only by design — a verifier that writes can mask the drift it exists to detect. Also fixed to answer `--help` without running its ~5-minute scan. |
| Observe + control surfaces | ✅ 2026-07-29 verified | `GET /api/v1/system/job-queue` (JSON + a rendered Hebrew view) and WhatsApp **`תור`** (status), **`עצור`** (stop), **`המשך`** (resume), all added to the help menu. Stop lands on a clean boundary — it means "do not start the next batch", never a kill, so the queue can never be interrupted mid-write. All three verified live end-to-end. |
| Supervised + resumable | ✅ 2026-07-29 verified | `_job_queue_loop` registered via `_supervised_task`; **29/29 supervised loops running, 0 dead** after deploy. One batch per iteration, so a stop request or a container restart costs at most a single batch. |
| Test coverage | ✅ 2026-07-29 | `devtests/job_queue_test.py` — **30 assertions, all pass**: plan order, nothing auto-starts, lowest-order claim, completion by measurement without running, uncounted steps proving themselves, idle detection, the exited-0-wrote-nothing trap, periodic measurement, retry→terminal-fail, stop/resume on a clean boundary, honest status rendering, and every real `count_sql` executing against the LIVE schema. Uses throwaway steps whose "job" is a shell command — real table, real subprocess, real transactions, zero catalogue writes. |

**Rules reinforced:** (1) *A module that states a principle in its docstring can still violate it in its code* — "measure, never self-report" was written at the top while the first implementation spent 150s per batch scanning; state the principle AND measure the cost. (2) *"No progress" only means finished if the last attempt SUCCEEDED* — otherwise a broken step reports done and everything downstream inherits the failure. (3) *Command lines written from memory are wrong* — `--scope backlog` did not exist; validate flags against the target script's parser, in a test, before the plan can run. (4) *An orphan reference is a bug* — the plan named `pipeline_parity_check.py`, so it had to be written in the same change, not later.

## Session — 2026-07-29d (Owner comms: QR page, notification policy, WhatsApp agents)

| Item | Status | Detail |
|------|--------|--------|
| **QR / `/api/v1/go` page did not look like our product** | ✅ 2026-07-29 verified | The channel-picker the QR code on every NOA post leads to was generic slate (`#0f172a`, `system-ui`) belonging to no part of the platform. Rebuilt on the REAL tokens from `docs/UI_UX.md` — hero `#070e1d`, card `#0d1524`, footer `#021737`, primary `#2563eb`, highlight `#3b82f6`, Inter — plus the gear mark and the `AutoSpare`**`Finder`** treatment from the landing hero. Verified live from inside the container: all four tokens present, `#0f172a` absent. |
| **ROOT FIX: the "reversed Hebrew" on that page was NOT reversed data** | ✅ 2026-07-29 verified | The stored Hebrew was in correct LOGICAL order throughout. What rendered as reversed was the browser's bidi algorithm reordering **Latin runs** (`WhatsApp`, `Instagram`, `AutoSpareFinder`, the domain) inside a `dir="rtl"` container, which also throws punctuation to the wrong end of the line. Fixed by wrapping every Latin run in `<bdi dir="ltr">` — **6 isolations** verified on the live page. **Reversing the strings would have created genuine corruption** — the exact trap behind the 3,674-row visual-order incident earlier the same day. Noted in the file so no one "fixes" it by reversing. |
| **ROOT FIX: hourly "still working" WhatsApps buried the alerts that mattered** | ✅ 2026-07-29 verified | `_harvest_supervisor_loop` sent a progress report EVERY hour inside the 09:00–21:00 notify window — ~13/day, ~91/week — regardless of state. The stall signal (`d_models == 0 and d_parts == 0`) was already computed; it was just buried inside an unconditional send, so the one message that mattered looked exactly like the twelve that didn't. Now notifies **by exception only**: `stalled` (work queued, not moving), `idle` (queue drained — what the owner explicitly asked to hear about), and once on `recovery`. A persistent stall re-alerts every 6h (`HARVEST_STALL_REALERT_S`), never hourly. `HARVEST_REPORT_MODE=hourly` restores the old behaviour. |
| Policy extracted so it is testable | ✅ 2026-07-29 | `_harvest_status_decision()` is a pure function the loop calls, so the notification policy can be asserted directly instead of by watching a 30-minute loop for a day. `devtests/harvest_notify_policy_test.py` — **11/11 pass**, including the two volume cases: **0 messages** across 24h of a healthy harvester, and **4** across 24h of an unresolved stall (detect + three 6-hourly chases). |
| A test failure that was the TEST's fault | ✅ 2026-07-29 | The stall-volume case first reported 3 vs an expected 4. The code was right: the harness advanced its clock *after* the decision, adding an hour of lag per cycle that production does not have (the loop computes `_now - _harvest_alert_sent_utc` from an absolute timestamp). Fixed the harness to model elapsed time the way the loop does. **A simulation of a time-based policy must derive elapsed time from a clock, not from a counter bumped after the fact.** |
| **NOA invented a link and a discount, in the owner console** | ✅ 2026-07-29 verified | A live probe of `@נועה` produced `https://autosparefinder.co.il/oil-filters-corolla` — a route that does not exist — and a *"הנחה מיוחדת"* that does not exist. Cause: `agents/owner_console.py` kept a **private, minimal copy** of NOA's policy that forbade invented prices but said nothing about invented links or promotions. Same class as the recurring "one policy, many private copies" entries. Added both prohibitions to `_WA_REPLY_RULES` (so all three owner agents inherit them) **plus** a post-processing guard in `_clean_wa_reply` that collapses any `autosparefinder.co.il/<path>` to the site root unless the prefix is genuinely routed (`pay/`, `admin`, `api/v1/go`, `search`) — prompt rules alone do not hold under a fallback model, and a forwarded 404 link reaches a real customer. |
| **NOA answered a DIRECTIVE by generating a post** | ✅ 2026-07-29 verified | Told *"from now on always put a real price in every post"*, NOA replied asking the OWNER for a price. It answered the topic instead of the intent — precisely what reads as a bot rather than an agent. The `_SAVE_INTENT` detector already existed but only persisted the guideline; it did not change the REPLY. Now a detected directive injects `_DIRECTIVE_NOTE` into the system prompt for **any** agent: acknowledge, state in one line what changes, do not produce an artefact. Live after the fix: *"הקלטתי – מעתה כל פוסט יכלול מחיר אמיתי."* |
| **ROOT FIX: the owner's reply could be silently dropped** | ✅ 2026-07-29 verified | `routes/webhooks.py` spawned the owner-console reply with a bare `asyncio.create_task(...)`. The event loop holds only a **weak** reference to a task, so an unreferenced task may be collected mid-await — the webhook still ACKs, no reply is sent, nothing is logged. Now held in `_OWNER_REPLY_TASKS` until completion. **Honest scope: a synthetic 200-task GC probe did NOT reproduce the loss on this CPython build, so this is a documented hazard removed, not a proven cause of the owner's silent replies.** |
| Two concrete causes of silence, same symptom | ✅ 2026-07-29 verified | (a) **No deadline** — a hung provider call waited forever, which is indistinguishable from being ignored. Now `asyncio.wait_for(..., OWNER_REPLY_TIMEOUT_S=120)` with an honest "too slow, try *סטטוס*" reply. (b) **`if r:`** — an empty reply string sent nothing at all; now an empty/blank answer still gets a message. (c) An exception now WhatsApps the error instead of only printing it. `devtests/owner_reply_delivery_test.py` — 12/12 pass. |
| All three owner agents verified answering | ✅ 2026-07-29 | Live round-trip after deploy: **AVI** — *"שאיבת הקטלוג: הושלמה 100% – 6,695 מודלים, 182 מותגים"*, independently confirmed against `harvest_queue` (6,695/6,695, 182 brands, 0 pending — AVI was accurate). **NOA** — directive acknowledged, no invented link, no invented promo. **@עוזר** — correct command list. Routing verified for all 8 handles (`@noa/@נועה/noa`, `@avi/@אבי`, `@עוזר/@claude`, bare→AVI). 28/28 supervised loops running after both restarts. |

**Rules reinforced:** (1) *Routine success reports are not information — they are camouflage.* A notifier that speaks when nothing is wrong trains the owner to ignore it, which costs exactly the alert it was built to deliver. Notify by exception; make "idle" a reportable state. (2) *"Reversed Hebrew" in a browser is usually a bidi RENDERING problem, not corrupt data* — isolate Latin runs with `<bdi>`; reversing the string creates the corruption you were trying to fix. (3) *A policy duplicated into a second prompt loses the clauses nobody remembered to copy* — and a prompt rule needs a deterministic post-processing guard behind it. (4) *Detecting an intent must change the BEHAVIOUR, not just record it* — `_SAVE_INTENT` was saving the guideline while the reply ignored it. (5) *Never spawn the only reply channel as an unreferenced task, and always give it a deadline* — silence is the one failure mode a user cannot diagnose.

## Session — 2026-07-29c (NOA: CoT leak root-fix + semantic post guard on the LOCAL model)

| Item | Status | Detail |
|------|--------|--------|
| **NOA ↔ marketing department: CONNECTED (verified)** | ✅ 2026-07-29 | `dept-brand` voice rules and the `dept-positioning` differentiator are both folded into NOA's live system prompt. Matches the documented design — prompt-embedded, deliberately NOT a runtime skill-file read. The department wiring was never the problem. |
| **ROOT FIX: chain-of-thought published as a post** | ✅ 2026-07-29 verified | The 2026-07-28 17:00 post was literally the model thinking out loud: `"5 letters? Hebrew letters: ה (1), ר (2), ע (3), ד (4) maybe 4 letters? plus initial ה?"`. Root cause: `_extract_post_from_reasoning` began with **`if len(text) <= 600: return text`** — the stripper only inspected LONG responses, so every SHORT leak passed untouched. The leaked post was **322 chars**. A terse leak is arguably the commoner kind, so the guard was inverted. |
| **The fix needed THREE layers — the first two did not work** | ✅ 2026-07-29 | (1) Removed the length gate; detect reasoning by CONTENT (`_REASONING_TELL`: character counting, `maybe/hmm/let me`, `ה (1)`, `Option N:`). (2) Detection alone still returned the leak, because the old extractor picked "the first paragraph containing Hebrew" and the leaked line ENDS in Hebrew — so it now strips reasoning SENTENCE BY SENTENCE. (3) `Option 1:` is a PREFIX; dropping the sentence would have discarded the real caption, so prefixes are cut rather than triggering a drop. 4/4 pass incl. the real leaked text; a clean post passes through byte-identical. |
| **DIAGNOSIS: the Hebrew problem is GROUNDING, not language skill** | ✅ 2026-07-29 | The owner asked whether NOA should use the local model for better language.测试 showed NOA already runs on an LLM (`PREMIUM_MODEL` = gpt-oss-120b) and that its Hebrew is **grammatically fluent but semantically wrong**: asked for windscreen WIPERS copy it produced *"הגנה מפני קרני השמש, חיסכון בדלק והפחתת טמפרטורת הפנים"* — the benefits of a SUNSHADE. Same root cause as the real post's `שמלאות` (dresses) for `שמשות` (windscreens). **A stronger Hebrew model would only produce more fluent nonsense.** |
| **NEW: `social/post_guard.py` — the LOCAL model JUDGES what the LLM writes** | ✅ 2026-07-29 verified | Owner's insight: use the locally-installed multilingual MiniLM. It cannot generate, but it measures meaning extremely well — exactly the missing check. Generator proposes → local model gates, the same shape as the Phase-1 controlled write path for categorisation. Wired into `_noa_enqueue_social_post`; the score is stored on the post (`guard_sim`). |
| **Threshold MEASURED, and the first measurement was WRONG** | ✅ 2026-07-29 | First pass compared each part to **its own first six words** → MATCHED 0.920, which would have set the threshold near 0.50 and rejected legitimate posts. Redone with copy generated by the live LLM for 12 real parts (one per category): **MATCHED mean 0.653 / min 0.487**, **MISMATCH mean 0.296 / max 0.390** — no overlap. **Threshold 0.40** sits in the gap and accepts 0.0% of MISMATCH while rejecting 0.0% of MATCHED. Independently validated on the real failure: published wrong copy **0.257 → REJECTED**, correct wiper copy **0.417 → allowed**. |
| **Fails OPEN by design** | ✅ 2026-07-29 | Two of the twelve measurement generations hit provider 429s. An embedding error, a missing model, or an unscorable input allows the post and logs it — blocking NOA on a scoring outage would trade a rare wrong post for a silent halt of ALL posting. Every score is logged (pass and fail) so the n=12 threshold can be re-tuned on real traffic. |

**Rules reinforced:** (1) *A guard gated on SIZE will miss the small case it exists to catch* — `len(text) <= 600` skipped a 322-char leak. (2) *Fluent ≠ correct* — grammatical Hebrew describing the wrong product is more dangerous than broken Hebrew, because it reads as authoritative. (3) *Measure a threshold on REAL output* — a near-duplicate proxy inflated separation and would have mis-set it. (4) *A quality gate must fail OPEN* — never let a checker outage stop the pipeline it guards.

## Session — 2026-07-29b (Infrastructure gate: everything verified before any import)

| Item | Status | Detail |
|------|--------|--------|
| **`warranty-capture` 7 → 0** | ✅ 2026-07-29 | All 7 supplier_parts writers now call `warranty_policy.resolve()` and store BOTH `warranty_months` and `warranty_source`. Params appended LAST so no existing `$N` shifts; parity verified per file. |
| **BUG: `resolve("24", None)` returned the platform default** | ✅ 2026-07-29 | `parse_months()` handled ints and unit-bearing text but NOT a bare numeric string — which is exactly what `csv.DictReader` yields. Every supplier stating a plain `warranty_months` column would have had its warranty replaced by ours AND `warranty_source` wrongly recorded as `'platform_default'`, defeating the whole point of provenance. Fixed; 10/10 cases incl. boundaries (`'0'`, `'999'`, `'24.0'`, empty, non-warranty text). **Found only because the verification PRINTED the value instead of assuming the wiring was enough.** |
| **BUG: `bdv_playwright_scraper` wrote 3 NON-EXISTENT columns** | ✅ 2026-07-29 | It inserted `sku`, `currency`, `in_stock` into `supplier_parts`, whose real columns are `supplier_sku` / `is_available`. Every supplier offer it ever attempted would have failed at runtime. Fixed incl. the `ON CONFLICT` clause. |
| **NEW RULE `column-does-not-exist` (schema-validated)** | ✅ 2026-07-29 fixture-tested | Validates every INSERT column against the LIVE schema, because a wrong column name passes `py_compile` AND a static audit and only dies on the first real row. Degrades safely — if the DB is unreachable it reports nothing rather than guessing. **I nearly shipped it broken:** it reported `NONE`, which reads as "clean"; a fixture test showed 0 findings because the fixture had not been copied into the container. Once copied it correctly flagged all 3 columns. |
| **`skip-locked` 10 → 0 — ALL were false positives** | ✅ 2026-07-29 | Every hit was a single-row `UPDATE … WHERE id = $n` inside a per-row loop — the ordinary upsert. The rule was matching a nearby `SELECT … LIMIT 1` lookup. **Adding SKIP LOCKED there would have been actively WRONG** — it changes which rows a statement touches and can silently skip the row you meant to write. Rule now requires the LIMIT to be INSIDE the write (a CTE/subquery feeding it); fixture-tested both directions (1 finding on a real batched write, 0 on the upsert). |
| **All 28 supervised loops verified RUNNING (new endpoint)** | ✅ 2026-07-29 | Added `GET /api/v1/system/tasks`, which reads the actual asyncio task registry and reports per-task `running`/`DEAD`/`cancelled` plus the exception. **Log-counting was actively misleading: 19 of 29 loops showed ZERO log hits and every one of them was healthy** — a quiet long-interval loop and a crashed loop are indistinguishable from outside. 29 registered, 1 intentionally disabled (`embed_warmup`, `ENABLE_LOCAL_EMBED_WARMUP` unset), **28/28 alive, 0 dead**. |
| **Thumbnail pipeline PROVEN end-to-end** | ✅ 2026-07-29 | The pipeline was drained (0 pending) so a passive check proved nothing — deleted a live `part_thumbnails` row and forced a real part back through: candidate detected → source fetched → cured → `object_exists: True` in S3 → served `http=200 image/jpeg 29,630B 500×500` → anonymous bucket GET still **401 (private)**. Cure step tested directly: synthetic ad/label image → `promo=True`, plain part photo → `promo=False`. |

**Rules reinforced:** (1) *Print the value, don't assume the wiring* — the warranty bug was invisible until the test output was read. (2) *A rule that reports NONE may be blind, not clean* — fixture-test every new check in both directions. (3) *Log-hit counting is not liveness* — read the task registry. (4) *A drained pipeline cannot be verified passively* — force a real item through it. (5) *When a rule is wrong more often than right, fix the RULE, not the code it flags* — 5 rules narrowed this way across the audit.

## Session — 2026-07-29 (Pipeline death-spiral fix + MASTER-RECORD merge)

| Item | Status | Detail |
|------|--------|--------|
| **Both permanently-failing `run_all_tasks` tasks root-fixed** | ✅ 2026-07-29 verified | `refresh_min_max_prices` and `dedup_catalog_parts` had failed on EVERY cycle for weeks. Root cause was identical and structural: the checkpoint was saved **only on success**, so once a task was slow enough to hit the statement timeout it never saved at all — the delta window grew every cycle and it could never recover. Measured: refresh at a **5-day / 1,003,775-part** window, dedup at **27 days / 4,500,766 rows**. `_save_task_checkpoint()` now takes an optional timestamp and both tasks persist a **high-water mark after every batch**, so a run that stops on its time budget still makes permanent progress. Verified live: refresh `status=ok`, checkpoint advanced 1h30m in one run, backlog 1,003,775 → 994,775 (shrinking for the first time). |
| **Three measured dead-ends behind the refresh fix** | ✅ 2026-07-29 | (1) `GROUP BY … ORDER BY MAX(updated_at) LIMIT n` must aggregate the WHOLE match set before the LIMIT applies — the limit bought nothing; a windowed index scan is **0.19s vs >120s timeout**. (2) `FOR UPDATE` cannot coexist with `DISTINCT` (verified on PG 16.14 with a control). (3) Batch size is NON-LINEAR: **500→3.8s, 1000→5.5s, 2000→53.3s** — the planner flips above ~1000, so bigger batches are dramatically worse. |
| **OWNER DECISION: a duplicate is manufacturer + OEM, never the NAME** | ✅ 2026-07-29 | The old dedup defined a duplicate as same `lower(name)` + manufacturer. Generic names are reused across thousands of genuinely different parts — `key master pic` 66,144 rows, `key insert` 37,426, `harness` 28,577 — so it returned **265,774 "duplicates" from a 2,000-row batch** and would have flagged most of the catalog. The correct rule (manufacturer + normalised OEM) returns **351 true duplicates in 7.9s**. **The 27-day failure was partly PROTECTING the catalog** — same shape as the normalize_categories pass-2 entry, where the timeout was the only thing preventing damage. |
| **OWNER DIRECTIVE: dedup = BUILD ONE MASTER RECORD, not "find duplicates"** | ✅ 2026-07-29 | New `maintenance/merge_master_parts.py`. Identity = `manufacturer_id` + normalised OEM. Canonical chosen by SCORE (price +8, supplier offers +4, fitment +3, image +2, +1 per populated field, ties → oldest). Then **every field the master lacks is filled from the best loser**, prices take the best value, specifications are unioned, and all offers/fitment/images move across deduped. Loser → soft delete with `dedup_merged_into`. |
| **The old merge was silently DISCARDING data** | ✅ 2026-07-29 | Dry run on 40 Jaguar groups: **79 fields filled, 15 images moved, 5 cheaper supplier costs rescued** — all of which the old script would have retired with the loser. Live on 300 groups: **610 fields filled, 50 images moved, 15 costs improved**. |
| **27 stranded supplier offers repaired** | ✅ 2026-07-29 verified | The old script left offers on retired rows: 27 stranded, **22 at a LOWER cost** than the surviving record (avg 41%). Because search reads only `is_active` parts and derives the customer price from the **cheapest reachable** offer (`routes/parts.py: ORDER BY sp.price_ils ASC`), a stranded cheaper cost silently raises the quoted price. `--repair-stranded`: moved 26, price_improved 1, dropped 1 → **0 remaining**. |
| **Merge verified safe to stop mid-run** | ✅ 2026-07-29 | Killed the Jaguar run at 2,000/137,302 groups deliberately. Post-stop integrity: **orphans supplier_parts=0 fitment=0 images=0**, retired rows holding offers **0**, merged rows wrongly active **0**, losers pointing at a missing/inactive master **0**. Per-group transactions mean an interrupted run leaves no partial state. **NOT run wide yet — queued to run before the category backfill.** |

**Correction I had to make mid-session:** I described the stranded offers as customers being "quoted 3× more". Wrong framing — `supplier_parts.price_ils` is the supplier COST, masked from customers; the customer price is `cost × 1.45 + conditional VAT` via `_customer_price_fields`. The substance survives only because search derives the price from the cheapest reachable offer, so a stranded cheaper COST does raise the quoted price — but the price policy itself was never violated. **Trace cost → price before characterising a pricing impact.**

**Rules reinforced:** (1) *A checkpoint saved only on success is a death spiral* — persist a high-water mark per batch. (2) *When a broken data-writing task is also slow, read what it WOULD have written before making it fast* — second confirmed instance. (3) *A part NAME is not an identity; the OEM number is.* (4) *Merging must ENRICH, never discard* — anything the loser knows that the master does not must move across, especially a cheaper supplier cost.
