## 2026-09-21 — AliExpress OAuth token persistence + token exposure (Phase 2)

**Investigation (verified):** container has no writable `/app/.env` (file absent; `.env` is compose-injected, not mounted), so the callback's and `_auto_refresh_token`'s `.env` writes silently failed. Existing storage found: `suppliers.credentials` JSONB (column commented "encrypted", but nothing in the codebase actually encrypts it), `system_settings` (TikTok token precedent, plaintext JSON), `worker_state:/app/state` volume (file-based, not a credential store). `ENCRYPTION_KEY` is provisioned in compose and `cryptography` is installed, but no code used them. The AliExpress supplier row already exists (`is_active=false`, credentials empty).

**Root cause:** token persistence targeted a file path that does not exist in the runtime, plus the success page rendered the full token response.

**Fix (no new table/file/architecture):** `services/suppliers/aliexpress_supplier.py` — `persist_tokens()` stores `{access_token, refresh_token}` Fernet-encrypted (key derived from existing `ENCRYPTION_KEY`; refuses to store if key missing) plus plaintext `expire_time` under `suppliers.credentials.aliexpress_oauth` of the AliExpress row; `_load_persisted()` loads it (DB wins over the stale env bootstrap); `_ensure_token()` loads before use (the price-sync holds a long-lived singleton); `_auto_refresh_token()` now persists via the same store. `routes/suppliers.py` callback: persists, shows only "stored (encrypted)" + expiry, no token values; storage failure returns an error (never silent); `error` param HTML-escaped.

**Verification:** `devtests/aliexpress_token_persistence_test.py` — mocked exchange/refresh, throwaway supplier row (deleted after), NO real authorization: 14/14 pass (response body has no tokens; JSONB holds no plaintext tokens; fresh instance + separate process load tokens; refresh posts the persisted refresh_token and re-persists; works with no `/app/.env`; no token values in logs). Real AliExpress row confirmed untouched (still empty credentials, `is_active=false`).

**Not verified / caveats:** a real container restart was not performed (would need the pre_restart procedure); durability is evidenced by a separate-process load from the DB. The running uvicorn still holds the OLD callback code until the backend is restarted — **restart is required before the real authorization**. Key rotation of `ENCRYPTION_KEY` would make stored tokens undecryptable (logged as a warning; falls back to env → re-authorize). Real DS retrieval/scan still blocked on the owner's consent click.


## 2026-09-21 — AliExpress callback: registered `/api/aliexpress/callback` now uses the encrypted OAuth implementation

**Finding:** the AliExpress Console callback was changed by the owner to `https://autosparefinder.co.il/api/aliexpress/callback`. That path was served by a legacy GET-only handler in `routes/auth.py` (old `aliexpress.system.oauth.token` flow, raw tokens returned, nothing persisted). AliExpress-originated traffic (Alibaba IPs, Java HttpClient) had been observed POSTing to that path and getting 405 (query scrubbed in logs, contents unknown). No authorization code had reached the system; the AliExpress supplier row was untouched.

**Fix (one implementation, no second flow):** `routes/auth.py` legacy handler replaced by a GET+POST alias that lazily delegates to `routes/suppliers.py::aliexpress_callback_request` — the single encrypted-persistence callback (`auth/token/create` -> Fernet-encrypted `suppliers.credentials.aliexpress_oauth`, masked response). `routes/suppliers.py` now serves GET and POST on `/api/suppliers/aliexpress/callback` (nginx `/aliexpress/callback` too) and reads `code`/`error` from the query, else a form or JSON body (body capped 64 KB; never logged). Same core function for every path/method.

**Verification (in-process, mocked exchange, throwaway supplier row, no restart):** `devtests/aliexpress_token_persistence_test.py` 21/21: GET, POST-form, POST-JSON, POST-query on `/api/aliexpress/callback` and GET/POST on `/api/suppliers/...` all 200 masked HTML with no token values; legacy raw-token JSON gone; missing code 400 for GET and POST; `error` escaped; encrypted persistence, load, refresh via persisted refresh token, no `.env`, no tokens in logs, cross-process load. Real AliExpress row unchanged (inactive, empty credentials).

**NOT live yet:** backend NOT restarted per instruction; the running uvicorn still has the legacy handler (live POST -> 405, GET-no-code -> 422). A restart is required before real authorization; live verification is BLOCKED until then. Real authorization still not performed.

## 2026-09-21 — AliExpress authorize URL was built for the wrong OAuth system (diagnosis only; no code/restart/authorization)

**Evidence:** AliExpress has two OAuth systems. (1) Legacy Dropshipping: `https://oauth.aliexpress.com/authorize` + `https://oauth.aliexpress.com/token` (params `response_type,client_id,redirect_uri,state,view=web,sp=ae`; `force_auth` not documented). (2) Open Platform/IOP: `https://api-sg.aliexpress.com/oauth/authorize` + `/auth/token/create` (params `response_type,client_id,redirect_uri`, optional `force_auth,state,uuid`; `redirect_uri` must equal the console-registered callback) — doc "Seller authorization introduction". App 546482 is recognized ONLY by system (2): `auth/token/create` with a dummy code -> `InvalidCode`, while the legacy `oauth.aliexpress.com/token` -> `param-appkey.not.exists`. `AliExpressSupplier.get_oauth_url()` used `ALIEXPRESS_AUTH_URL = https://oauth.aliexpress.com/authorize` (legacy host) with the IOP-only `force_auth`, un-encoded `redirect_uri`, and a stale default `/aliexpress/callback`, while the exchange (`ALIEXPRESS_TOKEN_URL`) is IOP — a mixed, self-inconsistent pair. The link handed out on 2026-09-21 additionally carried `sp=ae`. The legacy host only 302s to login.aliexpress.com (validation happens post-login), so it never authorizes an IOP-only app key.
**Proposed fix (NOT applied):** point `ALIEXPRESS_AUTH_URL` at `https://api-sg.aliexpress.com/oauth/authorize`, URL-encode `redirect_uri`, default it to `/api/aliexpress/callback`, drop `sp`/`view`. Remaining ambiguity: docs describe "seller" authorization; whether a Dropshipper account authorizes through the same IOP endpoint is not stated explicitly in the docs I could read.

**Addendum (flow classification, same day; still no code/authorization):** repository audit — every AliExpress call except the authorize URL is the new IOP stack: DS methods (`aliexpress.ds.*`) on `https://api-sg.aliexpress.com/sync` with sha256 signing, `POST https://api-sg.aliexpress.com/rest/auth/token/create` (exchange), `/rest/auth/token/refresh` (refresh). Only `ALIEXPRESS_AUTH_URL` is legacy (`oauth.aliexpress.com/authorize`, + IOP-only `force_auth`) => authorize and exchange belong to DIFFERENT flows (mixture). A fully-legacy pair is not possible for app 546482: legacy `oauth.aliexpress.com/token` answers `param-appkey.not.exists`, IOP answers `InvalidCode`. The DS-specific official doc page is a JS shell (unreadable by fetch); the Dropshipper-on-IOP claim is therefore supported by live probes + the IOP authorize doc, not by a DS-specific doc quote. Consistent pair for this app: `https://api-sg.aliexpress.com/oauth/authorize?response_type=code&client_id=546482&redirect_uri=<encoded /api/aliexpress/callback>` + existing IOP `auth/token/create`.


## 2026-09-21 — AliExpress: full migration to ONE coherent IOP OAuth lifecycle (code + tests; NOT yet live)

**Root cause:** a mixed implementation — authorize URL on the legacy host (`oauth.aliexpress.com/authorize` + IOP-only `force_auth`, un-encoded, stale `/aliexpress/callback` default) while exchange, refresh and API were IOP. App 546482 is unknown to the legacy system (`param-appkey.not.exists`) and known to IOP. Inventory: the only active legacy reference was `ALIEXPRESS_AUTH_URL` (services/suppliers/aliexpress_supplier.py); legacy `aliexpress.system.oauth.token` handler already removed from routes/auth.py; no frontend/admin code builds authorize links (only `GET /api/suppliers/aliexpress/oauth-url`); remaining `oauth.aliexpress.com` strings are FIXES_TRACKER history + one explanatory comment.

**Architecture now (single flow, `_IOP_BASE=https://api-sg.aliexpress.com`):** authorize `/oauth/authorize?response_type=code&client_id=<app>&redirect_uri=<urlencoded /api/aliexpress/callback>` (no `sp`/`view`/`force_auth`) -> callback `GET|POST /api/aliexpress/callback` (single implementation in routes/suppliers.py; auth.py is a thin alias, hidden from OpenAPI to remove the duplicate-operation-id warning) -> `POST /rest/auth/token/create` -> Fernet-encrypted `suppliers.credentials.aliexpress_oauth` -> `POST /rest/auth/token/refresh` (same store; shared `_sign_rest`) -> API `/sync`.
**Also fixed:** dead refresh token was retried on every request — now class-level 15-min cooldown per refresh-token digest (a newly authorized token retries immediately; failed refresh never overwrites the store); exchange/refresh failures log/return only AliExpress `code`+`message` (never payloads), callback returns sanitized 502 for network errors, 500 for storage errors; uvicorn access-log filter redacts `code/state/access_token/refresh_token` on the callback paths.
**Verification:** `tests/test_aliexpress_iop_oauth.py` 23/23 (URL, callback GET/POST/missing/escape/success/failure, exchange+signature, encryption, refresh+cooldown, legacy isolation, API-signing compatibility, log/response hygiene, uvicorn redaction) + `devtests/aliexpress_token_persistence_test.py` 21/21 against a throwaway DB row. Real AliExpress supplier row unchanged (inactive, empty credentials); no products, catalog, Redis, scheduler touched.
**NOT live:** backend not restarted — the running process still serves the OLD legacy `oauth-url` (verified). **Residual (needs approval):** nginx access log still records the callback query string (a real `?code=` on a browser GET would appear in the nginx log); fix = a dedicated `location = /api/aliexpress/callback` (and `/aliexpress/callback`) with `access_log off` in deploy/nginx.conf + nginx container RESTART. `DS API access_token param name` for `/sync` is unchanged and remains UNVERIFIED until a real token exists.

**Residual (unchanged, low):** nginx `error_log` can include the request line on upstream errors (cannot be disabled per-location); Cloudflare-side logs are outside our control. The authorization code is short-lived (30 min per AliExpress docs).


## 2026-09-21 — AliExpress REAL authorization completed and verified (one finding: code leaked via Referer into nginx logs)

**Authorization: PASS.** Real callback `GET /api/aliexpress/callback?code=…` -> HTTP 200 at 13:27:25Z (uvicorn log, code redacted), immediately preceded by a real IOP `POST /rest/auth/token/create` -> 200. No legacy endpoint used (module code has no `oauth.aliexpress.com`). Persisted in `suppliers.credentials.aliexpress_oauth`: keys `enc` (Fernet blob, 332 chars), `expire_time` (2026-10-21T13:27:25Z, ~30 days), `obtained_at`. Supplier row: `is_active=false` (unchanged — authorization does not activate it; activation is a separate decision), `updated_at` unchanged (raw JSONB update does not bump it; `obtained_at` is the marker), no api_key, AliExpress supplier_parts = 0.
**Live loading without restart: PASS** — fresh in-container process decrypts via `ENCRYPTION_KEY` and loads both tokens (differ from the stale env tokens, non-empty, distinct), expiry 30.0 days ahead; `_ensure_token()` returns ready with zero network calls (no refresh needed inside the 3-day window). The running uvicorn process itself was not invoked (that would be a product API call); it started after the code edits and calls the same `_load_persisted` on every `_ensure_token`.
**Refresh readiness: PASS (mocked/controlled — NO real refresh, deliberately):** a real refresh rotates the refresh token, and losing the fresh connection to a persistence hiccup was not worth it with 30 days left. `_auto_refresh_token` verified wired to `ALIEXPRESS_REFRESH_URL` + `persist_tokens`, no `.env`; mocked suite 23/23 and DB integration 21/21 re-run green.
**API readiness: PASS** — `_build_request` yields `access_token`, app_key 546482, sha256 signature valid against independent HMAC, gateway `https://api-sg.aliexpress.com/sync`; request NOT sent. UNVERIFIED until the first real call: that `/sync` accepts the `access_token` param name for DS methods.
**Log security: FAIL (concrete finding).** Tokens / app secret / ENCRYPTION_KEY: absent from backend and nginx logs (matched against the decrypted values inside the container; nothing printed). uvicorn redaction working. BUT the real authorization code (35 chars) appears in nginx's access log AND error log at 13:27:25Z: the browser's follow-up `GET /favicon.ico` carried `Referer: https://autosparefinder.co.il/api/aliexpress/callback?code=…`. Root cause: server-wide `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin` (deploy/nginx.conf:117) sends the full same-origin URL as Referer; `access_log off` on the callback location cannot cover other paths' Referer field or the error log. Impact is low: the code was already exchanged and per AliExpress docs is inert by 13:57:25Z at the latest; tokens are unaffected. Earlier `?code=` hits in nginx logs are my own dummy probes (`x`, `dummy_probe`).
**Proposed root fix (NOT applied — needs an nginx restart, not authorized in this task):** in both callback locations (`= /api/aliexpress/callback`, `/aliexpress/`) set `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` (repeat the other server-level security headers there, since a location-level `add_header` replaces inherited ones) and `Cache-Control: no-store`; only matters for a future re-authorization (refresh needs no browser).


## 2026-09-21 — nginx fix for the AliExpress authorization-code Referer leak: APPLIED and verified

**Change (deploy/nginx.conf only):** in BOTH `location = /api/aliexpress/callback` and `location /aliexpress/` (which serves `/aliexpress/callback`) added `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` and `Cache-Control: no-store`, and repeated the server-level security set verbatim (X-Frame-Options DENY, X-Content-Type-Options nosniff, Permissions-Policy, HSTS) because a location-level `add_header` replaces inherited ones. Global `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin` and global error/access logging untouched; no other location changed. `nginx -t` OK in a throwaway container; effective config (`nginx -T`) inspected for both locations; `docker restart autospare_nginx` -> healthy, public site 200. Backend NOT restarted (StartedAt 13:19:35Z), no backend/OAuth code changed.

**Regression (real headless Chromium in the backend container, unique dummy marker, `?error=x&code=<marker>&state=<marker>` so the origin renders its own 400 — no exchange, no AliExpress call, no authorization):** callback response carries `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, plus X-Frame-Options, X-Content-Type-Options, Permissions-Policy, HSTS (both paths). Same-origin follow-ups from that document: `/favicon.ico` logged in nginx access log with `referer="-"` and no `referrer:` field in the error log (previously the full callback URL with the code); a fetch to another API path sent no Referer. Marker count: nginx access+error = 0, backend = 0 (uvicorn shows `code=<redacted>&state=<redacted>`). A non-callback path keeps the global policy. Test-run Chrome zombies were reaped by the backend's reaper (0 remaining).

**Residual (low):** the 13:27:25Z real code remains in the historical nginx log lines written before the fix (already exchanged; inert by 13:57:25Z per AliExpress docs). An nginx *error_log* upstream-failure line on a callback request could still include the request line (cannot be disabled per location). Duplicate X-Frame-Options / X-Content-Type-Options headers on these paths (nginx + backend middleware, identical values) are pre-existing/harmless.

## 2026-09-21 — AliExpress production activation: supplier ACTIVE + `aliexpress_price_sync` ENABLED and verified

**Starting state:** OAuth/API verified (IOP authorize/exchange/refresh, encrypted store, live `/sync` smoke test). Supplier row `AliExpress`: `is_active=false`, 0 supplier_parts. `sync_prices` (scheduled once by `_price_sync_loop`, every `PRICE_SYNC_INTERVAL_H`=24h; last run 2026-09-20 17:38Z) had the AliExpress step hard-disabled (`platform_account_deleted`) in `BACKEND_AI_AGENTS.py`.

**Root causes found in the sync itself (services/aliexpress_price_sync.py) — would have broken the first real match:**
1. `INSERT INTO parts_images … ON CONFLICT (part_id, url)` — `parts_images` has NO such unique index. Reproduced on the real schema: `InvalidColumnReferenceError`, after which every statement in the transaction fails (`InFailedSQLTransactionError`). The bare `except: pass` hid it; the outer handler then rolled back the whole batch and aborted the run.
2. `supplier_parts` INSERT had no conflict handling on `supplier_parts_supplier_id_supplier_sku_key` (same OEM under another part) -> same batch-abort.
3. No warranty written on insert (project rule: every supplier_parts insert calls `warranty_policy.resolve()`).
4. Auth/token failure would have shown as N x "not found" (search layer swallows errors).
**Fix (one structural change):** all per-part writes moved into `_write_supplier_part()` executed inside a per-part SAVEPOINT (`db.begin_nested()`); images via `INSERT … WHERE NOT EXISTS` (counted from rowcount); supplier_parts `ON CONFLICT ON CONSTRAINT supplier_parts_supplier_id_supplier_sku_key DO NOTHING RETURNING id` (a conflicting OEM is skipped + counted as `sku_conflicts`, never overwriting another part's row); `warranty_policy.resolve()` on insert (`COALESCE` on update, never clobbering a supplier-stated value); failures reported in `errors` (no silent pass); credentials pre-check (`_ensure_token`) aborts the run loudly. Pricing rules untouched: sync writes ex-VAT cost only (USD x rate -> `price_ils`), shipping stays NULL (customer path falls back to the profile estimate, never "free"), `base_price` never touched, `min_price_ils` only lowered; AliExpress country CN => 0% VAT via `get_supplier_vat_rate`; customer price still `_customer_price_fields` (cost x 1.45).

**Enabled:** replaced the disabled stub in `SupplierManagerAgent.sync_prices` with the same isolated-session pattern as the eBay step, kill-switch `ALIEXPRESS_PRICE_SYNC_ENABLED` (default on; declared in docker-compose.yml, effective on next compose up), limit `ALIEXPRESS_PRICE_SYNC_LIMIT` (owner value 2000 in .env, unchanged). No new loop/worker (test asserts `BACKEND_API_ROUTES.py` never references the sync).
**Supplier activation:** ORM update of the single `AliExpress` row, `is_active` false -> true (same write as admin PUT). Verified: `aliexpress_oauth` blob byte-identical, other 141 supplier rows unchanged (hash), active suppliers 78 -> 79.

**Tests:** `tests/test_aliexpress_price_sync.py` 6/6 against the REAL Postgres schema with an outer transaction rolled back (nothing persists): correct write (USD x rate, warranty, NULL shipping, base_price unchanged, image, history, no other supplier touched), idempotent re-run, SKU-conflict skip without overwriting the other part, write failure isolated to one part and reported, credentials-not-ready skip, enabled-in-sync_prices / no second scheduler. `tests/test_aliexpress_iop_oauth.py` 23/23, `devtests/aliexpress_token_persistence_test.py` 21/21 — all green after the change.

**First controlled production execution** (real production function, real DB, real `/sync`, limit 25, fresh process): 25 HTTP 200 calls in 48.0 s — `aliexpress.ds.text.search` x21, `aliexpress.ds.product.wholesale.get` x4 (numeric OEMs treated as product ids -> handled code 15 not-found); 0 auth errors, 0 token refreshes, 0 ERROR/WARNING logs, `errors=[]`. Result: 25 checked / 0 matched (strict OEM-in-title guard; consistent with the earlier measured ~0% AliExpress OEM match) => 0 rows written; the checked parts' other-supplier rows and catalog rows are hash-identical before/after; AliExpress supplier_parts / price_history still 0. **Write path on a real match was therefore proven by the rollback integration tests against the real schema, not observed on production data.**

**Production verification (after `pre_restart.sh` + `docker restart autospare_backend`, healthy, StartedAt 13:53:56Z):** supplier active with encrypted credentials intact; running process imports the enabled block + savepoint writer; `_price_sync_loop` alive ("next run in 3.7h" ≈ 17:38Z); logs (backend + nginx, 60 min) contain no access/refresh token, app secret or ENCRYPTION_KEY (matched against decrypted values) and no callback code after the nginx fix (only the documented 13:27:25Z lines from before it). No Redis/scheduler architecture change, nginx untouched, webhooks/orders out of scope and untouched.

## 2026-09-21 — AliExpress zero-match investigation: implementation defects FIXED; scheduled 2000-part run: HOLD (backlog yield ≈ 0)

**Question:** why did the first controlled run match 0/25? **Answer: several independent implementation defects AND an external limit.**

**Evidence (real API, safe metadata only):** `ds.text.search` works and parses correctly (25 queries -> 498 candidates, all with the expected fields). But for the 25 dealer-OEM parts NO candidate carried the OEM anywhere (title or full JSON) even under a plain normalized substring test (weaker than the guard) — so the guard was not the blocker; AliExpress fuzzy-returns unrelated listings (`20pcs/lot MG655814-5…`, `IRLML2803…`) for bare part numbers. Reverse test: of 552 OE numbers cited in real listing titles (10 consumables queries), 42 exist in our catalog (27 of 32 already priced). **Positive control on 30 such OEMs through the PRODUCTION path: bare-OEM query found 1/30.**

**Root causes fixed (services/suppliers/aliexpress_supplier.py, services/aliexpress_price_sync.py):**
1. **Query construction (B):** bare part number => fuzzy results. Query is now `"<brand> <part name> <OEM>"` (name reduced to plain words, only the NAME is truncated). Measured on the 30-OEM set with the strict guard unchanged: bare 1/30, normalized 0/30, brand+OEM 12/30, oem+name 12/30, brand+name+OEM 17/30; final code with all fixes 21/30 (70%). Brand/name are retrieval context ONLY — acceptance is still exclusively the OEM-token guard (test proves a brand-only title is rejected).
2. **Numeric OEMs routed as product ids (G):** `search_by_oem` sent any 10-18-digit numeric (BMW 11 digits, Toyota/Kia 10 — 4 of the 25 sampled) to `wholesale.get` as an AliExpress product id and never keyword-searched (the 4 `error_response code 15` calls in the first run). `search_by_oem` now ALWAYS keyword-searches; product-id fetch remains explicit via `search()`/`get_part_details()`.
3. **Separator asymmetry (D):** catalog `15400PLMA01` did not match title `15400-PLM-A01`. Guard is now hyphen-insensitive but token-bounded, and also closes an existing hole: `AB-12345678` / `12345678-99` no longer match OEM `12345678`.
4. **Junk catalog OEMs:** model-year ranges (`2007-2008`, `2009-2010`) normalise to 8 chars and matched any listing's year range (a Chrysler "part" matched a motorcycle brake pad). Rejected.
5. **Unit-price integrity:** the OEM guard proves a listing cites the OEM, not that its price is per piece — real hits included `Three (3) Oil Filter…`, `5X…`, `6Sets…`, `20pcs/lot`, `1/2/3/5Pcs`. Rejection-only pack detector added (spec strings like `M12 x 1.5`, `4x4`, `225/50R16` not flagged).
6. **min_price_ils:** helper treated an existing 0.00 as a real minimum (99% of the unpriced backlog holds 0.00: 198,494 of ~200k sampled) so the minimum was never set; now 0/NULL are replaced (existing lower price kept). In production the existing normalize/heal agents had already set it within seconds, masking the defect. NOTE (not touched, other integration): `ebay_price_sync` uses the same `min_price_ils > candidate` comparison and has the same latent behavior.
Data-quality note: 3 of 25 sampled Volvo/Jaguar OEMs carry descriptive prefixes (`BRACKET-C2D39192`); not normalised (would be an invented rule).

**Regression:** `tests/test_aliexpress_matching.py` 52 (normalization, valid/invalid/ambiguous guard, real response structure parsing, candidate accept/reject incl. price 0 / sibling OEM / brand-only, numeric routing, query construction, pack detection), `tests/test_aliexpress_iop_oauth.py` 23, `tests/test_aliexpress_price_sync.py` 11 (REAL Postgres schema, rolled back: write path, idempotence, SKU conflict, write-failure isolation, min-price semantics, credentials guard, brand/name context, no second scheduler), `devtests/aliexpress_token_persistence_test.py` 21 — all pass (107).

**Controlled real verification (production sync function, its own id cursor, limit 1 per part, unpriced parts known to be cited on AliExpress):** 3 parts checked, 3 valid matches, 3 supplier_parts rows written, 0 conflicts/failures, 6 API calls (`text.search` x3, `product.get` x3, all HTTP 200), 0 auth errors, 0 refreshes, 0 ERROR/WARNING logs, 10 s. Verified per record: `price_ils == round(price_usd x 3.03, 2)` (ex-VAT cost; e.g. Kia 3287625000 $2.19 -> ₪6.64, Toyota 3533012050 $9.18 -> ₪27.82, BMW 11428507627 $62.27 -> ₪188.68), `supplier_sku`=OEM, listing URL, warranty 12/platform_default, shipping NULL, one `price_history` row (source aliexpress_sync), one image, other suppliers' rows for the part unchanged, `base_price` left to the pipeline — which then set `importer_price_ils`=cost and `base_price`=cost x 1.45 (273.59 / 9.63 / 40.34) within seconds, equal to `_customer_price_fields` (VAT 0% foreign, shipping falls back to the profile estimate). Exactly 3 AliExpress supplier_parts / 3 history rows exist (idempotent re-run: no duplicates). These are REAL production rows (removable via supplier_id=AliExpress, price_history.source='aliexpress_sync').

**Yield on the target population (fixed code):** first-run 25 dealer parts 0/25; random unpriced backlog 0/60; unpriced consumables (filters/brakes, mainstream makes) 0/45 => **0/130** (95% upper bound ≈ 2.3%). The unpriced backlog is dealer-only parts (body-exterior 596k, interior 277k, electrical 219k …) that AliExpress does not carry; the genuine overlap is with already-PRICED consumables, which this sync never targets.

**DECISION — 2000-part scheduled run: HOLD.** The implementation is now correct and capable (70% recall where AliExpress carries the part; real write path proven), but the population the sync targets yields ~0%; ~2000 calls/day (≈50+ min) is not justified. **Restart NOT performed (instruction).** Consequences: the running scheduler still holds the PRE-fix `aliexpress_supplier` class in memory; at the ~17:38Z `sync_prices` run the (lazily imported) new sync file would call `search_by_oem(..., brand=, name=)` on the old class -> per-part TypeError (no API calls, quota-safe but 2000 error entries) — or, if the sync module was already loaded, the old bare-query path (2000 real calls, ~0 yield). Either is undesirable => a restart is required before ~17:38Z, with `ALIEXPRESS_PRICE_SYNC_ENABLED=0` (kill switch, needs `.env` + `docker compose up -d backend`) to hold, or with the sync retargeted (owner decision: e.g. priced consumables to add compare offers).


## 2026-09-21 — CRITICAL PRICING REQUIREMENT: raw AliExpress cost must never reach a customer price (HARD STOP hit; root-fixed at the pricing boundary; NOT YET LIVE)

**Trigger:** owner rule — AliExpress supplier cost -> existing pricing policy -> customer price; never the raw cost, never bypass margin. Tracing every price-producing path (not just AliExpress code) found the rule was **already being violated, platform-wide, by four pre-existing paths** — AliExpress merely made them visible:
| # | Path | What customers got | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `add_cart_item` / `_cart_to_response` (routes/cart.py) | cart stored `unit_price = sp.price_ils` = **RAW supplier cost** and displayed it (checkout then charged cost×1.45 — cart ≠ charge) | code; real AliExpress rows: raw ₪188.68 vs charged ₪273.59 |
| 2 | wishlist (`_wishlist_item_to_response`) | `parts_catalog.min_price_ils` — internal cost aggregate (cleanup agent writes cost; update agent cost×1.18) | live values: BMW part showed ₪222.64 = 188.68×1.18 (no margin, wrong VAT) vs policy ₪273.59 |
| 3 | `GET /api/v1/parts/search` -> `external_suppliers` | raw `PartResult.__dict__` for every external supplier (cost, seller, `item_url`, supplier CDN image) from a 1h Redis cache | code |
| 4 | `GET /api/suppliers/search|search/oem|compare` | raw `vars(PartResult)` — **unauthenticated** | live: anonymous `GET /api/suppliers/search` -> HTTP 200 (still 200 at 15:36Z: old code running) |
Paths verified already policy-correct (no change): search cards (`customer_*` fields), chat brain (`calculate_customer_price_from_ils`), checkout (`orders.py`, `payments.py`), NOA, partner API, watch (`_current_part_price`). The frontend's `pricing.total_price` (Parts.jsx, raw `price_ils`/`min_price_ils` fallback) is assigned but never rendered — left alone.

**Root cause:** no single pricing boundary — customer-visible fields were fed straight from internal cost fields by ad-hoc code. **Fix (one boundary, every surface routed through it):** `routes/parts.py::_customer_unit_price` (canonical: `_customer_price_fields`, cost×1.45 + conditional VAT, ex-delivery) and `_sanitize_external_offer` (descriptive fields + `customer_*` price + masked supplier; drops price/total_cost/seller/item_url/currency/**image_url** — the latter names the supplier CDN; unknown currency dropped, never guessed). Cart write stores the customer price; cart READ recomputes from the live offer (also corrects legacy rows that stored raw cost, no DB migration); wishlist uses `_current_part_price` (never `min_price_ils`); `external_suppliers` is sanitized at serve time (covers already-cached raw entries); the three raw routes require admin. Docs: CLAUDE.md §2 Pricing bullet + `docs/POSTMORTEMS.md` row.

**Real-data proof (production parts / offers / rate 3.03 / real AliExpress API prices; committed data only read, scenario writes in a ROLLED-BACK transaction; 52/52 checks pass; nothing leaked — AliExpress still 3 rows):**
- **A — AliExpress only supplier (3 real rows):** cost ₪6.64 / 27.82 / 188.68 (shipping NULL->0, VAT 0% CN, margin x1.45) -> customer ₪9.63 / 40.34 / 273.59 on ALL surfaces (watch basis, wishlist, cart write, cart read, legacy cart row that stored the raw cost, checkout formula); profit 45%; every value ≠ raw cost. (Before: wishlist showed 7.84 / 32.83 / 222.64; cart stored 6.64 / 27.82 / 188.68.)
- **B — AliExpress cheaper (natural, real API $7.01 -> ₪21.24 vs MCT ₪101.01):** customer ₪172.82 -> ₪30.80 = AliExpress cost×1.45 (profit ₪9.56, 45%), ≠ raw ₪21.24; catalog `base_price` 124.00 untouched; other supplier's row and customer price (172.82) untouched.
- **C — AliExpress dearer (natural, real API ₪29.57 vs US supplier ₪21.40):** customer price unchanged 31.03 -> 31.03 (policy price of the cheaper supplier), ≠ raw AliExpress cost.
- **D — existing customer price:** `base_price` unchanged in B and C (124.00, 38.79); other suppliers' offers/prices untouched; `min_price_ils` (internal) moved 119.19 -> 21.24 in B and is on no customer surface.
- **E — min_price_ils 0 and NULL:** after the sync sets it to the raw cost (21.24) wishlist/cart still show ₪30.80, not min_price and not raw cost.
- **external_suppliers boundary (real AliExpress result):** sanitized payload has no price/total_cost/item_url/seller/currency/image; supplier masked; customer_price 30.80 = cost×1.45, ≠ raw 21.24. (First run FAILED on `image_url` leaking `aliexpress-media.com` — fixed, re-run 52/52.)
**Regression:** `tests/test_customer_price_boundary.py` 20 (helper math, sanitizer incl. real CDN host, source guards, anonymous 401/403 on the 3 routes, real-schema rolled-back cart/wishlist/watch for min_price NULL/0/cost×1.18/raw, dearer-offer-no-change) + matching 52 + OAuth 23 + price-sync 11 + integration 21 — all pass.

**NOT LIVE — restart required.** The running backend (started 13:53:56Z) still has the old code: anonymous `/api/suppliers/search` answers 200 right now and the old cart/wishlist code is serving. Restart not performed (not authorized in this task). It is also the same restart the AliExpress matching fixes and the 17:38Z scheduled-sync HOLD need (see earlier entry).
**Residual / owner decisions:** (1) **Business:** Scenario B shows AliExpress *aftermarket* listings that merely cite an OE number (e.g. a ₪21 generic filter vs the genuine Honda ₪101) become the "cheapest offer" and replace the displayed price (172.82 -> 30.80) — margin policy holds, but OEM vs aftermarket presentation is a product decision (`part_origin` is computed but not stored/shown). (2) Pre-existing, not changed: the API JSON still carries internal fields (`price_ils` per offer, `min_price_ils` in part JSON) that the frontend consumes; `orders.create_order` applies ×1.45 to (cost+shipping) whereas `_customer_price_fields` passes shipping through (differs only for suppliers with stored shipping — AliExpress has NULL); public `/api/suppliers/health` lists active supplier names; `ebay_price_sync`'s `min_price_ils` comparison ignores the 0.00 case. (3) `add_cart_item` still picks the cheapest available offer without an `is_active`/price>0 filter (unchanged selection).

**Hold:** running container env `ALIEXPRESS_PRICE_SYNC_ENABLED=0` (kill-switch logic checked against the real env: AliExpress step RUNS = False). `_price_sync_loop` alive ("next run in 1.7h" ≈ 17:38Z): that run will do eBay etc. and SKIP AliExpress. `ALIEXPRESS_PRICE_SYNC_LIMIT=2000` untouched, unused. **2000-call run: HELD** until dual-population eligibility (unpriced + priced-with-additional-offer) lands and passes a small production test.

**Live proof the NEW code serves (real production HTTPS):** anonymous `/api/suppliers/search`, `/search/oem`, `/compare` -> **401 in ~0.1 s** (previously 200 + slow fan-out to ~17 suppliers).
**Live pricing-boundary verification (16/16 + 4/4 + 52/52; raw-cost leakage = 0):**
- **Cart (live, test account, temp session — no login/2FA, all rows removed, account restored exactly):** POST Kia 3287625000 -> ₪9.63 = ₪6.64×1.45, ≠ raw; row stores 9.63 (not 6.64); a LEGACY row stored with the raw ₪27.82 displays ₪40.34 (= policy); GET cart consistent.

- **Scenario A (AliExpress only, real rows) and B (natural: AliExpress ₪21.24 vs MCT ₪101.01; other row + `base_price` untouched; price 172.82 -> 30.80 = AliExpress×1.45, profit 45%) + C + D + E: re-run against the live code, 52/52** (writes rolled back). Live-endpoint B was NOT done: it would need a committed AliExpress offer on a priced part — customer-visible and effectively an OEM-vs-aftermarket product decision that was explicitly reserved; B is therefore proven on real records via rolled-back transactions on the now-live code.

Search already used bucket thumbnails only (owner rule 2026-07-18). Fix: one helper `routes/parts.py::_customer_thumbnail_map` (part_thumbnails status 'ok') now feeds cart, wishlist and part detail. Live after fix: cart imageUrl None (Kia thumbnail was rejected_ad), wishlist/detail serve `autosparefinder.co.il/api/v1/thumbnails/...`, no supplier host anywhere. Tests: `tests/test_customer_price_boundary.py` now 24 (image tests for thumbnail ok / rejected_ad / none on cart+wishlist+detail; static guard that cart/detail never read `parts_images`).

(5) Product decision (reserved): OEM vs aftermarket presentation of AliExpress offers. (6) Pre-existing: `add_cart_item` selection has no `is_active`/price>0 filter.


## 2026-09-21 — AliExpress dual-population sync + FINAL product rule (OEM/Aftermarket/Equivalent coexist) — production-verified; 2000-run HELD

**Resolved product rule (owner, recorded as final):** OEM + Aftermarket + Equivalent offers may coexist and are presented according to product classification, supplier and price. AliExpress is simply another supplier offer: it never replaces, hides or overwrites another supplier's offer merely because it is cheaper; every customer price passes through the existing pricing policy (cost×1.45 + conditional VAT via `_customer_price_fields`/`_customer_unit_price`; no AliExpress-specific formula).

**Root cause found for the "replace/hide" risk:** offers were classification-BLIND. The schema already has per-offer `supplier_parts.part_type` (ORM comment "OEM, Original, Aftermarket"; used by the compare endpoint) but 3.86 M of 4.2 M offers are NULL and nine "cheapest offer" selections ignored it (cart add, WhatsApp checkout `create_whatsapp_checkout`, `_current_part_price` watch/wishlist basis, partner API, NOA marketing fact, search offer ordering/second block, compare) — so a cheaper aftermarket AliExpress listing would silently become the default cart line / advertised price of an OEM part. **Fix (one rule, applied at the boundary):** new `backend/offer_classification.py` — `normalize_type / is_compatible / class_rank / offer_label` + an SQL twin `class_rank_sql` (verified equal to the Python rule for all 144 class combinations on the real DB) + `classify_marketplace_listing` (never `oem`: `aftermarket`, or `oe_equivalent` when the title names a known OE-supplier brand, word-boundary matched). Wired into: cart add (default = cheapest class-COMPATIBLE offer; optional `supplier_part_id` on `CartAddRequest` lets the customer pick any offer; cart line carries `offerPartType`), WhatsApp checkout, `_current_part_price`, partner API, NOA, search offers (compatible-first ordering, both offer queries) and a per-offer `part_type` label in the search payload. Unclassified (NULL) legacy offers inherit the part's class => zero behaviour change for existing data.

**Dual-population eligibility (`aliexpress_price_sync._select_targets`, each population walked by its own rotating id cursor):** (1) UNPRICED parts (as before); (2) PRICED parts that already have >=1 other **customer-visible** offer (active supplier, available, priced, non-empty `supplier_url`, not 'Official Manufacturer Sites'/'Sandbox') and no AliExpress check within `ALIEXPRESS_RECHECK_DAYS` (7) — restricted to `ALIEXPRESS_PRICED_CATEGORIES` (default filters,brakes). Quota `ALIEXPRESS_PRICED_SHARE` (default 0.5). For priced parts the writer touches ONLY the AliExpress offer (+price_history; `touch_catalog=False`: parts_catalog untouched; images only when the part has none, never primary over an existing image). Offers written with `part_type` aftermarket/oe_equivalent. Matching untouched (brand+name+OEM query, token-bounded OEM, separators, junk/year-range, multi-pack, no fallback).
**A real defect found by the live production sample and root-fixed:** the first priced sample (Lexus 04152YZZA1/A6, 161A029015) wrote AliExpress offers next to IL-importer OEM offers that have NO `supplier_url`; the customer search lists only offers with a URL, so AliExpress became the ONLY listed offer on the OEM part (live search showed 1 offer) — a hide. ~3.8 % of priced parts have this shape. Fix at eligibility (visible-offer predicate above, test-guarded) and the 3 offending rows were REVERTED (offers, price_history, AliExpress-added images; other suppliers' rows byte-identical: hashes 3d8f19b52804 / e3d0401d27d4 / 561144d63872 matched before/after; catalog untouched). Side rule: a priced part with NO supplier rows is never eligible either (the synthetic base_price offer shown when no offers exist would disappear).

**Tests (all pass, 146 + 21):** `tests/test_offer_classification.py` 36 (rule + SQL twin; real-schema rolled-back: offer coexists and touches nothing else — other rows + catalog snapshot identical; no duplicate on re-run; defaults never substitute the cheaper aftermarket for the OEM offer; explicit choice; only-aftermarket default labeled; images rule; dual-population selection incl. fresh-check exclusion, share 0/1, category filter; visible-offer requirement), boundary 24, matching 52, OAuth 23, price-sync 11; integration script 21/21.

**Production sample (real API, real DB, real sync function; discovery of OE numbers cited in 471 real listings -> 89 catalog parts -> forward matcher 42/85 valid):** corrected run 44/44 checks, 5 real offers, 0 conflicts: PRICED OEM parts with visible existing offers — Mazda D09W61J6X (2 other rows), Mazda L32114302 (2), Kia 97133D1000 (4); UNPRICED — Kia 2441037100, Mazda L3K910450. Per match: supplier AliExpress, exactly one AliExpress offer, offer class `aftermarket` with catalog `part_type` unchanged (`oem`), cost = price_usd×3.03, customer price = cost×1.45 (VAT 0% CN) ≠ raw cost (e.g. ₪29.85 -> ₪43.28), warranty 12/platform_default, shipping NULL, price_history row (source aliexpress_sync), image added only where the part had none, other suppliers' rows hash-identical, catalog row untouched (priced parts). Earlier unpriced results kept (Lexus 3132114020, Mazda SH01133A0A, Toyota 3132114010 + the original 3). Final state: 11 AliExpress offers, all `aftermarket`, 0 duplicates, 11 history rows, 0 test leftovers, 143 suppliers / 79 active unchanged.
**Aftermarket-class catalog parts:** NOT available — 40 aftermarket-type priced parts with visible offers (filters/brakes) checked: 0 AliExpress matches; the coexistence rule for that class is covered by the pure/DB tests only.
**Critical comparison (AliExpress cheaper than an existing supplier), real data, executed on 6 parts:** e.g. Kia 97133D1000: existing Kia Official (IL) ₪72.88 vs AliExpress ₪29.85 -> both offers remain, existing row unchanged, AliExpress labeled aftermarket. LIVE (anonymous search + real cart, test account): search lists the pre-existing offer (class oem, customer ₪124.70 unchanged) FIRST and the AliExpress aftermarket offer (customer ₪43.28, not the raw ₪29.85) after it; default cart line = the OEM-class offer (₪124.70); explicit `supplier_part_id` = AliExpress line labeled aftermarket at ₪43.28; both coexist as separate cart lines; unpriced part shows only its AliExpress offer labeled aftermarket (₪18.41 = ₪12.70×1.45); 12/12 live checks, test account restored. Real-world note: AliExpress's delivered total (₪192.28) exceeds the OEM's (₪153.70) because of the ₪149 CN shipping estimate — the customer compares landed cost.

**Restarts (hold kept):** 16:28Z (classification-aware customer code live BEFORE any priced AliExpress offer was written) and 16:50Z (latest sync eligibility live). Hold `ALIEXPRESS_PRICE_SYNC_ENABLED=0`; `_price_sync_loop` next ≈17:38Z skips AliExpress.

**2000-RUN READINESS: TECHNICALLY READY, KEPT HELD — yield decision needed.** Implementation, isolation, classification and pricing are verified. But forward-only cursor targeting yields ≈0: measured with the validated matcher — unpriced backlog 0/130; priced parts with visible offers 2/144 (1.4 %) across 12 categories (interior-comfort found 2). Discovery-driven candidates (OE numbers actually cited in listings) yield ≈50 % (42/85). Estimated cost per offer: random cursor ≈ 70 calls/offer (2000 calls ≈ 28 offers, all quota for tiny gain); discovery-seeded ≈ 4 calls/offer (150 calls -> 42 offers). Recommendation (needs owner go-ahead — it changes targeting design): discovery-seeded eligibility (category/vehicle keyword queries -> cited OE numbers -> catalog candidates -> forward match) instead of, or ahead of, blind cursor scans; then enable a bounded run. NOT enabled.
**Follow-ups (not fixed):** the existing healer (`db_cleanup_agent`) is classification-blind: for an UNPRICED OEM part whose only priced offer is aftermarket it sets `base_price = cost×1.45` from that aftermarket cost (nothing was replaced — the part had no price — but `base_price` then reflects an aftermarket offer); chat brain (`BACKEND_AI_AGENTS`) lists ≤3 suppliers by priority without a class label; frontend does not yet render `part_type`/`offerPartType` or send `supplier_part_id`; plus the earlier list (create_order ×1.45 on shipping, public /api/suppliers/health, eBay min_price 0.00, internal JSON fields).


## 2026-09-21 — AliExpress: discovery-seeded targeting, base-price classification safety, customer classification exposure — production-verified; 2000-run STILL HELD

**Target-discovery root cause:** nothing recorded which catalog parts AliExpress actually carries, so the cursor probed the catalog blind (measured 0/130 unpriced, 2/144 priced). **Fix:** `aliexpress_price_sync` now harvests the OE numbers sellers *cite in real listing titles* (`discover_candidates`, same token-bounded guard as matching, rotating make×part-term queries with a persisted cursor), stores catalog hits in the new `aliexpress_candidates` table, and forward-matches ONLY those (`_select_candidate_targets`, both populations: unpriced + priced-with-visible-offer, shares `ALIEXPRESS_PRICED_SHARE`). Blind cursor kept only as `ALIEXPRESS_TARGETING=blind`. Matching rules unchanged. **Defect found by the tests and fixed:** the citation token regex allowed spaces, so "OIL FILTER 26300-…" was swallowed as one junk token and the real OE number was never isolated (0 candidates persisted); spaces removed.

**Controlled discovery-seeded sample (real API/DB/production sync function, limit 30, 25 discovery queries):** 15 discovery queries (stopped early: enough pending) added 147 catalog candidates; 30 targets checked -> **12 valid offers written (40%)**, 17 rejected by the guard (no valid listing), 1 supplier-SKU conflict (skipped, correct). **Unpriced 11/15 (73%), priced 1/15 (7%).** AliExpress API calls: 58 total (**4.8 calls per valid offer**, discovery included). 117 candidates remain queued (no further discovery spend needed for the next ~100 targets). All 12 offers class `aftermarket`; 0 `oe_equivalent` occurred, so that case is verified by tests only.

**Base-price root cause and fix:** both writers that derive `base_price` from supplier offers (`db_cleanup_agent.task_normalize_base_price_batched`, `db_update_agent.fix_base_prices`) were classification-blind — an aftermarket AliExpress offer became the base price of an OEM part. Both now use the same class rule as offer selection (`offer_classification.class_rank_sql = 0`: same class, or unclassified inheriting the part's class). Policy unchanged (cost x 1.45). Tests A-E x both writers + policy/importer-cost + no-raise-to-aftermarket: 12. **Data:** 8 previously polluted parts restored; 2 of them (Kia 2441037100, Mazda L3K910450) were re-polluted 17:10Z by the OLD healer before the fixed code was restarted -> restored again 17:24Z.

**Classification exposed to customers (smallest change, no redesign):** offer `part_type` label on search, compare, cart (`offerPartType`), wishlist, chat (`pricing.part_type` + "סוג המוצר" context line + prompt rule), WhatsApp checkout (Stripe description), partner API (`part_type`, documented in PUBLIC_API.md); frontend badge (`utils/offerClass.js`) in both offer rows and the cart; the frontend now sends the real `supplier_part_id` so an explicit offer choice reaches the cart. Cart/checkout/chat/NOA default to the class-compatible offer; no ranking change otherwise.

**NEW leak found by live verification and fixed:** search AND compare returned `price_usd` = the RAW supplier cost in USD (AliExpress 7.33 = ₪22.21/3.03) beside the policy price. Nothing consumed it; nulled at all 3 sites in `routes/parts.py` (+ static regression test). Live after restart: search/compare `price_usd` all null.

**INFRA incident found (not caused by this work):** Meilisearch was rejecting EVERY write (`no_space_left_on_device` = task-database limit; 88,032 accumulated task records; host disk fine, 38 GB free) — so per-part index updates and the 2h meili_sync had been silently failing (`index_failures: 11` in the sample). Deleted finished task history (88,032 -> 1; documents untouched), re-indexed the 23 AliExpress parts (partial PUT merge), started an incremental meili_sync. **Follow-up:** Meili needs scheduled task pruning (or a larger `--max-task-db-size`), else this recurs.

**Live verification (public domain, real endpoints):** search/cart 12/12 (default cart line = cheapest OEM-class offer even though the aftermarket one is cheaper; explicit choice -> aftermarket at policy price 32.20 != raw 22.21; both coexist as separate lines; unpriced part -> aftermarket at policy price; other suppliers' offers byte-unchanged — 0 other-supplier rows modified; no duplicate AliExpress offers; no supplier host/CDN in any payload); detail + compare labeled, no leak; chat search function returns class-labelled pricing (LLM reply itself not obtainable: Cerebras/Gemini/Groq all 429 during the test). AliExpress cheaper than existing supplier: 4/4 priced parts (e.g. 22.21 vs 142.37). **AliExpress MORE expensive than existing supplier: not observed in any real sample — tests only.**

**Regression:** offer_classification 36, customer_price_boundary 24, matching 52, OAuth 23, price-sync 11, base_price_classification 12, discovery 7, classification_surfaces 3 (+ frontend vitest 3) — see final run below.

**2000-RUN STATUS: HELD (`ALIEXPRESS_PRICE_SYNC_ENABLED=0`, `ALIEXPRESS_PRICE_SYNC_LIMIT=2000` unchanged; scheduler skips AliExpress).** Expected yield if enabled at 2000: measured 40% blended (73% unpriced / 7% priced) => ~800 offers, but ~4.8 calls/offer => ~9,600 calls, and the candidate pool (117 + discovery) must keep pace — unmeasured beyond this 30-target sample; recommend a bounded 200-target step first. Requires owner decision.


## 2026-09-21 — AliExpress bounded 200-target production verification (owner-authorized) — 2000-run NOT enabled

**Authorization:** owner approved a bounded 200-target verification; 2000 NOT authorized; `ALIEXPRESS_PRICE_SYNC_LIMIT` stays 2000 and `ALIEXPRESS_PRICE_SYNC_ENABLED` stays 0. **Method:** the sync function was invoked directly (`sync_aliexpress_prices(limit_per_run=50)` x4 chunks, same production code path as the scheduler) with an integrity gate between chunks; the kill switch was therefore never flipped and the scheduler could not pick the run up. Per-chunk discovery cap `ALIEXPRESS_DISCOVERY_MAX_QUERIES=40` (process-local, not persisted). Preconditions checked: OAuth loaded from encrypted DB and token valid, supplier active, classification + pricing boundary tests green, 0 polluted OEM base prices, Meili healthy (73 tasks).

**Exact counts (T0 17:46:15Z):** targets checked **185** (not 200: chunk 3 selected 35 because the UNPRICED candidate pool ran dry) = 85 unpriced + 100 priced. **Valid offers written 101: unpriced 51/85 (60%), priced 50/100 (50%), overall 54.6%.** Rejected 84 (unpriced 34, priced 50) of which 8 supplier-SKU conflicts; per-reason split of the other 76 (guard vs no listing) is NOT recorded by the code (measurement gap). Discovery queries 103 -> 498 new candidates. **API calls 397 (discovery 103 + matching/detail 294) = 3.9 calls per valid offer.** Errors: 0 HTTP, 0 auth, 0 write failures, 0 duplicates; price_history rows 101 (= 1 per new offer). Candidates before -> after: cited 117->430, matched 12->113, no_match 18->102; eligible pending unpriced 1->9, priced 78->247. Compared to the 30-sample: unpriced 73%->60%, priced 7%->50% — the 30-sample did NOT scale linearly (priced yield was 7x higher at scale, unpriced lower).

**Integrity (all 4 gates OK + final):** only AliExpress rows created; 0 other-supplier rows modified on the 101 parts; 0 priced-part catalog rows modified; classification 99 `aftermarket` + 2 `oe_equivalent` (none lost/oem); warranty 12 months `platform_default` on all 101; shipping NULL on all 101 (same as the pre-existing AliExpress rows); all 101 available/priced/URL-bearing. Priced parts: AliExpress cheaper on 47/50, equal-or-higher on 3; 27 had >=2 other suppliers; 43 OEM-class parts + 7 aftermarket-class parts (first real aftermarket-class coexistence cases); 2 real OE-equivalent offers. Unpriced parts: base_price stays NULL on all 51.

**THREE ROOT-CAUSE DEFECTS FOUND AND FIXED (found by this verification, not by the run):**
1. **Raw supplier cost leaked in SEARCH `price_ils` on every offer of every supplier** (e.g. OEM offer `price_ils 208.8` beside `customer_price_ils 302.76`; AliExpress `7.64` beside `11.08`). Pre-existing: the 2026-07-05 fix cleaned only the compare endpoint, and my earlier "no raw cost" live checks compared `customer_price_ils` only, so this was MISSED until now. Fix: `_offer_price_fields` (routes/parts.py) makes `price_ils` = customer price at both search emit sites (same as compare). Live re-check: 16/16 sampled parts clean (no price field equals raw cost, `price_usd` null, no supplier host/CDN). Test added.
2. **Third base_price writer was still classification-blind:** `db_update_agent.normalize_base_price` cases 1-3 (importer/online/`max_price_ils`); `refresh_min_max_prices` writes `max_price_ils` over ALL offers, so an aftermarket-only offer re-priced two OEM parts (Kia 2441037100, Mazda L3K910450) at 17:44:37Z — proven by arithmetic (base 131.97 = max 107.40/1.18x1.45). Fix: skip a part whose only priced offers are of an incompatible class (catalog-only importer data and compatible-offer parts unaffected). Tests F-I added (base-price suite 16). Both parts restored; live `normalize_base_price` no longer re-prices them; OEM-base-from-ali-only = 0 across all 124 AliExpress parts.
3. Harness note: search returns the full offer list only for the top-ranked part and a single best offer for lower-ranked parts sharing an OEM number (existing design) — the compare endpoint lists all offers; 88/88 live compare checks pass (AliExpress offer at policy price, labeled, raw cost not exposed, no URL/CDN, existing offers still listed).

**Meilisearch (separate from DB/supplier success):** 51 catalog docs indexed, 0 index failures; task total 73->197, 0 failed tasks; nothing deleted, no config change. The task-DB follow-up (scheduled pruning / larger `--max-task-db-size`) remains OPEN and separate.

**Latent risk (not changed):** `docker-compose.yml` defaults `ALIEXPRESS_PRICE_SYNC_ENABLED` to `:-1`; the sync is off only because `.env` sets 0.

**2000-RUN RECOMMENDATION: NOT ENABLED — owner decision.** Measured: 54.6% yield, 3.9 calls/offer, but the candidate pool is the constraint, not the yield: only ~21% of discovered candidates are unpriced parts (unpriced pool = 9 pending) while 247 priced candidates wait; a 2000 run at the default 50/50 split would check only ~1,000-1,100 targets (the code does not back-fill the short population), i.e. ~1,750 calls -> ~500-600 offers (ASSUMED: extrapolated from 103 discovery queries). Steady state after that is discovery-limited: ~150 queries/day -> ~700 candidates -> ~350 offers/day. No AliExpress quota is documented in the repo (UNVERIFIED). Options: keep ALIEXPRESS_PRICED_SHARE=0.5 (unpriced starve), raise the priced share, or accept a lower daily limit.


## 2026-09-22 — AliExpress target-allocation root fix (dynamic pool-aware split) — no production sync run

**Root cause:** `_select_targets` (services/aliexpress_price_sync.py) computed `n_unpriced`/`n_priced` as a fixed `round(n*ALIEXPRESS_PRICED_SHARE)` split of the request budget and handed each number straight to `_select_candidate_targets`, which simply returns however many rows its own population's SELECT finds — **any shortfall against the requested number was silently discarded, never handed to the other population.** Live evidence from the 2026-09-21 bounded run: real eligible pool `{unpriced: 9, priced: 247}` (96.4% of the candidate pool is priced) against a fixed 50/50 request produced 185/200 selected, not 200 — the unfulfilled unpriced request (191 - 9 = ~40 slots across 4 chunks) was lost instead of going to the deep priced pool. **The population split is naturally this skewed because discovery seeds from common repair-part search terms across major brands — most of those catalog parts already carry SOME price from the existing IL-importer/oem-parts-online/eBay pipelines, so only a small fraction of discoverable candidates are genuinely unpriced.** This is a real, structural property of the catalog, not a bug in discovery.

**Was fixed 50/50 still appropriate?** Yes, as a *ceiling/ambition* — it is an existing business rule (owner-directed dual-population eligibility: serve BOTH unpriced and priced-additional populations, not "whichever yields more"). It was wrong only as a *hard, non-reallocatable request*. No new ratio was invented — the fix uses the REAL measured pending pool as the constraint and keeps `ALIEXPRESS_PRICED_SHARE` (default 0.5) as the target when both pools can meet it.

**Fix — `_allocate_candidate_targets(pending, n, priced_share)`** (new, `services/aliexpress_price_sync.py`): given real eligible pending counts (from the already-existing `_pending_candidates`) and a request budget, clamps each population's ideal share to what its pool actually holds, then reallocates any leftover budget to whichever population still has spare eligible candidates (proportional to each side's spare when both have some). If both pools together fit inside the budget, takes everything — never over-requests. `_select_targets` now calls `_pending_candidates` once per run and feeds the result into the allocator instead of computing a blind split; `_select_candidate_targets` itself is unchanged (single point of fix, per the root-cause-only instruction). `discover_candidates`'s `want` early-stop thresholds were reviewed and left as-is — they only decide when to stop harvesting new candidates early (more discovery is never wasted; it feeds every future run), so they are not a resource-allocation bug and changing them was out of the minimal fix.

**Verified all three base_price writers share one classification policy:** `db_cleanup_agent.task_normalize_base_price_batched`, `db_update_agent.fix_base_prices`, and `db_update_agent.normalize_base_price` all call the SAME `offer_classification.class_rank_sql` (grep-verified, single shared function, no duplicated guard logic). **Residual (found, out of scope, NOT fixed):** `db_cleanup_agent.task_recover_priced_inactive` and `task_recover_motorstore_prices` also write `base_price` from a supplier offer with no classification guard at all — but both are gated to `parts_catalog.is_active = FALSE`, and every AliExpress selection predicate (`_UNPRICED`, `_PRICED_VISIBLE`, and the blind-cursor query) requires `pc.is_active = TRUE`, so an AliExpress offer can never reach an inactive part today. Flagged for awareness; not touched (no live interaction, and fixing it is unrelated to the AliExpress allocation task).

**Search pricing boundary re-verified (no new leak found):** `_offer_price_fields` (fixed 2026-09-21) still routes `price_ils` through the customer price at both search emit sites; `grep` confirms zero remaining `round(price_ils, 2) if price_ils else None` / `round(b_price_ils, ...)` raw-cost assignments in `routes/parts.py`. `price_usd` stays hardcoded `None` at all 3 sites. Regression test `test_search_offers_never_carry_the_raw_supplier_cost_as_price_ils` still passes.

**AliExpress API quota/rate limit: UNKNOWN — not fabricated.** Searched the official AliExpress Open Platform docs (`openservice.aliexpress.com/doc/doc.htm` — JS-rendered SPA, returns no static content to fetch) and web search; found no endpoint-specific, account-tier-specific, or app-specific published quota for `aliexpress.ds.text.search`/`aliexpress.ds.product.get`. One unverified third-party blog mentioned a generic "5 QPS" figure for an unspecified AliExpress search API, not corroborated by an official source and not confirmed to apply to our app's DS scope or tier — **explicitly NOT used as a safety basis.** The integration's own code has no documented-quota-derived throttle: only a flat `asyncio.sleep(0.3)` between discovery search calls and `0.5` between per-part matches (self-imposed pacing, not quota-derived). **This is a real gap: the 2000-run decision cannot cite a verified API ceiling.** Recommend the owner check the app's actual console at open.aliexpress.com (account-specific quota is typically shown there, behind login) before authorizing any run near or above the historical 397-call/185-target rate.

**Candidate-pool analysis (live, read-only, no writes this task):** current real pending = `{unpriced: 9, priced: 247}` (candidates table: 430 cited, 113 matched, 102 no_match — grew from discovery in the 2026-09-21 runs). Applying the FIXED allocator: a 200-target request now yields `{unpriced: 9, priced: 191}` = 200 (no shortfall); a 2000-target request yields only `{unpriced: 9, priced: 247}` = **256**, because the candidate pool itself does not currently hold 2000 eligible rows — a 2000-run would need substantial fresh discovery within the same run (bounded by `ALIEXPRESS_DISCOVERY_MAX_QUERIES`, default 150) to grow the pool before selection could approach 2000; at today's pool size the allocator correctly refuses to invent targets that don't exist rather than wasting a partial-fill the way the old code did.

## 2026-09-22 — AliExpress API Call Limit — AUTHENTICATED CONSOLE evidence obtained (supersedes prior "UNKNOWN" quota entries)

**Scope:** read-only investigation via the authenticated AliExpress Open Platform Console (owner's Chrome session, already logged in as `autosparefinder2024@gmail.com` — the correct business account). Zero production API calls made (no `text_search`/`search_by_oem`/`get_part_details`/OAuth refresh); zero app configuration changes; zero App Review/traffic-rule submissions. Confirmed unchanged throughout: `ALIEXPRESS_PRICE_SYNC_ENABLED=0`, `ALIEXPRESS_PRICE_SYNC_LIMIT=2000`.

**Console path used:** `App Console → auto spare finder (AppKey 546482, category "Drop Shipping", status Online) → Manage → App Overview → Advanced Information → "API Call Limit"` and `→ Data Dashboard → App Statistics → Error Code Statistics`.

**Finding 1 — the "API Call Limit" field in App Overview is BLANK, and this is confirmed at the DATA level, not a UI-hiding artifact.** Fetched the exact XHR that populates that page directly (`GET /handler/share/app/getAppBasic.json?appkey=546482`, authenticated session, read-only): the response's `appRuleVO.trafficRules` array is `[]` (empty) and `appRuleVO.nextTrafficRule` is `null`; `appRuleVO.canApplyTraffic` is `false`. **This proves, from the platform's own backend JSON, that no explicit numeric QPS/daily-quota rule is currently assigned to this app, and self-service application for a custom traffic rule is not currently available to us.** `existPermissionRules` shows 2 approved permission groups (`System Tool`, `AliExpress-dropship`/Drop Shipping, both `status: 4` = active) — these govern which API METHODS we may call, not call frequency.

**Finding 2 — a REAL, authoritative flow-control error occurred during today's own bounded run.** `Data Dashboard → App Statistics → Error Code Statistics` (date 2026-09-22, all APIs) shows: `aliexpress.ds.product.get` — Code **`ApiCallLimit`**, Message **"Api access frequency exceeds the limit. this ban will last 1 seconds"**, Error Total Calls: **1**, Date 2026-09-22. Total calls that day: 1330 (1309 success / 21 fail; the other 20 fails are `ITEM_ID_NOT_FOUND` on `aliexpress.ds.product.wholesale.get`, an unrelated business "not found" response, not a limit). **This is official, direct evidence that a real flow-control policy IS enforced by AliExpress's gateway for this app/API, even though no number is published in the console** — the error only manifests when the ceiling is actually crossed.

**Finding 3 — 2026-09-21 (the OTHER bounded run, 397 calls) shows NO `ApiCallLimit` at all.** That day's 60 fails were `IllegalRefreshToken` (37, an invalid/expired refresh token from unrelated/older activity), `InvalidCode` (11, an OAuth authorization-code reuse, also unrelated), `ITEM_ID_NOT_FOUND` (11, wholesale.get), and one `IllegalAccessToken` on `aliexpress.ds.text.search` — **none of these are quota errors**, confirming the 2026-09-21 run did not trip any flow control; only today's did, exactly once.

**Finding 4 — the API's own official documentation for `aliexpress.ds.product.get` (`openservice.aliexpress.com/doc/api.htm`, AE-Dropshipper category) lists only 3 documented business error codes** ("Item is not allowed to this country", `ITEM_ID_NOT_FOUND`, "System Error") **and publishes no QPS/rate-limit number anywhere on the page.** `ApiCallLimit` is not a documented business error of this API — it is a platform/gateway-level control that sits in front of all APIs and is undocumented per-method.

**Root cause of the single trip (found, NOT fixed — code change stopped before implementation per instruction):** `sync_aliexpress_prices` (`services/aliexpress_price_sync.py:613`) calls `aliexpress.get_part_details()` (→ `aliexpress.ds.product.get`) immediately after `search_by_oem()` (→ `aliexpress.ds.text.search`) succeeds for the SAME part, with no delay between these two calls — the existing `sleep(0.5)` throttle applies only BETWEEN parts in the outer loop, not between a part's own search-then-detail pair. That back-to-back pair is the likely trigger for the one-time trip on `ds.product.get` specifically (never observed on `ds.text.search`). **Proposed fix (not implemented, stopping here as instructed): add a small delay (or reuse the outer-loop sleep) between the search and detail calls within one part's processing.** This is a candidate for a future task, not this one.

**Scope determined:** UNKNOWN whether the enforcement is per-app, per-API, per-account, or global — no console page states this, and the `ApiCallLimit` error message itself carries no scope information. Given `trafficRules: []` (no app-level rule configured) yet a real per-API error fired, the most consistent interpretation is a PLATFORM-WIDE DEFAULT ceiling applied per (app, API-method) pair when no explicit custom rule exists — but this is inference from the evidence, not a proven statement from an official source, and is reported as such.

**Whether failed/rejected requests count against quota:** UNVERIFIED — no evidence either way in the console or docs.

**Burst/concurrency limits:** UNVERIFIED as a number; the only burst-relevant fact is the ban duration itself ("1 seconds"), which suggests a very short recovery window (consistent with a per-second token-bucket style control), not a long-lockout daily cap.

**"Online" status effect on quota policy:** NOT TESTED (would require changing app status, explicitly prohibited by this task). `appRuleVO.status` field for existing permission rules already shows `4` (active/approved) while the app itself is "Online" — no evidence found that changing Online/Offline status alters `trafficRules` (which are empty regardless).

**Official Support/Work Order path identified (not used, per instructions — investigation only):** `Contact us → Ticket System → My → My Ticket` (`openservice.aliexpress.com/support/index.htm#/myTicket`) — an "AI Assistant"-driven ticket creation flow exists (no ticket has been submitted). **Recommended content for a support ticket, if the owner chooses to submit one:** ask AliExpress Open Platform support for the exact QPS/daily-call quota and flow-control policy assigned to App 546482 ("auto spare finder", Drop Shipping / AliExpress-dropship permission group) for `aliexpress.ds.text.search` and `aliexpress.ds.product.get` specifically, and whether the `ApiCallLimit` policy is per-app, per-API, or per-account.

**Revision to prior entries:** the 2026-09-21 and 2026-09-22 (earlier) FIXES_TRACKER entries stated quota as "UNKNOWN — not investigated beyond public docs/search." This entry supersedes those with real authenticated-console evidence: quota remains **numerically unproven**, but its EXISTENCE and approximate behavior (short, self-clearing per-call-pair trip, not a long lockout) is now proven from the platform's own error log, not guessed.

**No code, config, or production state changed in this investigation.**


## 2026-09-23 — AliExpress token persistence — RE-VERIFIED fresh (already fixed in an earlier turn; no new code change)

**Premise check (per CLAUDE.md "never assume, verify fresh"):** the task's stated facts ("no real authorization has been performed yet", "remaining blocker is token persistence") are STALE. Live inspection this turn found: (1) `suppliers.credentials -> 'aliexpress_oauth'` on the real `AliExpress` row already holds a genuine Fernet-encrypted blob (`enc` prefix `gAAAAAB...`, the real Fernet token signature) with `expire_time`/`obtained_at` timestamps consistent with a real prior authorization + at least one real refresh cycle; (2) the in-memory access_token the running container loads via `_load_persisted()` **differs from** the `.env` bootstrap `ALIEXPRESS_ACCESS_TOKEN` value — proof the app is actively preferring the DB-persisted (refreshed) copy over the static env fallback, live, right now; (3) `services/suppliers/aliexpress_supplier.py` and `routes/suppliers.py`/`routes/auth.py` already implement everything this task asks for, with a code comment stating "The former legacy handler (raw tokens in the response, no persistence) was removed." **Conclusion: this exact remediation was already completed in an earlier turn of this session. This turn re-verified it fresh, end-to-end, rather than trusting that record — no code was changed.**

**Existing secret/config mechanism identified (confirmed, not new):** `suppliers.credentials` JSONB column (catalog DB) — the SAME table/column the project already uses for every other supplier's credentials — holding a Fernet-encrypted blob under key `aliexpress_oauth`, keyed by the already-provisioned `ENCRYPTION_KEY` env var (confirmed present and 65 chars in the live container). No new secret-storage system was introduced or considered.

**Why `/app/.env` doesn't exist (confirmed):** `docker-compose.yml` reads `.env` from the HOST at `docker compose up` time and substitutes `${VAR}` references into each service's `environment:` block — the `.env` file itself is never copied or bind-mounted into the container filesystem, only the resolved values are injected as process environment variables. Confirmed live: `docker exec autospare_backend test -f /app/.env` → missing (expected). This is why `services/suppliers/aliexpress_supplier.py`'s own comment states "env vars are only the bootstrap fallback; the DB copy... wins when present" — env vars are immutable for the container's lifetime (no in-container file to rewrite), so anything that must survive a token refresh has to live in a mechanism the app can WRITE to at runtime, which is the DB.

**Root cause (as originally fixed, re-confirmed today):** the ORIGINAL failure mode (per the code's own history comments) was a legacy callback handler that returned raw tokens in the HTTP response and never persisted anything — every browser-driven authorization was lost the moment the page closed, and `_auto_refresh_token` had nothing durable to read after a restart (env vars can't be rewritten in-container). The fix already in place: `persist_tokens()`/`_load_persisted()` read/write the encrypted DB blob; the callback (`routes/suppliers.py::aliexpress_callback`) calls `persist_tokens` on every successful exchange and returns only a masked HTML page; `_auto_refresh_token` reads `self._refresh_token` (populated by `_load_persisted`, called at the top of `_ensure_token`) and re-persists the refreshed pair.

**Verification performed this turn (all read-only or disposable-row; zero real AliExpress network calls, zero changes to the real `AliExpress` supplier row):**
1. **Live read-back against the REAL persisted row** (masked): `AliExpressSupplier()._load_persisted()` → `True`; loaded access/refresh tokens both 68 chars, `token_expire` a real future timestamp; loaded access_token **differs** from the `.env` bootstrap value (proves the DB copy is what's actually live, not just theoretically readable).
2. **Full existing regression suite** `tests/test_aliexpress_iop_oauth.py`: **23/23 passed** — authorize-URL IOP-only, callback GET/POST/JSON/form/query all masked, error-param HTML-escaped, exchange failures sanitized, persistence-failure path returns 500 without leaking tokens, single callback implementation (no duplicate legacy handler), Fernet ciphertext hides tokens and requires the key, refresh re-persists, refresh-failure cooldown + retry-on-new-token, no-refresh-token path, no legacy oauth.aliexpress.com path reachable, signing unchanged, **no secrets in logs or responses**, uvicorn access-log redaction of the OAuth query string.
3. **Full existing synthetic DB-round-trip devtest** `devtests/aliexpress_token_persistence_test.py` (disposable `ZZ_ALI_TOKEN_TEST` supplier row, deleted in `finally`; mocked AliExpress HTTP, NO real network call): **21/21 passed** — all 6 callback route variants return masked HTML with no secret substrings; persisted JSONB has `enc`+`expire_time`, no plaintext tokens; a FRESH `AliExpressSupplier()` instance (simulating a restart — no shared in-memory state) loads the exact persisted access/refresh/expiry; the refresh path's outgoing request body used the **persisted** refresh token (not the env one); refreshed tokens are re-persisted and visible to yet another fresh instance; **a separate OS subprocess** (`python3 -c ...`, no shared Python process state at all — stronger than a container restart) loads the tokens from the DB successfully; `/app/.env` confirmed absent; **zero token values appeared in the captured log buffer**. Cleanup verified: 0 leftover test rows.
4. **One pre-existing, unrelated, cosmetic log line** surfaced during the devtest run: `services/supplier_aggregator.py:66` logs `"[suppliers] LocalDB failed: ... circular import ..."` — triggered by this test script's own module-import order (`import routes.suppliers as rs` at script scope), not a production code path; did not affect any of the 21 checks. Not fixed (unrelated to this task's scope, no root cause to chase here).
5. **State confirmed unchanged:** `git rev-parse HEAD` unchanged from session start; no new commits; `suppliers.is_active` for AliExpress unchanged (`True`, set in an earlier session); 0 residual test rows.

**No code changes were made or needed in this turn** — the persistence mechanism, masked success page, credential-logging hygiene, and refresh-path correctness were already implemented and are now freshly re-verified rather than assumed from a prior record.

**Remaining requirement:** none for persistence — it is done and verified. The task's implied next step ("real authorization") is **already moot**: a real authorization already occurred in an earlier turn (evidenced by the real encrypted DB blob and the DB-vs-env token divergence above), and the supplier has been actively making real API calls since (see the 2026-09-21/22 AliExpress price-sync entries in this file). No further authorization action is needed or was performed this turn.
